# Open Questions & Decisions

## Decisions made

*The log is append-only: an entry's text, once written, changes only by
appending an **Update** or **Correction** paragraph after the entry's last
line, never by editing in place — the rule is CI-enforced by
[`tests/test_decision_updates_are_appended.py`](../tests/test_decision_updates_are_appended.py)
against the base branch. (D25, D26 and D33 were edited in place before the rule
existed; their history predates it.)*

- **D1 — Project framing.** Build a *standalone, first-principles* skill suite for code review & maintenance. Existing skills/tools are prior art, not constraints. *(2026-06-08)*
- **D2 — Scope.** Maximal: intrinsic code properties **plus** all cross-cutting concerns. *(2026-06-08)*
- **D3 — Sequence.** Map first, then design the skill suite. Research/reference-gathering happens against the map before any skill design. *(2026-06-08)*
- **D4 — Repo.** `code-quality-atlas`, under `~/code/`. **Update (2026-06-11, D11):** flipped public and the name finalized — `brandondees/code-quality-atlas` is the live GitHub repo, plugin-marketplace name, and install path (README, `plugin.json`); no longer private, no longer provisional. *(2026-06-08)*
- **D5 — Map pressure-test → taxonomy v0.2.** Resolved the candidate additions: **promoted** AI/LLM-integration (#25), Configuration & environment (#26), Compliance/licensing/provenance (#27); **broadened** #3 (distributed correctness) and #9 (caller ergonomics / internal-API DX); **cross-linked** money/units #4 ↔ #23; **kept folded** logging in #16. Now 27 categories. Reversible. *(2026-06-08)*
- **D6 — Docs are the source of truth; skills are derived, traceable & regenerable.** *(user, 2026-06-09)* The taxonomy + per-cluster research are canonical. Every skill must trace back to the categories/research sections it's built from, and be **rebuildable/refinable** as those docs improve. Research critique/refinement runs **async and in parallel** with skill-building — the architecture must let improving docs flow into improving skills over time (a compounding loop), never a one-shot generation that then drifts. This makes phase-2 partly a *pipeline* design, not just a set of skills.
- **D7 — Skills follow Anthropic's Agent Skills best practices; optimize for progressive disclosure, auto-trigger descriptions, and model portability.** *(user, 2026-06-09; ref https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/agent-skills/best-practices)* `SKILL.md` is a lean entry point (**<500 lines**, aim ≪) with frontmatter (gerund `name`; specific third-person `description` ≤1024 chars carrying explicit trigger keywords; provenance) + when-to-use + a lean checklist; detailed heuristics / tool-rules / references / examples live in **one-level-deep bundled files** loaded on demand (no context cost until read; >100-line files get a ToC). **Do not assume the model is Claude** — target portability down to ~8B local models: bundled files are explicit, concrete, checklist-style (low ambiguity), with concrete good/bad examples and a single default approach (no option-menus). Plain markdown+files = harness/model-agnostic. Forward-slash paths; no time-sensitive text; consistent terminology. **Addendum (2026-09-05) — deliberate exception for the collapsed entry points:** `collapsed/skills/reviewing-a-change/SKILL.md` (and its sibling collapsed entry points) directs `reference/lenses/<lens>/body.md`, with `tool-rules.md`/`sources.md` alongside — a `reference/lenses/<lens>/` nesting one hop deeper than the one-level-deep rule this same decision states, and than the artifact rubric (`reviewing-artifact-conventions/reference/skill-md.md`) checks for in others' skills. This is reviewed and intentional, not drift: a collapsed entry point bundles ~28 lenses' worth of per-lens body/tool-rules/sources under one shared `SKILL.md` (see [`docs/distribution.md`](distribution.md) Channel B), so each lens needs its own subdirectory to stay addressable — flattening to true one-level (`reference/<lens>-body.md`, `reference/<lens>-tool-rules.md`, …) would just rename the same hop into the filename instead of removing it, at a readability cost. A third-party single-purpose skill has no such multi-lens bundling problem, so the rubric's one-level-deep check keeps applying to it unchanged; only the collapsed entry points carry this named carve-out.
- **D8 — Eval-first.** Each skill ships **≥3 evaluation scenarios** (query + expected_behavior, with a no-skill baseline). Evals are the **regression net for regeneration**: docs change → drift-check flags affected skills → regenerate/refine → re-run evals to confirm no behavioral regression. Write evals before skill prose.
- **D10 — First-dogfood packaging fixes (Q7 partially resolved): self-sufficient SKILL.md + a router skill.** *(from in-session user feedback, 2026-06-11)* Five changes, all driven through the manifest/generator so regeneration stays clean: **(1)** every `SKILL.md` inlines its **top ~8 checks** (head of the source heuristics; cross_ref categories capped at 2) so the first disclosure level is reviewable without a second fetch; **(2)** a manifest `router:` section generates `choosing-review-lenses` — the composition layer mapping "what am I reviewing" → 2-4 lenses, with a one-line `picker` differentiator per lens (selection sharpness without touching the eval-tuned trigger `description`s); **(3)** a `design: true` flag marks which diff lenses also work on design docs/plans (◆ in the router catalog), and every SKILL.md states its shape explicitly; **(4)** skills with `cross_ref` categories carry a **dedupe note** naming the category's primary owner (G1, surfaced at review time, not just validation time); **(5)** the reference-file links say when each is actually needed (tool-rules/sources are not part of the judgment review). The router has `built_from: []` — it derives from the manifest, so docs drift never flags it; manifest edits regenerate it.
- **D12 — Composition back half (Q7 resolved): a synthesizer skill + advisory-by-default fan-out.** *(2026-06-12)* The router (D10) picks the lenses; a 24th skill, `synthesizing-review-findings`, merges their output into one report — **collect → dedupe → reconcile → rank → verdict**. Dedup reuses the existing G1 primary-owner attribution (shared findings reported once, under the owner); reconciliation uses a manifest `synthesizer.tensions:` table of known opposing lens pairs (restraint ↔ module-design / performance / test-quality / api-contract; performance ↔ readability) each with a default resolution; ranking uses a `severity_order` scale (Blocker > Major > Minor > Nit) with correctness/security/data-loss floated to the top; the verdict is one of block / approve-with-changes / approve. **Fan-out is advisory by default** (the agent runs the router's lenses, then applies the merge) — chosen over automated orchestration to honor D7 portability (plain markdown, no Claude/harness assumption) — but the skill ships a fixed **finding contract** (location/severity/lens/finding/fix) so a capable harness can *mechanize* the same deterministic merge. Generated from the manifest like the router (`built_from: []`, no docs drift); validator checks tensions name two distinct known lenses and `severity_order` is non-trivial. A `reviewer discipline` guard forbids inflating the merged report beyond the union of real lens findings.
- **D11 — License for public release: dual MIT (code) + CC BY 4.0 (content).** *(user, 2026-06-11)* The research atlas and skills (`docs/`, `skills/`, README) carry CC BY 4.0 — prose is the project's main value and CC BY is built for it; the pipeline (`tooling/`, `tests/`, CI/config) carries MIT. Python sources get `SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT` headers (dogfooding #27's per-file-header check); the plugin manifest declares `MIT AND CC-BY-4.0`. Root `LICENSE` is the explainer, full texts in `LICENSE-MIT` / `LICENSE-CC-BY-4.0`. Chosen over single-MIT for content-license fit, over copyleft to keep plugin adoption unencumbered. Unblocks flipping the repo public.
- **D9 — Packaging (Q12 resolved): the repo is itself a Claude Code plugin + marketplace.** `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (both inside the repo-root `.claude-plugin/` directory); `skills/` is already the plugin-default skill layout, so no restructuring. Install: `/plugin marketplace add brandondees/code-quality-atlas` then `/plugin install code-quality-atlas@code-quality-atlas`. **Versioning: commit-SHA** (no `version` field) — every merged commit ships, matching the regeneration loop. *(The original revisit-trigger here, "switch to pinned semver if/when the suite stabilizes for external users," is superseded by D23's checkable triggers.)* Skill-level provenance still carries `taxonomy_version`. Validated with `claude plugin validate` and a local end-to-end install (22/22 skills discovered). *(2026-06-10)*

  **Addendum (2026-09-07) — a static `version` field was added to `plugin.json`, but the commit-SHA policy above is not actually reversed.** Issue #389 item 2 added `"version": "0.1.0"` to `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (PR merged as commit `1683e7a7`, 2026-09-05), stated there as "plugin version visibility" — a real, optional field per the plugin manifest schema, added so a marketplace listing has something to display. This was **not** a judgment that D9's own named trigger ("switch to pinned semver if/when the suite stabilizes for external users") had been met — no such judgment was recorded anywhere in that PR, this doc, or `docs/session-log.md`, and the field has stayed at the placeholder `0.1.0` unbumped ever since (confirmed against current `plugin.json` and its git history at authoring time). The commit-SHA policy is in fact still the operative one for anyone actually trying to tell *which build* they're running: the same PR added a `hooks/route.sh` build-identifier line that resolves `git rev-parse --short HEAD` against the plugin's own installed checkout, with an explicit comment that this was chosen over "a hand-maintained version string that can drift from it" — i.e. over trusting `plugin.json`'s `version` field for that purpose. Net effect: `plugin.json` now carries a cosmetic, static `version` for marketplace display; the suite's real versioning signal is unchanged and still commit-SHA-based. `docs/distribution.md` is not updated with this nuance as of this addendum — flagged here rather than left to be inferred from the code diff.
- **D15 — Artifact-scoped lenses get their own `shape: artifact`; G11 factor lands at #30 (Q18 resolved).** *(user, 2026-06-12)* Chose option (b): a fourth review shape **`artifact`** (sibling to diff / repo / decision), hosted as **one entry-point lens** that presence-detects an artifact and loads the matching rubric from a bundled file, driven by a manifest `artifacts:` table (artifact → detector glob → rubric source). Chosen over the minimal one-off lens (a) because it *generalizes* the whole §3 artifact-standard catalog at one always-on description's cost — the foundational pattern the owner asked to strengthen — and over retrieval-routed (c) because (c) breaks D7 plain-markdown portability (a longer-horizon harness bet, parked). The **G11 authoring-quality factor lands at #30** (meta-artifacts), keeping #32 cleanly about *runtime* agent safety; `SKILL.md`-authoring is the first instance (highest-confidence rubric — we already enforce it on ourselves via the generator/validator). Borrows presence-based activation from the linter world (MegaLinter activate-on-file, ESLint glob `overrides`, Spectral rulesets-by-type) and gives Q14 its cleanest relevance signal (file presence). Research: [`research/artifact-scoped-lenses.md`](research/artifact-scoped-lenses.md). **✅ Built 2026-06-24** — the `shape: artifact` capability landed in `manifest.py`/`generate_skill.py` (an `Artifact` row carries name/detect/slug/rubric; rubric sections are numbered ≥101 so they flow through the existing `built_from`/drift machinery without colliding with the 1–37 taxonomy in the G1 single-owner check); the entry-point lens `reviewing-artifact-conventions` presence-detects an artifact and loads its bundled `reference/<slug>.md` rubric; a manifest `artifacts:` table drives it; the first rubric — `SKILL.md` / agent-skill authoring (#101 in the new [`research/artifact-rubrics.md`](research/artifact-rubrics.md), mined from the Anthropic guide we already enforce on ourselves) — ships with 4 evals and a router route. Disposition of the two open build questions: it sits **alongside** the implicit artifact lenses (#20/#31/#19), and detector reliability stays file-presence prose for now (content-sniffing is a follow-up when a non-standard-path artifact is added). **Cross-model re-gate passed 2026-06-24** (qwen2.5:7b floor + llama3.1:8b cross-confirm; presence-activation correctly returns "No findings" on a no-artifact diff — see session-log). The next rubrics (Dockerfile, OpenAPI, Terraform, …) are additive — a research section + an `artifacts:` row each, no new always-on description.
- **D14 — Promote agentic/tool-use safety to its own category #32 (Q16 resolved).** *(user, 2026-06-12)* Agentic action-safety leaves #25 to become **#32 Agentic & tool-use safety**, scoped to the **action/tool surface** (tool least-privilege, approval gates & step budgets, tool-metadata-as-untrusted-input, confused-deputy/token-audience, inter-agent auth, sandboxed exec, memory hygiene, audit trail, excessive agency). Chosen over the cheaper "sharpen #25's trigger" middle path because the trigger gap is the decider — the highest-risk agent codebases (tool defs, MCP servers, autonomous loops) may not read as "LLM integration," so #25's trigger can miss them — plus G1 cross-cutting ownership (#13/#14/#24/#25) wants a single owner, and OWASP's *separate* Agentic Top 10 (ASI01–ASI10) is standards-grade external validation. **#25↔#32 boundary:** model-call concerns stay #25; action/tool concerns → #32; the **lethal-trifecta framing** stays in #25 but its exfil/action-leg mitigations are #32 (#25 references #32, no double-report). Build defaults (reversible): lens `reviewing-agentic-safety` (`shape: diff`, `built_from #32`, skip-clause "no tools/agents/MCP/autonomous loops"); a repo-shaped whole-agent-system audit arm is a noted follow-up. **`taxonomy.md` updated (#32 added, G2/Q16 marked resolved). ✅ Lens built 2026-06-24** — the agentic heuristics moved from cluster-4 #25 into a new #32 research section (8 ASI heuristics + the lethal-trifecta exfil/action-leg mitigation, 2 ★), the `reviewing-agentic-safety` lens (`shape: diff`, design-capable, `built_from #32`, skip-clause "no tools/agents/MCP/autonomous loops") generates with examples + 4 evals, a dedicated router route lands, and #25 keeps the model-call concerns with a boundary note (drift clean, 102 tests pass). No `taxonomy_version` bump — #32 already shipped in v0.7's taxonomy. **Cross-model re-gate passed 2026-06-24** (qwen2.5:7b floor + llama3.1:8b cross-confirm; ASI02/03, autonomy-bound, and token-passthrough all caught, clean scenario "No findings" — see session-log). 32 categories.
- **D13 — Taxonomy v0.3 (round-2 gap hunt): four promoted categories + a third review shape.** *(user, 2026-06-12; "full v0.3 draft")* Promoted **#28 Operational & resilience design**, **#29 Decision lifecycle**, **#30 Enforcement apparatus & meta-artifacts**, **#31 Infrastructure-as-code**; added factors to #16/#17/#19/#20/#22/#25/#27; named **decision-time** as a third review shape alongside diff and repo/cron ([`decision-time-review-shape.md`](decision-time-review-shape.md), Q15). Resolves the G9 #12 drops (→ #28) and the G10 framing gap (→ #30). Decision-time is modelled as a **mode orthogonal to topic** (formalizing the existing `design:` flag) plus a few decision-native lenses — **not** a 7th cluster (would re-create G1 double-booking). Governance slices (build-vs-buy TCO, deep fairness, DevEx-as-a-system) held **out-of-scope** via the G8 detect-and-escalate boundary. Disposition: [`taxonomy.md`](taxonomy.md#candidate-additions--resolved-v03). 31 categories (D14 later added #32 → 32). **v0.3 build complete (2026-06-12):** all four promoted categories now ship skills — `reviewing-decision-lifecycle` (#29), `auditing-enforcement-and-meta-artifacts` (#30), `reviewing-resilience-and-scalability` (#28), and `auditing-infrastructure-as-code` (#31) — each with research section, manifest entry, examples, evals, and a cross-model gate on the 7-8B tier (qwen2.5:7b + llama3.1:8b). The add-factor regenerations landed earlier (drift clean). 26 lens skills + router + synthesizer. **(#32 from D14 shipped 2026-06-24 as `reviewing-agentic-safety`; the `shape: artifact` lens from D15 shipped 2026-06-24 as `reviewing-artifact-conventions` — both built.)**
- **D16 — Review-depth modes (Q14 resolved): separate relevance from depth; three tiers; manifest-driven.** *(user, 2026-06-24)* The router (D10) conflated **relevance** (which lenses apply) with **depth** (how much to run) on one 2-4 list — the cause of G9's coverage suppression (soft lenses never fire, so #5 emits no findings). Resolution: relevance becomes a **ranked list**, depth decides how far down. **Three depth modes** — **triage** (critical tier only: correctness/security/data-safety/concurrency; floor raised to Major+; pre-merge gate), **review** *(default)* (relevance-ranked top-N — the **2-4 cap survives as the default depth, overridable**; today's round-based floor escalation; per-PR), **comprehensive** (every applicable lens, **uncapped**; floor **pinned at Nit** with no per-round escalation; on-demand/scheduled). The repo audits are the comprehensive **repo arm**; comprehensive adds the **diff arm**. **Addendum (2026-07-06):** the review-mode default breadth widened from **2-4 to 3-8**, still non-strict — see [`review-depth-modes.md`](review-depth-modes.md). **The per-mode severity floor — comprehensive pinned at Nit — is the actual G9 fix** (running more lenses alone wouldn't surface readability-class findings; the escalating floor would still trim them). Mode lives in a manifest **`modes:`** section (generated like the router/synthesizer, `built_from: []`, no drift; D7-portable — a mode is just *which subset the router emits* + *which floor policy the synthesizer applies*, advisory fan-out unchanged), surfaced via commands (`/atlas-triage`, `/atlas-review-pr` = review, `/atlas-audit-comprehensive`) and/or a `--depth` arg. **Q13 hook:** the team-preferences overlay sets the per-repo default mode + critical-tier definition when built. Refines D10 (router gains a depth axis + ranked relevance) and D12 (synthesizer gains a per-mode floor policy); resolves the router-under-selection half of G9 (the budget half — the `★` marker — already shipped). Design write-up + open implementation sub-questions: [`review-depth-modes.md`](review-depth-modes.md). **Build deferred** (manifest/generator/synthesizer pass). **Folded into Q20's design (2026-06-25):** the relevance/depth separation, three modes, and per-mode severity floors are now part of [`collapsed-entrypoints-and-depth-modes.md`](collapsed-entrypoints-and-depth-modes.md) and ship with that build — **✅ built 2026-06-26 (PR #80)**; D16 is resolved-by-Q20-build.
- **D17 — Self-improvement loop (Q17): stage 1 approved for build; stages 2-5 stay design-only pending stage-1 evidence.** *(user, 2026-07-06)* Reviewed [`self-improvement-loop.md`](self-improvement-loop.md) end to end. **Two calls made:** (a) the tier-1 local learnings log is **committed** to the consumer repo (not gitignored) — it becomes the team's own retro history and Q13-overlay evidence over time, and the design's "privacy boundary at record creation, not transmission" principle already makes small abstracted records safe to commit, resolving §8 sub-question 2; (b) **approval is scoped to stage 1 only** (§7: the manifest `feedback:` section generating a synthesizer "Process notes" appendix + a one-line lens footer, plus the `PostToolUse`/`SessionEnd` invocation-logger hooks, gated on opt-in tier ≥ `local`) — no network, no new infra, defaults off. Stages 2-5 (the `/atlas-retro` transcript digestion, the GitHub outcome-auditor, the intake routine, tier-3 auto-filing) remain **design-only**, to be re-reviewed and approved separately once stage 1 produces real usage evidence that the signal is worth collecting — mirrors how Q15 was handled (shape approved, then each lens reviewed/built individually) rather than approving transcript-injection and autonomous-filing risk surfaces (§6, §5's tier-3 threat model) before any evidence the loop pays for itself. §8's other sub-questions (dedup identity, issue-transport privacy at scale, model-tier shunting, the outcome-auditor-as-dashboard carrot) are implementation-time calls for their respective stages, not blockers on stage 1. **✅ Stage 1 built 2026-07-18:** no manifest schema change proved necessary — the Process-notes appendix and lens footer are generator-level prose (mirroring how the Q15 decision-record checklist shipped, `built_from: []`-equivalent, drift-clean) rather than a new `feedback:` manifest section; the two hooks (`hooks/log-skill-invocation.sh`, `hooks/queue-session-retro.sh`) and their shared tier resolver (`hooks/lib/feedback-tier.sh`) gate on a `feedback:` line in `.code-quality-atlas/preferences.md` (new §7 in the template) or an env-var override, defaulting to `off`, and degrade to a clean no-op on a missing `jq` or malformed hook input. The `Skill` tool's exact `tool_input` shape is undocumented as of this writing, so the logger avoided guessing a field name — but the shipped hook initially stored the payload verbatim instead, contradicting the design's "abstracted at creation" promise (self-improvement-loop.md §5, templates/preferences-template.md §7) and leaking absolute local transcript paths besides. **Fixed 2026-09-03 (#364):** `log-skill-invocation.sh` now records only `tool_input`'s byte length and SHA-256 digest, and `queue-session-retro.sh` now records only the transcript's basename — see the design doc's revisited assumption (a) and §5's "what abstracted means" note. **Note (2026-09-05) — why the stage-2-5 gate cannot open from this repo's own usage.** The stage-1 hooks gate on a `feedback:` tier read from a consumer's `.code-quality-atlas/preferences.md` (default `off`); this repo — the suite's own reference consumer — has no such file, so `feedback:` here defaults to `off` and the hooks are no-ops in this repo's current sessions (the shared resolver, `hooks/lib/feedback-tier.sh`, also honors a `CODE_QUALITY_ATLAS_FEEDBACK_TIER` env-var override, so this is a statement about the current default, not an unconditional "can never run here"). The gate condition as written ("once stage 1 produces real usage evidence") is correct for a consumer that *has* opted in — evidence can and does accumulate there — it just cannot be satisfied by this repo dogfooding itself, since nothing here currently opts into `feedback: local` or higher via either mechanism. Whether to enable `feedback: local` in this repo is a separate, standing operational choice left to the owner, not changed by this note; absent that (or evidence reported back from an external consumer), stages 2-5 stay exactly where D17 left them — reviewed design, not approved for build.
- **D18 — Findings-only; no quantitative per-dimension scores (Q4 resolved).** *(2026-07-18)* Skills emit **findings only** — located, actionable, severity-tagged — never a numeric score per dimension (à la `type-design-analyzer`'s per-type ratings, flagged as a design question in [`prior-art.md`](prior-art.md)). This wasn't a new call so much as recognizing what every other decision already committed to: **D12**'s synthesizer ranks by a *categorical* `severity_order` (Blocker > Major > Minor > Nit), never a scalar; **Q13**'s team-preferences overlay explicitly ruled out "no scoring or numeric 'preference compliance %'" *citing Q4's vanity-metric failure mode by name* (`team-preferences-overlay.md` §8); and no shipped lens's manifest schema has a score field — findings are the only output type that exists in the built suite. Making it official closes the doc/build gap rather than opening new design space. **Rationale, stated plainly:** a per-dimension score invites exactly the failure Q4 itself warned about — teams optimize the number instead of the underlying quality, a score can rise while real defects are reworded to survive detection, and a single scalar erases the *kind* of problem a finding names (a security Blocker and a naming Nit cannot be added together meaningfully). The suite already has richer, non-scalar differentiators that do real work without that risk: **severity** (categorical, D12), **tier** (floor/preference, Q13 Wave A), and **valence** (defect/improvement, G26) — three orthogonal axes instead of one lossy number. **Not in conflict:** `review-depth-modes.md`'s mention of eventual "signal-based scoring" for the *router's* lens-*selection* ranking (Q14) is an internal relevance signal deciding which lenses to run, never a quality score reported to the user — a different mechanism at a different layer, out of Q4's scope. Trend-tracking (the original motivation for scores) stays available another way: count findings by severity/category over time (a `git log`-shaped historical query), which needs no score to compute and doesn't reward gaming a single metric. No build follow-up — this is a decision, not a feature; the manifest/generator already conform.
- **D19 — No type checker on `tooling/` for now (#380).** *(2026-09-04)* `tooling/` stays untyped-checked: `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` (now both gated in CI) plus the 540+-test suite are the enforcement surface; no `mypy`/`pyright` run locally or in CI. Not a rejection on principle — the codebase already leans on dataclasses and type hints for readability — but adopting one is real, ongoing cost (a new dev dependency and CI step, an initial pass to clear whatever it flags across `tooling/`'s ~20 modules, and upkeep on every future change) that hasn't been weighed against what it would actually catch beyond what the test suite + `ValidationError`-raising runtime checks already do (`manifest.py`'s loader, in particular, treats a malformed manifest as a first-class error case, not an untyped-data footgun). Revisit if `tooling/` grows enough surface, or a bug a type checker would have caught actually ships — either resets this from "not yet" to a real proposal with a concrete trigger, rather than adding the dependency speculatively. Recorded here specifically so the question stops re-surfacing as an unresolved audit finding each sweep.

  **Superseded (2026-09-19).** PR #514 (`c966e29`, "feat: add a mypy gate for tooling/, and fix the 28 errors it surfaces", 2026-09-18) adopted `mypy` — a `[tool.mypy]` table in `pyproject.toml` scoped to `tooling/` (`python_version = "3.12"`), a `type-check (mypy)` step in `ci.yml`'s gate job, `mypy`/`types-PyYAML` added to `requirements.in`/`.txt`, and the 28 errors it surfaced fixed in the same PR. `CLAUDE.md`'s Development setup block already lists `mypy` as a standard command, consistent with the new gate. This is a real, deliberate adoption (not scope creep — the PR names the dogfooding gap directly: this suite's own review content tells *consumer* repos to run mypy/pyright, while nothing in this repo's own CI did) that simply never touched this decision record, leaving D19's "no `mypy`/`pyright` run locally or in CI" claim false as of `c966e29`. Flagged and fixed by a scheduled maintenance audit that found the gate in `ci.yml` and cross-checked it against this entry — the exact "decision record went stale the moment its own premise reversed" pattern D19's own closing sentence was written to prevent recurring. The stated revisit trigger ("`tooling/` grows enough surface, or a bug a type checker would have caught actually ships") was not the reasoning found in PR #514's commit message — record that gap honestly rather than retrofit a trigger that wasn't actually invoked. D19 stays here as the historical record of the original deferral; **the operative state as of 2026-09-19 is: mypy runs in CI, scoped to `tooling/`.**
- **D20 — No lens-rename alias; a stale `preferences.md` reference is surfaced, not migrated (#447).** *(user, 2026-09-06)* #447 asked three questions about a renamed/retired lens: how long an alias should stay resolvable, whether a stale `preferences.md` entry warns or silently no-ops, and whether `preferences.md` gets a real schema. Resolved all three: **no alias mechanism at all** — a rename carries no migration path; keeping old names resolvable (forever or for a stated sunset) has little value against the ongoing cost of tracking them. **A stale entry is surfaced loudly, never silently dropped or guessed at** — `choosing-review-lenses`'s "Load team preferences first" step now instructs the reviewer to report a `preferences.md` directive naming a lens that no longer matches any current manifest entry through the normal finding contract (`location: .code-quality-atlas/preferences.md`, `valence: defect`, `route: implementer`, attributed to `choosing-review-lenses`) rather than the synthesizer's capped Process notes, so the finding is ranked, deduplicated, and tracked round to round instead of competing for one of Process notes' 0-3 slots. **`preferences.md` stays hand-authored prose — no schema, no version field** — for as long as this awareness mechanism reliably steers the reviewing agent to flag a stale entry. If that proves unreliable in practice, the fallback is a mechanical schema layered alongside the existing prose instructions, not a wholesale replacement of it. `templates/preferences-template.md` states the no-alias rule up front so an author isn't surprised by it later.
- **D21 — Acknowledged: `pip-tools`/`pip-audit`/`pip-licenses` CI installs stay version-pinned, not hash-verified, and outside Dependabot's tracked manifests (#472).** *(2026-09-08; extended same day to add `pip-licenses`, round-1 review finding on PR #481)* `ci.yml` installs all three straight from PyPI by exact version pin in a `run:` step, unlike `requirements.txt`'s hash-verified install — a trade-off each step's own comment already reasoned through (CI-only tooling, never shipped or imported by this repo's own code; a hash-pinned `requirements-dev.txt` for a handful of tools was judged not worth the added Dependabot/maintenance surface) and each carries its own revisit trigger (bump manually on a new release; move to a tracked `requirements-dev.txt` if drift becomes noticeable). #472's audit flagged that this reasoning lived only in the workflow file, not in the decision log, so it could be rediscovered as a surprise on a future sweep; `pip-licenses` (added by #472's own fix, in the same PR that recorded this decision) was initially missed from this list for the same reason the other two needed recording in the first place — caught before merge rather than on a later sweep. No behavior change — recording the existing trade-off here so it reads as a considered decision rather than an unaddressed gap next time it's audited, matching how **D19** already did this for the type-checker question.
- **D22 — Findings still open at merge: advisory by default, one configurable setting (#526).** *(user, 2026-09-25)* #526 found a genuine Major that a later round re-noted as still open and that then merged with no follow-up; nothing tied an open Major+ to the merge decision or kept it as tracked work. The owner chose **advisory by default, but easy to configure stricter**. `REVIEW.md` (read from the PR's base ref, like the rest of the convergence policy, so a PR can't loosen its own setting) gains `unresolved_findings: note | require-followup | file-followup | block` and `unresolved_threshold: Blocker | Major | Minor`, defaulting to `note` at `Major`. `note` lists every still-open finding in each posted summary and names them in one final comment on an observed merge. `require-followup` also asks for a tracked issue per finding and titles the approve-on-clean summary "approve pending follow-up". `file-followup` files one idempotent follow-up issue (`<!-- atlas-followup pr:<number> -->`) at stand-down. `block` holds `REQUEST_CHANGES` for an open finding, the one opt-in override of the Blocker-only review-state rule; it only gates merge where branch protection requires an approving review (#492), and never on a PR authored by the account the reviewer runs as, since GitHub forbids `REQUEST_CHANGES` there and the own-PR `COMMENT` substitute applies. The setting lives in `REVIEW.md` rather than `preferences.md` because it is reviewer-workflow policy (floors, cap, approve-on-clean), not a lens-judgment preference. Implemented in `commands/atlas-review-pr.md` step 5; `tests/test_unresolved_findings_policy.py` keeps the three restating files in sync.
- **D23 — Versioning stays commit-SHA; `plugin.json`'s `version` is cosmetic; D9's revisit-trigger made checkable (#519).** *(user, 2026-09-25)* The 2026-09-20 weekly audit (#347) flagged that D9's trigger, "switch to pinned semver if/when the suite stabilizes for external users," names a motivation but no condition anyone can check. Meanwhile a static `"version": "0.1.0"` had been added to `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (#389) with no recorded judgment that the trigger had fired, which D9's 2026-09-07 addendum documented but left unresolved. The owner re-affirmed D9 rather than switching: **the commit SHA is the version.** `hooks/route.sh` reports it for the installed checkout, and every merged commit ships. `plugin.json`'s `version` stays a fixed marketplace-display string, deliberately not bumped per change, and there is no `CHANGELOG`: `docs/session-log.md` and `git log` are the change history. **Revisit when any one of these holds, whichever comes first:** (1) a consumer asks, in an issue or PR, to pin the suite by version or release tag rather than by commit; (2) the first PR from an account other than the owner or the repo's own automation is merged; (3) **2027-03-31** arrives with neither having happened (a scheduled re-look, not a presumption that semver is due). Semver adoption, when it comes, means bumping `plugin.json` per content change, adding a `CHANGELOG`, and a CI check that fails on skill-content changes without a bump.
- **D24 — A manual, draft-only reviewer service lives in this repo as `atlas_reviewer/` (a scoped step under Q24, not its resolution).** *(user, 2026-11-06)* **Where the code is:** the `atlas_reviewer/` package lands through the stack split from #598 (steps 2-8, #601-#607). Until step 8 merges, a tree without those steps has no `atlas_reviewer/`, and links into it resolve only on those branches. The owner approved one manual review path. An operator-triggered controller takes a PR event (repo, PR number, head SHA), reuses CI evidence (check runs, workflow runs and logs, artifact metadata) only when it matches that exact head SHA, runs extra tools in a fresh, credential-free Sprite (deny-all egress set and read back from the outside API, external deadline, destroy-only cleanup), and writes a review **draft** to stdout or a file. It publishes nothing to GitHub, has no webhooks, no queue, no billing or BYOK, and runs one review at a time. It lives here as a separate top-level package with its own entry point, tests, and CI steps, on the repo's existing Python 3.12 / pytest-94% / ruff / mypy stack with stdlib-only adapters (amended by D25: one vetted dependency for App-token signing). It consumes the skill bundle (the collapsed `reviewing-a-change` entrypoint) as data and changes nothing under `skills/`, `collapsed/`, or `tooling/`. It is deliberately separate from the private webhook router. Harness and model provider sit behind small ports; only an offline provider ships, so tests and `--dry-run` need no credentials (D25 adds a capped, opt-in OpenRouter provider; offline stays the default). Evidence for any other SHA, or missing evidence, is UNKNOWN, never a pass. Its design admits PR text to a prompt only as redacted text (amended by D33: the diff is repository content and goes to the model fenced but unredacted, with committed secrets flagged instead). Design and adapters: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md) (as of 2026-11-06 the live-run gaps named here were a pre-minted App token, an unverified Sprites exec shape, and no provider or model chosen; amended by D25, which mints the App token in-process and pins the model, and by D28, which settled the exec request shape; the current list is that plan's *Live-run gaps*). **Q24 stays open.** This is one controlled execution substrate for a manual path, not a decision to migrate the review pipeline. Revisit when a live run is attempted, or before adding any write path to GitHub, which would need its own decision.
- **D25 — The reviewer service's live-readiness slice: OpenRouter pilot settings, in-process App tokens, bounded evidence ingestion (amends D24).** *(user, 2026-11-07)* Still draft-only and still a scoped step under Q24. **Provider:** OpenRouter replaces the earlier Anthropic Messages API suggestion. The key comes from `ATLAS_OPENROUTER_API_KEY`. The model is exactly `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`, with no fallback models, no plugins, no web search, and provider routing pinned (`only`, `allow_fallbacks: false`) so OpenRouter cannot substitute. A response from another model is rejected. Live inference is off unless `--provider openrouter --allow-live-inference` is passed; offline stays the default. **Hard limits, enforced in code before any request:** at most **3 model calls per review, retries included** (the owner explicitly rejected a research suggestion to allow a fourth); a bounded input, max output, and reasoning budget; and a **$3.00 per-review cost reservation** computed from the model's live catalog prices at the highest listed tier. Unknown or unparseable pricing or limits fail closed. A call whose worst case could push spend past $3, or past the key's remaining credit, is refused. A **preflight** reads the key's own status and refuses to run unless the key has a hard limit of at most $5. **GitHub auth:** short-lived installation tokens are minted in-process from `ATLAS_GITHUB_APP_ID`, `ATLAS_GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` and `ATLAS_GITHUB_INSTALLATION_ID`, read-only and scoped to one repository, and cached until shortly before expiry. This adds `PyJWT[crypto]`, used by the controller only. **Addendum (2026-11-12):** the OpenTelemetry packages (`opentelemetry-api`, `opentelemetry-sdk`, `opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-proto-http`, and the dependencies they pull in) are runtime dependencies too, for the optional trace export in D45. `PyJWT[crypto]` is not the only one. **Evidence:** every GitHub list is paginated to a hard page cap; job logs and artifact ZIPs are fetched only for the matched head SHA, size-, count- and ratio-capped, and parsed in memory as text, never extracted or executed. Every limit hit is listed in the draft, and whatever it left unread is UNKNOWN. **Sprites:** exec follows the published OpenAPI document, and a tool-availability probe feeds UNKNOWN for missing tools. The undocumented exec response framing is checked by a one-off `probe-runner` the owner runs from a trusted machine. All of this is fixture-tested only. **Provider slugs, corrected the same day:** `provider.only` names `amazon-bedrock`, `google-vertex/global`, `google-vertex/us`, and `google-vertex/europe`. The routing docs say a bare slug matches every region of that provider, and a read-only check of the public provider list found no bare `google-vertex`, only those three region slugs. Bedrock's region slugs were reported and not named, so they are not pinned. A slug outside this set is refused before any request. Still unverified against live responses: the Sprites exec response body, the GitHub and OpenRouter response shapes, and the input-token bound. Remaining live setup: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md#live-run-gaps). Revisit with the first live run's usage and cost figures. **Addendum (2026-11-11, [#621](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/621)):** Brandon decided at 2:59 AM CT on 2026-11-11, via delegation, to change the controller rather than set `include_byok_in_limit` through OpenRouter's management API. The first live Fly review was blocked at preflight, which refused a key whose `include_byok_in_limit` was present and not true (a missing field was treated as true). OpenRouter's key-limit guide says the field defaults to false, so a key created without setting it does not count BYOK spend against its credit limit (https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/best-practices/capping-shared-agent-keys, fetched 2026-11-11). Brandon found no toggle for the flag on the website, on key creation or on edit. That guide says the dashboard toggle is shown once the account has BYOK provider keys configured. **Decision.** Preflight still refuses a key with no credit limit, a limit over $5, or no credit left, and it accepts a key whose `include_byok_in_limit` is false or absent. That run is in no-BYOK mode. A model response is used only when `usage.is_byok` is boolean false. `true`, a missing field, any other value, or an HTTP 200 whose body cannot be parsed ends the review with a boundary error (exit 3) and no further model call, and no draft is written, so the call is not a partial review. Spend accounting adds `cost_details.upstream_inference_cost` unless `usage.is_byok` is boolean false (the rule for that case is the 2026-11-11 #623 addendum below). A missing or non-boolean `is_byok` still adds a reported upstream cost. A missing upstream field keeps the full reservation only when `is_byok` is true; an accepted call that omits both the flag and the upstream field settles `usage.cost`. A present, non-null, unreadable upstream value keeps the reservation on any response, including an explicitly non-BYOK one. A null upstream keeps the reservation unless `is_byok` is exactly false. In no-BYOK mode a refused response is marked BYOK before settlement, so a missing upstream cost on that call keeps the reservation. When the flag is true, a BYOK response is still accepted and its upstream cost still counts. **Why a missing `is_byok` fails closed.** OpenRouter's usage cookbook shows `is_byok: false` on its Responses API example and omits the field from its Chat Completions example, and the SDK types the field as optional (https://openrouter.ai/docs/cookbook/administration/usage-accounting, fetched 2026-11-11). Treating absence as non-BYOK would accept an unlabelled BYOK call, whose `usage.cost` is only OpenRouter's fee, so the upstream charge would sit outside the key limit. A review that stops is the conservative result. Revisit if a live Chat Completions response shows that ordinary non-BYOK calls always send `is_byok: false`. **Addendum (2026-11-11, after the first completed live Fly review, [#623](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/623)):** One call on PR #585 (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`, 36,614 tokens in, 576 out, `usage.is_byok` false) moved the key's usage from $2.27 to $2.35, a charge of $0.0790. The controller settled $0.1580. The raw `usage` object was not captured. OpenRouter's usage-accounting page calls `cost` the total amount charged to the account and `cost_details.upstream_inference_cost` the upstream provider's charge, and says that when usage is read back by generation ID that upstream field is only available for BYOK requests and is otherwise 0 or null (https://openrouter.ai/docs/cookbook/administration/usage-accounting, fetched 2026-11-11). The same page's Chat Completions example reports both `cost` and a non-null `upstream_inference_cost` and omits `is_byok`. The generation API example shows `is_byok: false` together with both `total_cost` and a non-zero `upstream_inference_cost` (https://openrouter.ai/docs/api-reference/get-a-generation, fetched 2026-11-11). Those examples do not say whether a non-BYOK `cost` already includes the upstream amount (verify). The SDK says that on a BYOK request `cost` is the OpenRouter fee only (https://openrouter.ai/docs/client-sdks/typescript/models/decisionsresponseusage, fetched 2026-11-11). **Decision.** When `usage.is_byok` is boolean false the controller settles `usage.cost` alone, as the controller's policy and not a confirmed OpenRouter fact, with two over-counts: a readable `cost_details.upstream_inference_cost` above `usage.cost` settles at the upstream amount, and a present, non-null, unreadable upstream value keeps the full reservation; a null or absent upstream, or one equal to or below `usage.cost` (the PR #585 case, $0.0790), settles `usage.cost`. A null upstream keeps the reservation unless `is_byok` is exactly false. With no usable `usage.cost` the call is priced from the highest catalog rates and upstream is still not added. The settlement line names the raw upstream value, cut to 200 characters. Every other `is_byok` value still adds a reported upstream cost. Revisit when a live raw `usage` object is captured, which would settle whether a non-BYOK `cost` already includes the upstream amount. **Update (2026-11-12):** OpenRouter's `reasoning.max_tokens` is a hint, not a cap. The one-shot reservation prices the whole output cap at the completion rate plus the reasoning rate, so a reply that reports more reasoning tokens than the hint still fits the reservation. The 3-call ceiling, the $3.00 ceiling, the 200,000 input cap, and the 24,000 output / 12,000 reasoning-hint defaults are unchanged. **Update (2026-11-12, D51):** the model and the provider allowlist are configurable. The default model is still `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5` and the default allowlist is still those four slugs. `zdr: true`, `data_collection: deny`, `allow_fallbacks: false`, `require_parameters: true`, and `max_price` are unchanged.
- **D26 — The reviewer service's pilot-driven fixes (amends D24 and D25).** *(user, 2026-11-07)* One live pilot (owner-run, head `9ff859b`) found: the probe and exec framing worked; the image has `python3` but no `ruff` or `shellcheck`; one model call cost $0.17; the source upload (about 3 MiB) timed out so no in-Sprite tool ran; and the model said no lens ran. Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no new live call made here. **Upload:** the exec already received the remaining deadline, so the 30-second client default was not the cause as the code reads; the cause is unknown. It now has an explicit 300-second budget and extracts with `head -c N | tar` so it does not wait for a stdin EOF (a hypothesis, not a confirmed cause), and `probe-runner` sends 3 MiB through that mechanism and reports the bytes received. **Tools:** `ruff` and `shellcheck` leave the defaults, since a deny-all, never-reused runner cannot install them and CI already reports lint. **Lenses:** the controller selects lenses from changed paths and PR text (selection replaced by D32's lens picker, cap raised to 8), inlines their `body.md` under a 60,000-character, 6-lens budget, and the draft reports included, truncated and omitted lenses (superseded by D35: a lens now runs whole or is NOT RUN with a reason, and nothing is truncated); naming a lens in a prompt without its body never ran it. **Checks:** a check run, its job (by the documented `check_run_url`), the job's log and its workflow run count as one independent check, and an unexplained run failure stays independent. **Controller notes:** a code-generated draft section (runner cleanup proof, exec failures, tool results, omissions, lens coverage) is also given to the model as established fact. **Truncation:** a diff, PR body or issue body cut at its cap is an omission naming the files the model never saw (the PR body cap is now part of D38's conversation block, whose every cut and dropped remark is an omission). **Sweep** takes the single-review lock. **OpenRouter:** the key counter can lag a call by about 90 seconds (pilot observation); it is read only by the preflight, so a $0 readback is not a mismatch. Still unverified live: the Sprites exec response body for a large upload, the GitHub and OpenRouter response shapes beyond what the pilot's draft implies, and the input-token bound (the prompt is now larger). Details: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md#first-live-pilot-owner-reported-2026-11-07-head-9ff859b). Revisit after the next live pilot.
- **D27 — The reviewer service's lifecycle telemetry and risk-ordered diff coverage (amends D26).** *(user, 2026-11-07)* The second owner-run pilot (head `d63f087`) passed a 3 MiB stdin probe in about 2 seconds, then failed the review with an unlabelled `POST …/exec failed: The read operation timed out`, and its one model call saw 13 of 77 files. Which exec timed out is unknown: only upload errors were labelled. Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. **Telemetry:** one `EventSink` (JSONL, plus a summary table in the draft and `probe-runner` output) gets a redacted event for every phase (fetches, preflight, create, deny-all set and read-back, each exec with purpose, timeout, bytes, frames, exit and duration, upload, unpack, each tool, each model call, destroy and verify), and every boundary error is re-raised with its phase, step, duration and time into the run. **Runner:** upload and unpack are separate execs and the upload's byte count is confirmed. **Probe:** `probe-runner --sequence review` runs the review's exec sequence on a real checkout with no model. **Diff:** risk-ordered first-fit parts, one model call each, within the existing 3-call (retries included), $3 and preflight caps, with the call cap and per-call characters as explicit config (`--max-model-calls`, `--diff-chars-per-call`; D32 changed the per-call default from 60,000 to 176,000); unsent and unreviewed files are listed by risk tier and coverage is never reported complete unless it is. Details: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md#pilot-fix-decisions-d27). Revisit after the next live probe.
- **D28 — One exec request shape, and fail-fast on silent execs (amends D27).** *(user, 2026-11-07)* The owner's `probe-runner --sequence review --tools review` at `fd56ca0` passed create, egress, tool check, upload (3,062,571 bytes, 0.25 s) and unpack (0.33 s). Then `python3 --version` with no stdin timed out at 135 s. In code, the tool check, unpack and version execs were identical requests apart from the third `cmd` value and the timeout. The cause is not the request shape; it is unknown. The leading candidate is how `python3` is provided in the image, the first binary ever executed rather than only resolved. The old blocking read also discarded partial output, so "no frames" was not measured. Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. **Decision:** every exec is built by `sprites_api.exec_request` (`cmd` repeated, always `stdin=true`, body exactly the stdin bytes with an explicit `Content-Length`, never `env`/`dir`/`path`/`tty`). Every script is wrapped by `runner.exec_argv`: start marker, exactly N stdin bytes then EOF via `head -c N` or `/dev/null`, exit marker. Responses are streamed. A no-input exec with no byte in `--first-frame-seconds` (default 30) fails with a stage-aware label, and a stall reports the bytes and frames that arrived. The tool check reports each binary's resolved path and link target. Details: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md#third-live-probe-and-the-exec-shape-decisions-d28). Revisit after the next live probe.
- **D29 — Tools run a controller-chosen Python by absolute path, under a minimal environment (amends D28).** *(user, 2026-11-07)* The owner's probe at `9805cfc` got the start marker after the unpack, then `python3 --version` hung to 135 s. The tool check had resolved `python3` to `/.sprite/bin/python3`, a pyenv shim. Pyenv selects a version from `.python-version` in the cwd or its parents, this repo pins `3.12`, and the image's default is 3.13.x. **Hypothesis, unconfirmed:** the PR's pin makes the shim select an uninstalled version and hang. Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. **Decision:** every exec runs under `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=/tmp LANG=C.UTF-8 /bin/sh -c`. This is set in the shared wrapper, not by the API's `env`/`dir` parameters, so the D28 request shape is unchanged. Before the upload, one exec from an empty directory reports the interpreters under `/.sprite/languages/python/pyenv/versions/*/bin/python3` and `/usr/bin/python3`. The controller accepts only those exact path shapes and picks the newest pyenv version, else the system one, recording path and version. Python tools must name it as `{python}` and run it with `-I`, so PR files can choose neither the interpreter nor a tool binary; with none resolved, a Python tool is UNKNOWN. `probe-runner --sequence review` adds non-fatal diagnostics that test the hypothesis: the shim read as text, the installed versions, the trusted interpreter from the checkout, the shim with `PYENV_VERSION`, and finally bare `python3 --version` bounded to 15 s and labelled expected-may-hang. Details: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md#fourth-live-probe-and-the-trusted-interpreter-decisions-d29). Revisit after the next live probe.
- **D30 — Third-pilot findings are verified against code before fixing; untrusted values in the controller notes are quoted data (amends D26 and D27).** *(user, 2026-11-07)* The owner's third pilot at `a246625` ran end to end and its draft raised five findings. The draft is model output, so each was checked at `a246625`: four real, one partial. Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. **Decision:** the assignment redactor (amended by D33: it no longer runs on the diff, only on PR, issue, CI and runner text and the published draft) matches keywords inside longer names (`OPENAI_API_KEY`, `client_secret`) with bounded name parts; archive entries are charged their declared size before decompressing, so a failed read still spends the budget; streaming execs get one cumulative cutoff before the body, so trickled headers cannot outlast the window (the non-streaming transport is unchanged); a failed diff fetch makes no model call and reports NOT REVIEWED; CI workflows, dependency manifests, lockfiles and build hooks form a risk tier right after security, matched before any directory; and every PR-, runner- or upstream-controlled value in the controller notes appears only through `notes.quote_untrusted`, a one-line JSON string literal in a code span, with the notes intro and system prompt saying such values are data. Details: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md#third-live-pilot-and-the-hardening-decisions-d30). Revisit after the next live pilot.
- **D31 — PROPOSAL, not decided: a lens-based agentic reviewer on the controller and a read-only Sprite.** *(proposed 2026-11-07, awaiting the owner; direction accepted by D34, budgets still pending; D34 replaces this entry's "output redacted" rule for bridge results, see below)* Run one tool-using model loop per review lens instead of one prompt per diff part. Reuse the suite's existing machinery unchanged:
  - the picker `skills/choosing-review-lenses`, depth modes and floors from `skills/manifest.yaml` `modes:`;
  - each lens's `body.md` verbatim;
  - `reference/tool-evidence.md`, and `reference/synthesis.md` for the finding contract, merge and coverage;
  - `REVIEW.md`, `auditing-a-repository` for audit mode;
  - the reviewer service's `ReviewBudget` reservations, which give each lens a slice.

  Only four things are new: the controller-run loop, a closed set of read-only bridge tools the controller runs in the deny-all, credential-free Sprite (`git diff BASE...HEAD`, list, read ranges, grep, log, blame, the existing checks; each bounded in bytes and time, and its output redacted and fenced as untrusted; amended by D33: output that is repository content, such as `git diff`, reads and grep, is fenced but not redacted, and is scanned for committed secrets), the security boundaries, and the credentials split (every key stays on the controller). It can't run under D25's 3-call, $3.00 cap: a rough estimate is about $1.1 per lens uncached, so the cap is the first owner decision it needs. Update (2026-11-12, D43): the per-review reservation is $5, and the one-token-per-byte input bound stays. The reservation prices the whole output cap at the completion rate plus the reasoning rate. Design and open questions: [`plans/2026-11-07-lens-based-agentic-reviewer.md`](plans/2026-11-07-lens-based-agentic-reviewer.md). Nothing was built when this was proposed. Since then, the shared PR context is built offline (D38) and the read-only bridge is built offline (D39). The lens loop is not built. **Update (2026-11-12, D40):** D40 supersedes the status "The lens loop is not built": the offline controller loop and one attested lens are built, and the loop calls the bridge. No live run had been made when D40 landed; the 2026-11-12 probe is recorded in D41.
- **D32 — The reviewer service fits diff parts to the 200k-token bound and runs the repo's own lens picker (amends D26 and D27).** *(user, 2026-11-07)* Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. **Part size:** `--diff-chars-per-call` defaults to the whole prompt room under the token check, `min(200k input, 200k assumed context − 24k output)` = 176,000 characters. The suggested ~450k would need a characters-per-token ratio, and the input bound deliberately assumes none (one token per UTF-8 byte), so it could never pass. `diffs.fit_diff` measures every planned prompt and shrinks the part size from the byte-largest actual part until the largest prompt fits. One full-room call reserves at most about $1.60 at the top fixture tier, under the unchanged $3.00, and a later call is reserved only after earlier ones settle. The 3-call cap is unchanged. A diff over capacity is PARTIAL: "too large for one review pass", with the unsent files listed by risk. (Amended by D33: parts are cut from the unredacted diff, so no file is lost to redaction before fitting.) **Lenses:** the path-and-keyword route digest in `lenses.py` is retired. `picker.py` runs `skills/choosing-review-lenses/SKILL.md` as one model call at the operator's `--depth`, with the reply parsed strictly. Triage, a 1-call budget or an unusable reply runs the critical tier from `docs/review-depth-modes.md`. The picker call is one of the 3 calls. Details: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md#diff-fitting-and-the-repos-own-lens-picker-d32). Revisit after the next live pilot.
- **D33 — The PR diff goes to the model unredacted; committed secrets are flagged, not hidden (amends D24, D30, D31 and D32).** *(user, 2026-11-07)* Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. **Why:** the diff is already in the repository, and other reviewers (CodeRabbit, Copilot) read it raw. A committed secret is already exposed and needs flagging and rotating, not hiding. Regex redaction of code also silently dropped or mangled review input. On #601's own live run, an unterminated private-key BEGIN in a test file swallowed the four files after it (`pyproject.toml`, `requirements.in`, `requirements.txt` and a test), it mangled type annotations, and it missed `token: str = "..."`. **Decision:** the diff and the changed-path list reach the model as `RepoText`, which is unredacted but still inside the per-prompt nonce fence as untrusted data. The controller scans each file of the diff with `redact.detect_secrets`, which reuses the redaction patterns. Each hit becomes a controller-generated HIGH finding in the draft: "possible committed secret in `<path>`; rotate if real", with the file line, side and pattern label, never the value. Detection removes no text. The system prompt tells the model never to repeat a value, and the scanned values are scrubbed from the finished draft as a backstop, each untrusted string before it is encoded into a table cell or a quoted span (so a value containing a pipe, a backslash or a backtick cannot survive by being rewritten first). The status line and the warning's own words are not scrubbed, so a value cannot rewrite the review event. Redaction stays for PR and issue text, CI logs (redacted before the tail is kept), artifacts, runner and tool output, and anything quoted into the published draft, since those can carry runtime secrets that are not in the repository. The controller still lists, as omissions with PARTIAL coverage, any file the diff names that the fitted selection does not account for. **Where the code is:** detection and `RepoText` are in step 2 ([#601](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/601)), the controller wiring and draft section in step 7 ([#606](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/606)), and the README in step 8 ([#607](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/607)). **Accepted risks:** the raw diff, including any committed secret, goes to the model provider. The scrub matches exact values only, so a model that paraphrases or partly quotes a secret the scan missed can still echo it. Assignment detection has false positives and negatives. Revisit if a posted draft ever echoes a secret, or before any write path to GitHub.
- **D34 — Agentic reviewer direction (sets the direction for D31; budgets pending).** *(user, 2026-11-08)* The owner accepted a research note's recommendations as proposed: "I can roll with recommendations as proposed for now." No live run is approved. **(1) Phase 0:** the one-shot reviewer on #601-#607 stays mergeable and is the fallback. Agentic mode is built beside it, and one-shot is retired only after measured pilots. **(2) Lens loops:** each selected lens runs a tool loop over a Sprite checkout of head against base, plus the default branch when needed, at controller-fixed SHAs. When the base is not the default branch, a third read-only snapshot is staged at the pinned DEFAULT SHA. The loop must start with that lens's `body.md` injected. A review that names a lens without its body counts as not run, and the controller retries once and then escalates. D31's source-read attestation, quote checks and ID-reconciled merge stand. **(3) Context:** the reviewer gets the full PR conversation, with about a 64 KiB budget and omissions named, instead of the 2,000-character body cap. It also gets branch-wide CI evidence labelled by SHA. Only head-SHA evidence can support a current pass, and older evidence stays visible and marked stale. Missing evidence stays UNKNOWN. **Secret rule (extends D33 to every bridge result; replaces D31's "every bridge result is redacted"):** repository content, whether changed or unchanged, is not redacted. Each result is scanned, every hit becomes a HIGH controller finding by path, line and pattern, never the value, and flagged values are scrubbed from the model's findings, the merge and the draft. Tool and test output, CI logs, artifacts, PR and issue text stay redacted. **(4) Security:** every key stays on the controller, which places every model call. The Sprite stays deny-all and credential-free. Free, self-hostable tools (`ruff`, `mypy`, Semgrep CE, Gitleaks and others) are baked into the image, and lenses get summarized tool hits, not raw logs. Tools that run PR code, such as `pytest`, run in a second disposable Sprite holding only the head tree, so they cannot change the Git state the lenses read. **(5) Scope and scoring:** D25's 3-call, $3.00 caps and model pin are unchanged until the owner sets agentic numbers. Agentic mode starts triage-only, with the four critical-tier lenses. It uses a small controller loop of our own, not the Claude Agent SDK. Progress is scored by issue-hit precision and recall on owner-labelled pilot PRs, plus coverage honesty. **Pending the owner:** a provisional agentic budget before phase 3's first live run, then the measured agentic dollar reservation, key limit, call and turn caps, wall clocks, the PR-conversation and CI budgets, tool timeouts, pool size, and the pilot bar for retiring one-shot. Design and phased plan: [`agentic-reviewer.md`](agentic-reviewer.md). Since then, phase 1 (the context in (3)) is built offline (D38), and phase 2's read-only bridge is built offline (D39). The lens loop is not built. Revisit when the owner sets the agentic budgets, or after the first measured pilot. **Update (2026-11-12, D40):** D40 supersedes the status "The lens loop is not built": the offline controller loop and one attested lens are built, and the loop calls the bridge. No live run had been made when D40 landed; the 2026-11-12 probe is recorded in D41. **Update (2026-11-12, D43):** the measured per-review dollar reservation stays $5, and the one-token-per-byte input bound stays. The reservation prices the whole output cap at the completion rate plus the reasoning rate. The other items in the pending list above are not that decision.
- **D35 — A lens runs whole or is NOT RUN, and the lens picker is skipped when no pick could fit (amends D26 and D32).** *(user, 2026-11-10)* Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. **Whole or NOT RUN:** no lens body or instruction text is cut. `lenses.plan_lenses` takes the critical tier first, then the picker's names in rank order. A lens that does not fit what is left of the 60,000-character budget whole, or that comes past the depth's cap (triage 4, review 8, comprehensive 40 lenses), is NOT RUN, with the reason and its size. A name with no `body.md` in the bundle is NOT RUN and takes no slot. The Lenses line of the controller notes lists every lens as run whole or NOT RUN. **Current coverage:** today's critical-tier bodies are 34,750 characters (`tracing-correctness-and-invariants`), 15,000 (`sweeping-for-security`), 22,656 (`reviewing-migration-and-data-safety`) and 10,984 (`reviewing-concurrency-and-async`). Tracing and security run; migration and concurrency are NOT RUN. The 10,250 characters left are fewer than the smallest other lens (`reviewing-test-quality`, 11,015). So every depth runs 2 of the 4 critical lenses and 0 picker lenses, pinned by `test_the_real_bundle_runs_exactly_two_critical_lenses_whole`. **Picker skip:** when the room left after the critical tier is smaller than every non-critical lens body, `picker.select_lenses` makes no picker call and the notes say why. The controller then gives that call to diff review. **Known limitation:** the inlined skill text still points at `reference/*.md` files the model cannot open. That text is left whole and unedited, not cut or rewritten. **Where the code is:** whole-or-NOT-RUN and the picker skip are in step 6 ([#605](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/605)), the call accounting and notes in step 7 ([#606](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/606)), and the README in step 8 ([#607](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/607)). **Next:** per-lens fan-out, one call per lens (the D31/D34 direction), is the next PR after #607. It is what would lift coverage beyond 2 of 4. Details: [`plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md`](plans/2026-11-06-manual-reviewer-service.md#whole-lenses-and-the-picker-skip-d35). Revisit when fan-out lands.
- **D36 — Partial file coverage blocks approval by default (amends D27 and D32).** *(user, 2026-11-10)* Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. Before this, a review that left files unreviewed said so only in the PARTIAL coverage line and the omissions table, and the draft carried no review event. That is easy to miss, and in an agentic pipeline with no human at review time, nothing need act on it. **Decision:** each diff part's review ends with a proposed GitHub review event (`Review event: APPROVE`, `COMMENT` or `REQUEST_CHANGES`), parsed strictly from the reply's last line; the controller decides. APPROVE needs every part to propose it, and any REQUEST_CHANGES wins. If any changed file was not reviewed in full, the review never approves: APPROVE becomes COMMENT, marked review incomplete. That covers a file unseen for lack of room; one in a part whose call failed, was refused by the call or cost cap, or was used up by a retry; one answered by the offline provider; one cut to fit; one dropped before the calls; and one planned into a part but never narrated. Fail closed: coverage that cannot be determined counts as incomplete. That covers no diff tied to the event SHA; a fetched body that is empty or names no file; and a diff whose file count differs from GitHub's `changed_files` on the pull read after the diff fetch (an earlier read can go stale while the head SHA stays the same), or a pull payload with no such count. Lens NOT RUN status does not trigger this rule, so D35's interim 2 of 4 critical lenses is accepted. The rule is about files only. **Output:** the draft's first line is a machine-readable status comment (`review_event`, `proposed_event`, `coverage_complete`, `changed_files`, `unreviewed_files`; no paths). The body opens with the review event and, when coverage is incomplete, a "REVIEW INCOMPLETE -- not approvable" warning. The warning gives the count and names the unreviewed files, quoted as data, highest risk first, at most 100. The CLI's `review` command exits 4 when coverage is incomplete, so a pipeline can gate on coverage by the exit status alone. A dry run always exits 4, because no model reviewed anything. **Where the code is:** `verdict.py` and the controller and draft wiring are in step 7 ([#606](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/606)), and the exit status and README in step 8 ([#607](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/607)). This decides what partial coverage may produce, not whether anything may be excluded from review at all, which stays open as Q25. Revisit with Q25, or before any write path to GitHub. **Addendum (2026-11-10, [#620](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/620)):** Brandon chose the remaining exit codes on 2026-11-10, via delegation. With complete coverage, `review` exits 5 when the review requests changes and 0 when it approves or comments, so the exit status alone separates incomplete, changes requested and the rest, but not APPROVE from COMMENT; the status comment and the printed summary carry that.
- **D37 — The controller deploys as a Fly one-off Machine, with no public service and no standing Machine (names the host for D24's manual path; does not close Q24).** *(user, 2026-11-10)* **Decision.** One operator-started review at a time, on a Fly app in the org `personal`. `fly.toml` names the app, the primary region, and the Dockerfile, and has no `[http_service]`, no `[[services]]`, and no `[processes]`. The image is published with `fly deploy --build-only --push`, which the Docker blueprint says builds and pushes and does not deploy, under a label naming the commit; each review records the image digest with its output. Every flyctl command runs in a subshell with an org token for `personal` (`FLY_ACCESS_TOKEN` unset, `FLY_API_TOKEN` set) and names its app with `--app` or its org with `--org personal`. **A review** is `fly machine run --restart no` with no `--port` and no `--rm`. The machine-run doc says that command takes the app from `--app` and disregards the rest of `fly.toml`, so the no-service and no-restart properties are on the command. The review writes its draft and telemetry to the Machine's disk (`--out`, `--events`). A Machine's storage is ephemeral, a blank slate on every start, so the entrypoint, given `ATLAS_HOLD_SECONDS`, writes the CLI's exit code to a file and keeps the Machine running. The operator polls for that file, copies the draft and telemetry off with `fly ssh sftp`, stops the Machine, waits for it to stop, and destroys it. A hung review is stopped with SIGINT, which the entrypoint passes on to the CLI as SIGTERM (a non-interactive `sh` starts the CLI with SIGINT ignored) so its `finally` deletes the runner Sprite; the CLI ignores any later stop, so a second one cannot skip that delete, and holds a first stop that arrives during the delete until it is done. The Machine is then destroyed with `--force`. Success from `fly machine run` means the Machine started, not that the review finished; the CLI's exit (0, 4 for incomplete coverage under D36, or 5 for complete coverage and a review that requests changes) is the Machine process's. **Runner names.** A live review names its Sprite after the event, `cqa-rev-pr<PR>-<first 12 of the SHA>-<8 hex of a SHA-256 over repo, PR, and SHA>`, prints that name before creating it, and exits 3 when a Sprite already has it. The create is the claim: when two reviews of one commit race, Sprites refuses the second create, that review exits 3, and a review deletes only a Sprite its own create made. The runner ledger is on the review Machine's disk and goes with it, and `fly apps destroy` does not remove Sprites, so `sweep --event` and `sweep --runner` destroy a runner by name from any machine. No lock covers a sweep by name, nor a ledger name whose create was never confirmed and which a review on another Machine may now own, so any sweep with something to delete refuses to run without `--force-live`, given after checking that no review Machine is running. **Secrets:** `ATLAS_GITHUB_APP_ID`, `ATLAS_GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY`, `ATLAS_GITHUB_INSTALLATION_ID`, `ATLAS_SPRITES_TOKEN`, and `ATLAS_OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, set with `fly secrets import --stage` from a file kept outside the checkout (names only; `--stage` skips deployment). `.gitignore` and `.dockerignore` refuse `.env`, `*.env`, `secrets.env`, and `*.pem`. The secrets doc says an app's secrets are environment variables on every Machine of that app, Fly Launch or not, and a staged secret is available on Machines started after the command. The controller process is what reads them. This change does not pass them into a Sprite: `sprites_api.exec_request` still sends no `env`, `dir`, `path`, or `tty`, and create sends only the Sprite's name; the Sprites token is the control-plane `Authorization` header. The GitHub private key is used only to mint a read-only installation token (`contents`, `checks`, `actions`, `pull_requests` read). The OpenRouter key is read only with `--provider openrouter --allow-live-inference`. The redactor still scrubs the private key, the Sprites token, the OpenRouter key, and minted installation tokens from the draft, telemetry, and model-visible text. The App ID and installation ID are not in that scrub list. **One at a time:** the file lock and the runner ledger are on the Machine's disk. They do not cover a second `fly machine run`. The operator starts a review only when `fly machine list` shows no review Machine, and the Machine is named after the PR and commit. There is still no webhook and no queue. **Image:** `python:3.12.16-slim-bookworm` pinned to the registry manifest-list digest `sha256:935c3c5b12357a7e10caa9c1e6ca5692344d97cf229832ef584be2cec9fc03df`, which `registry-1.docker.io` returned for that tag on 2026-11-10. `pip install --require-hashes --only-binary=:all: -r requirements.txt` uses the repo's full hashed lock. The process user is uid 10001. The event JSON is not a secret; the entrypoint writes `ATLAS_EVENT_JSON` to a file that user owns. **Accepted costs:** every app secret reaches every Machine of the app, `probe-runner` and `sweep` included, rather than one app per credential set; the runtime image carries the whole dev lock, because there is no separate runtime lock; a held Machine is billed until it is destroyed. **Teardown:** `fly apps destroy`, after every runner Sprite is destroyed. **Code facts:** this repository does not create the app or set a secret, and no Fly, Sprite, model, or GitHub call was made for this change. Runbook: [`atlas_reviewer/deploy-fly.md`](../atlas_reviewer/deploy-fly.md). **Observed (2026-11-10, operator deploy; not part of the decision).** An operator created app `atlas-reviewer` in `personal` and ran one offline-provider review of #605 in the earlier shape, `fly machine run --rm` with the draft on stdout. `fly deploy --build-only --push` accepted the no-service `fly.toml` once the Depot builder had timed out and `--depot=false` was used. The pinned base image was pulled. The process ran as uid 10001 and exited 4, shown by the logs line `Main child exited normally with code: 4` and by `exit_code=4` on `fly machine status` after `--rm`. The controller destroyed the Sprite, and a check found it absent. No secret values appeared in `fly logs`. `fly secrets import --stage` was enough; `fly secrets deploy` was not run. A `fly logs --no-tail` fetch after `--rm` returned only the last 100 of the draft's 137 lines, which is why the decision now holds the Machine and copies the draft off its disk. That run did not exercise the held shape, a complete review, or live inference. Q24 stays open. Revisit after the first held review on Fly or the first Fly run with `--provider openrouter --allow-live-inference`, whichever comes first. **Addendum (2026-11-12, D48):** this one-off app, and `atlas_reviewer/deploy-fly.md`, still have no webhook and no queue. The webhook receiver is a separate process. It is not this Fly app.
- **D38 — Agentic phase 1: the full PR conversation and branch-wide CI evidence in the shared context (implements D34 (3); amends D26 and D30).** *(agent, 2026-11-10, within D34's accepted direction; the budgets are working defaults pending the owner; D37 is the Fly deploy decision recorded on [#613](https://github.com/brandondees/code-quality-atlas/pull/613))* Still draft-only, offline-tested only, no live call made here. It changes the one-shot path too, because both modes share this evidence code. **Conversation:** the 2,000-character PR body is replaced by one `pull-request` block of at most 65,536 characters (`conversation.ConversationBudget`). It holds the title, author, base and head branches, draft flag, labels, requested reviewers and teams; the body, up to 16,384 characters; the subject of every PR commit, up to 200 characters each and 8,192 in all; and every review, review comment and conversation comment, up to 4,000 characters each. Each remark carries its id, author, time and the commit it was made at (`at the reviewed head SHA`, `at commit <sha12>`, or `commit not reported`), and review comments carry their path, line (or outdated line) and the comment they reply to. Commit subjects and remarks keep the newest that fit; everything older and every cut is an omission, with the dropped remark ids listed oldest first (at most 20 named, the rest counted). A pending review has no time yet and counts as the newest remark. The fit is checked on the final redacted block, since redaction can lengthen it, so the newest remarks are never cut off its end unnamed: older remarks are dropped, and listed, until it fits. A list that fails to fetch, stops at the page cap, or carries an entry that does not parse is an omission too, so nothing is left out unnamed. Every one-line PR-written field (title, author, branches, labels, reviewers, teams, commit subjects) is a JSON string, which escapes every line terminator, and every body line is quoted with a leading `>` after splitting at every terminator (`\r`, `\r\n`, U+2028, U+2029 and the rest `str.splitlines` knows).
