A commit subject ends at its first terminator of any kind. So no PR text can begin an unquoted line and forge a remark's heading, and the system prompt says so. The whole block stays PR text under D24: redacted, fenced as untrusted, redaction counted. The lens picker still sees only the title and body. Review-thread resolved state is not fetched, because the REST API does not report it (GraphQL only, (verify)). **CI:** check runs are now listed at the head SHA and at up to 20 other commits of the PR (`IngestConfig.max_ci_commits`), newest first. GitHub's docs do not say what order a PR's commits are listed in, so the order is not relied on: `parse_commits` puts each commit after its listed parents, ties by committer date, then listed position (a parent cycle falls back to date order). Workflow runs were already branch-wide. Jobs, logs and artifacts stay head-only. Commits past the cap are an omission plus one UNKNOWN per commit. A PR at GitHub's 250-commit listing limit gets a note that other commits may be missing. Every item keeps the SHA GitHub reported, so `classify` still makes anything off the head SHA stale, and only head-SHA evidence can pass. The draft adds an "Evidence at other commits of this PR (stale)" table, newest first, labelled by commit; a summary count of checks that failed at another commit and have no result at the head; and one controller note per such check (at most 10). The telemetry step for a check-run listing now names its SHA (`check runs at <sha12>`). **Not decided here:** the owner's PR-conversation and CI budgets (D34 pending list). Code: [`atlas_reviewer/conversation.py`](../atlas_reviewer/conversation.py), `gather.py`, `classify.not_rerun_failures`, `draft.py`. Revisit when the owner sets those budgets, or when a live response contradicts the (verify) above.
- **D39 — Agentic phase 2: the offline read-only git tool bridge and Option B staging (implements D31 gap 2 and D34 (2); amends D31's bridge table).** *(agent, 2026-11-10, within D34's accepted direction; the caps are D31's proposed bounds as working defaults pending the owner, and the session and unpack caps below are working defaults too)* Offline only: no live Sprite, GitHub or model call was made, and nothing calls the bridge yet (that is phase 3). **Tools:** a closed set of five, `diff_stat`, `diff_file` (`path`, byte `offset`), `list_files` (`rev`, `prefix`), `read_file` (`rev`, `path`, lines `start`-`end`, at most 400) and `grep` (`pattern`, `rev`, `prefix`, `regex`), with D31's bounds: 16 KiB and 10 s; 32 KiB a page and 10 s; 2,000 entries or 32 KiB and 10 s; 400 lines or 32 KiB and 10 s; 200 matches or 32 KiB and 15 s. `rev` is the closed set of staged revisions (BASE, HEAD, DEFAULT when staged), default HEAD, as D34 (2) set; the model never names a commit. `log`, `blame`, `run_check` and `read_lens_reference` are not built. **Arguments:** unknown tools and keys, wrong types (a boolean is not an integer), out-of-range integers, paths that are absolute, start with `-`, hold a control character or an empty, `.` or `..` segment, or pass 512 bytes, and patterns over 256 bytes or with a newline are rejected before any exec. A rejection names a known tool or `(unknown tool)`, shows no arguments, and carries a fixed code (`unknown-tool`, `bad-arguments`, `unknown-argument`, `missing-argument`, `invalid-argument`) with a controller-written reason; only unknown argument names are quoted, cut to 64 characters, so nothing the model sent can write a status line. **Session limits:** each bridge serves at most 64 calls (rejected ones count) and shows at most 1 MiB of output in all, and no call is shown more than the output left; a call past either is "session limit reached" and is not run (working defaults pending the owner, sized from D31's 12 turns per lens at a few calls each and its about 10k characters of tool output per turn). **Exec:** each call is a fixed git argv with every value shell-quoted, run through `runner.exec_argv` (`env -i`), under `timeout -k 2 <bound>` and `ulimit -f` (8 MiB of raw output under dash's 512-byte blocks, (verify) the Sprite's `/bin/sh`). Output returns base64-encoded with a header of git's exit status, the output view's, the bytes and lines of the output, and the bytes of the capped part, so the exec framing's byte stripping and in-band text cannot bend it; the controller checks the decoded part against that count, so a failed view or encoding after git succeeded is "failed", not an empty "ok". The controller reports the cut and the next `diff_file` offset, and cuts a page only between whole UTF-8 characters: an incomplete one at a cut end is left for the next page, and a `diff_file` page asked for mid-character starts at the next whole one. Exit 124 or 137, or an exec whose output stalls, is "timed out" (a coverage gap, never a finding); 153 is "output over the raw limit"; a missing or malformed reply is "unavailable". **Hardening:** git only at `/usr/bin/git` or `/bin/git`, found before any PR content is uploaded ((verify) that the Sprite image ships git, `timeout` and `base64`); D31's `-c` options plus `color.ui=never`, `core.quotePath=true` and no CRLF conversion; `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS`, `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS` (no `:(...)` magic or globs), `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`; `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` on diff; and `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes` set to `* -text !eol -filter -ident !diff !working-tree-encoding`, which outranks any in-tree `.gitattributes`, so staging stores bytes exactly and no diff driver applies. **Departures from D31's table:** `list_files` and `grep` both run with `-z`. `list_files` shows each name exactly as `read_file` takes it back, and a name `read_file` would refuse (a control character, a leading `-`, not UTF-8) as a JSON string marked with the reason. `grep` output is parsed on its NUL-separated fields before newlines (a path may hold a newline, a matched line cannot, and each record must start with the staged commit id), then re-rendered as `path:line:text`, JSON-quoting a path that is not plain printable ASCII or holds a `:`, so a file name cannot fake a line or move a match to another file; a record out of step ends the parse and marks the result cut. `diff_file` diffs both sides of a rename: the bridge lists the PR's renames once (`git diff -z --name-status --diff-filter=R`), so either path shows git's rename header, and a listing that fails or is cut makes `diff_file` unavailable. **Staging (Option B):** the merge-base (from `GET /repos/{repo}/compare/{base}...{head}`, its `merge_base_commit.sha` checked as 40-hex) and head tarballs, plus an optional default-branch tarball, each at most 9 MiB and refused before a runner exists, are uploaded and committed in one exec into a bare repository with a fixed identity and date; HEAD's parent is BASE and DEFAULT is a separate root commit. Staging reports each synthetic id, and a run that does not report every one fails. Fetching the default-branch tarball is not built (the controller does not yet read the repository's default branch). Known fidelity limit: GitHub tarballs apply `export-ignore` and `export-subst`, so such files differ from the git tree. **Unpack cap:** the 9 MiB cap bounds compressed bytes only, and gzip packs a run of one byte about 1,000 to 1, so before upload the controller walks each tarball's headers in memory (`source_archive.measure`), counting members and summing their declared sizes, and refuses an archive past 256 MiB or 50,000 members, or one that does not read as a gzip tar, at the first header over a cap and before a runner exists (working defaults pending the owner). The one-shot reviewer's source upload and `probe-runner` go through the same check (`runner.refused_archive`), so this changes the one-shot path: it now refuses, before any runner is created, an archive that is not a readable gzip tar, a repository with more than 50,000 members (files, directories and links), which can happen well under the 9 MiB compressed cap, and an archive with a member whose parent directory is not an earlier entry, as well as one that unpacks past 256 MiB. `measure` reads through every gzip member (`gzip.GzipFile`), because tarfile's own stream mode stops after the first, and still reads headers without seeking. A header over a cap stops the walk. Each header is read as one 512-byte block, and a member's data is decompressed only after its header has passed every cap. **`measure` fails closed** (amended 2026-11-10, twice: three review rounds each found another header that Python's tarfile reads as the end of the archive while the runner's GNU tar skips it and keeps extracting, and a fourth found that tarfile itself applies pax keys and parses numbers differently from GNU tar, so later members or sizes went uncounted): it accepts only what `git archive` writes, in a form both readers read the same way, and refuses everything else instead of matching GNU tar case by case. `measure` no longer iterates with tarfile at all. It reads the 512-byte blocks itself, skips exactly the data bytes it counts, and keeps only counters and one 16-byte digest per directory entry, never the members, the paths or the pax keys (tarfile keeps every member in a list, so 2,000 long names of about 1 MiB each held about 2 GB). Only an all-zero block ends the archive. A header is refused for any of these: a short read; a failed checksum (the unsigned or the signed sum is accepted); no ustar magic; or a numeric field that is not plain octal. Plain octal means optional leading spaces, octal digits, then only NULs or spaces. `devmajor` and `devminor` may also be empty, as Python's writer leaves them, and both readers read that as 0. Python's `int(field, 8)` accepts `1_000` and `0o1000`, which GNU tar rejects, reading that header's data as further headers; base-256 numbers are refused as well, since `git archive` never writes them. Member types are an allowlist of what `git archive` and GitHub's tarballs write: regular file, directory, symlink and hardlink, plus pax extended and global headers and GNU long-name and long-link entries. Any other type, and a non-file member that declares data, is refused. **Pax keys:** a global header may hold only `comment`, the one key `git archive` writes there. tarfile skips a member's data by its header size and then reports a global `size`, while GNU tar skips by the global size, so a global `size=0` let a cover file's data hide a 300 MiB member that measure counted as 0 bytes and GNU tar wrote whole. A per-member pax header may hold only `path`, `linkpath` and `mtime` (`mtime` as decimal seconds), whose meaning both readers share and none of which is a size. `size`, sparse keys, `hdrcharset`, ownership, times other than `mtime`, and vendor keys are all refused, even a `size` that agrees with the header: `git archive` writes one only for a file over 8 GiB, far past the byte cap. Each record must parse as `<length> <key>=<value>\n`, with the length counting the whole record and every payload byte inside a record. A key may not repeat. That caps an entry's records at its allowed keys (one global, three per member): 1,500 pax entries of 1 MiB of tiny records, which took tarfile about 130 s of CPU, are refused at the second record of the first entry. The same rule caps the global keys carried to later members at one `comment`, since 100 global headers of 10,000 keys each held about 1.7 GB in tarfile. **Metadata caps** (working defaults pending the owner): an entry is refused before it is read when it declares more than `MAX_METADATA_BYTES`, 1 MiB (tarfile read a 512 MiB long name into memory). Metadata is also refused once all entries together declare more than `MAX_METADATA_TOTAL_BYTES`, 16 MiB, which bounds what the walk decompresses and parses for metadata. The total is sized for 50,000 members each carrying a pax path of about 300 bytes; a GitHub tarball writes a pax header only for a path or link target that does not fit the ustar name fields. One entry of each kind may precede a member; a repeated kind, or metadata with no member after it, is refused. After the end block, only zero padding may follow, at most `MAX_TRAILING_ZERO_BYTES`, 1 MiB, and an archive with no members is refused. Any exception the reader raises (for example `ValueError`, `MemoryError` or an `OSError`) is a refusal, so none escapes as anything but a `BoundaryError`. **Paths** (added 2026-11-10, round 7, in `571c4dc`; working defaults pending the owner): `ulimit -f` bounds one file, not directories, and `tar` creates every missing parent of a member, so deep paths with no directory entries made directories the walk never counted. A 1,310-byte archive made 100,101 directories and about 410 MB (reproduced here with 1,000 members of 64-segment paths: 173 KB of archive, 63,001 directories and 247 MB in 1.5 s), and the plain ustar prefix reaches about 6 million directories for 0.34 MiB. `measure` now takes each member's name as GNU tar reads it (a pax `path` or GNU long name, else the ustar `prefix/name`; a member named by both a pax path and a long name, and an old-GNU header with a nonzero prefix region, are refused) and requires: no empty, `.` or `..` segment (so no leading or doubled slash); a trailing slash only on a directory; at most `MAX_PATH_BYTES`, 4,096 (PATH_MAX); at most `MAX_PATH_DEPTH`, 64 segments (the deepest path in this repository has 8, vendored dependency trees reach the 30s); at most `MAX_PATH_COMPONENTS`, 1,000,000 segments over all members (50,000 members at an average depth of 20; this repository's archive averages 5); and that the member's parent directory appeared earlier as a directory entry. A file, symlink or hardlink is not a parent, so a symlink cannot redirect a later member. The walk keeps one 16-byte blake2b digest per directory entry (at most 50,000; 5,000 of them trace under 2 MiB). `git archive` lists every directory before what is in it, so every directory unpacking creates is a member the 50,000 cap already counted: this repository's archive has 336 directory entries, and both GitHub tarballs above have no member without an earlier parent. A 1 MiB path no longer fits the path cap. Still accepted: real GitHub tarballs of this repository at `ad275df` and `e498655` (1,281 members each, with a global `comment`), and this repository's own `git archive --prefix=src/` (1,283 members). Also accepted is a `git archive` with long paths and link targets (pax `path` and `linkpath`), unicode and non-UTF-8 names, empty and executable files, symlinks, a submodule and an `export-subst` file. **Runner-side unpack limit** (owner, 2026-11-10: Brandon approved it as defence in depth, because controller-side parsing cannot be shown to match GNU tar on every archive). The one-shot unpack and bridge staging both run each tarball through `guest_unpack.unpack_script` in the runner, against the same `UnpackLimits` the controller checks. First, before anything is written, GNU tar measures the archive itself. `tar -xOzf` streams every member's data as extraction would write it (sparse files expanded) through `head -c` into `wc -c`, and `tar -tzf` lists the members through `awk`, which counts them and the directories their paths imply: every parent of a listed path, once each, walking up only until a parent already counted, so the work is the number of new directories. Past `max_bytes`, a listing over `max_members` members, a path nested deeper than `max_path_depth`, or more than `max_members` implied parent directories, the runner refuses and extracts nothing, so it never writes past the byte cap at all and `ulimit -f`'s blind spot, directories, is closed by count instead (a listing that names every directory implies at most as many as it has members, so a real archive never trips it). A pre-check whose tar fails also stops the exec, and says why: `timeout` for status 124 or 137, `unreadable` for any other failure or an `awk` that did not answer `ok`, never a bare exit 2. Then extraction runs under `ulimit -f`, sized to `max_bytes` in 512-byte blocks (524,288 for 256 MiB), so no single file can grow past the cap even if the two tar runs disagreed; the kernel stops the write with `SIGXFSZ`. When extraction fails and the script itself finishes, it removes the partial tree and exits non-zero, so the review or the bridge session fails with a `BoundaryError` before any later step uses the tree. A kill skips that cleanup: when the controller's exec deadline, or the runner dying, stops the script first, nothing removes the tree, and the disposable runner is destroyed instead. A limit refusal names its reason (`the runner refused to unpack the source archive: ...`). The tag that carries the reason is read only from an exec that already failed, so a member name that forges it can at most reword a failure. Measuring before extracting was chosen over checking totals after extraction, which would let the over-cap bytes be written first. It costs two more decompression passes: 0.2 s for this repository's tarball, while a 300 MiB bomb is refused about 1 s in, once `head -c` closes the pipe. Every tar run in the script, in both paths, runs under `timeout -k 2 60` (`guest_unpack.EXTRACT_TIMEOUT_S` and `KILL_AFTER_S`). Round 7 (`d310c07`) gave the one-shot unpack the wrapper staging already had: before it, a runaway unpack ran until the controller's 120 s timeout and the runner was destroyed without cleanup. The exec keeps its 120 s budget, which three slow 60 s runs can exceed; the exec deadline then stops it. **Not built:** extracting into a size-limited filesystem (a `tmpfs` mount with `size=`), which would bound all disk use, inodes included. Mounting needs privileges the Sprite's exec user may not have (verify), so the member, depth and implied-directory caps bound directories instead; revisit if a live run shows mount rights. Depends on the Sprite's `/bin/sh` counting `ulimit -f` in 512-byte blocks (dash, or bash in POSIX mode; a shell counting 1 KiB blocks would allow twice the cap per file, and the pre-check would still hold the total), and on GNU tar's `-O` and `-t`, `head -c`, `awk` and `timeout` (verify). The one-shot source unpack and the bridge staging exec both use `runner.UNPACK_BUDGET_S`, 120 seconds for the whole exec. Inside the staging exec, all trees' tar runs plus the git commits share the 120 seconds. The tool check is 60 seconds and the source upload is 300 seconds. **Results:** repository content, unredacted (D34 secret rule), scanned with `detect_secrets`; each hit is reported by path, line (the revision's line, or the head side of a diff) and label, and is pre-existing outside HEAD or in a file the PR did not change. The values are kept only for scrubbing (`Bridge.flagged`) and are left out of `repr`. git's stderr is runtime text and redacted. Every call, rejected or run, is one ledger record (tool, arguments, status, bytes shown and in all, cut, seconds, secret count), and telemetry names only the tool. **Runner:** `runner.run_session` hands a body a `Session` (exec and upload) on the same disposable lifecycle as a review; the one-shot path's scripts are unchanged except its unpack, which now runs the runner-side unpack limit above. Code: [`atlas_reviewer/bridge.py`](../atlas_reviewer/bridge.py), `bridge_args.py`, `bridge_staging.py`, tested against a `FakeSprites` that runs each exec on the test machine over a fixture repository built at test time. Revisit when phase 3 wires the bridge into a lens loop, when the owner sets tool timeouts, or before the first live bridge run (the (verify) items above). **Update (2026-11-12, D40):** D40 supersedes the status "nothing calls the bridge yet (that is phase 3)": the offline lens loop calls the bridge. No live run had been made when D40 landed; the 2026-11-12 probe is recorded in D41. **Update (2026-11-17, D77):** D77 re-scopes this entry's session caps to one lens attempt: the coordinator starts the attempt's call and shown-output counters over before each attempt it runs on a bridge (`Bridge.begin_attempt`), so a lens's retry begins with the full 64-call / 1 MiB budget its predecessor spent, a refusal names the attempt that spent it, and the runner session as a whole is bounded by the review's wall clock.
- **D40 — Agentic phase 3 (offline): the controller loop and one attested lens (implements D34 (2) and D31 gaps 1 and 3 for one lens; amends D31's merge step for phase 3).** *(agent, 2026-11-11, with the owner's go-ahead to start phase 3; Brandon set provisional defaults for the five limits on 2026-11-11)* Offline only: no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, nothing is wired to the CLI, and the tests run the real bridge through `LocalSprites` with a scripted model (`fakes.ScriptedLensModel`). **Lens body injection:** the lens worker's system prompt is the controller's instructions, then the nonce fence rule, then the lens's whole `body.md` between markers that carry its SHA-256, then `synthesis.md`'s whole *Finding contract* section through its last subsection. Nothing in it is cut: a lens with no body is NOT RUN and no runner starts. **Attestation:** an attempt is attested only when the prompt handed to the model port carries text between the markers that hashes to the body's digest (checked before the first model call; a failure there is `body not delivered whole` and no call is made) and the final reply names that lens and digest. Attestation and lens states are closed types: an attempt is attested, unacknowledged, not delivered, misacknowledged or unrecorded; a lens is clean, partial, failed or NOT RUN. **Finding contract:** the final reply must be one JSON object with exactly `lens`, `body_sha256` and `findings`, each finding with the contract's closed severity, valence, route and attribution sets; anything else refuses the whole reply (`lens_contract.parse_reply`), and no refusal echoes model text. **Quote checks:** every `file:line` finding's evidence must be the cited lines of the head file, and the cited span must be the quote's line count (one line means start equals end; whitespace-insensitive per line; a one-line fragment needs at least 8 non-space characters unless it is the whole line). A wider range does not verify. Removed diff lines of that file still verify, labelled removed lines; a whole-file finding needs its path at head or in the diff. A finding that fails is listed as not verified and is never reported as a finding or counted toward the verdict. **Ledger:** `attestation.AttemptLedger`, append-only JSON lines keyed by repository, PR, head SHA, lens and body digest; each attempt is fsynced when it starts, before its first model call, and when it ends, at mode 0600, with every text field scrubbed of flagged values. Writing a record again is a no-op and the first finish counts; a torn line is skipped and counted. **Retry and escalate:** the policy reads only the ledger (`attestation.next_step`). A failed attestation, a reply outside the contract, a failed quote or an abandoned attempt is retried once, with the body injected whole again and the reasons stated; a second such outcome escalates and stands as partial or failed, never clean. A turn cap, call cap, dollar reservation, wall clock, tool gap or no-source-read outcome is partial and not retried; a failed model call is failed and not retried. An attempt that started and never finished (a crash) counts as used, so a rerun resumes the count, and a lens already settled for this head and body is reused without a runner. **Loop termination:** the final reply, the per-lens turn cap, the review's model-call cap or dollar reservation (each call reserved at the model's worst case and settled at its reported cost, a missing or invalid cost staying at the worst case), the per-lens or review wall clock, a failed model call, or the runner's deadline during a tool call. A no-findings reply counts as clean only after a `diff_file` page or a HEAD `read_file` of a changed file; a diff of a path the PR does not change is empty and does not count. Update (2026-11-12, D42): that clean rule is amended. A no-findings reply is clean only when every changed file's diff was read in full, every page, and a HEAD read does not substitute. **Coordinator:** `coordinator.review_lens` resolves the limits before any model, runner or ledger work, runs the lens in one runner session (pool size 1), mints `L<lens>-F<n>` IDs in reply order (verified or not), ranks the verified findings by severity, and sets the verdict mechanically by `synthesis.md`'s rule: a verified introduced Blocker blocks (REQUEST_CHANGES), any other verified introduced defect above nit means approve with changes (COMMENT), otherwise approve. Coverage is complete only when every selected lens settled clean and, when this review ran a runner, that runner completed (a keepalive failure is a failed runner, and its note is quoted in the draft). Update (2026-11-12, D42): that coverage rule is amended. Coverage also needs every changed file's diff read and a recorded changed-file count. Otherwise the draft's first line is a status line with `"mode": "agentic"`, `"coverage_complete": false`, `"changed_files"` and `"unreviewed_files"` as counts, not paths, followed by a REVIEW INCOMPLETE warning, and the review never approves. Possible committed secrets the tools read are listed as controller findings by path, line and pattern. **Departures from D31:** no merge model call yet (one lens needs none; the ID-reconciled merge comes with phase 4's several lenses), the first user turn is the operator's subject, any caller-supplied fenced context, and the risk-ordered changed-file list with line counts (no diff text), and no CLI entry point. **Limits:** Brandon's provisional defaults (2026-11-11), for headroom, configurable, and to be revisited after live runs: `reservation_usd` $5, `max_calls` 50, `max_turns` 50, `lens_wall_s` 1200 seconds (20 minutes per lens), `review_wall_s` 3600 seconds (60 minutes per review). Update (2026-11-12, D43): `reservation_usd` stays $5, and the one-token-per-byte input bound stays. The reservation prices the whole output cap at the completion rate plus the reasoning rate. The other four limits stay provisional. An override must be positive and finite, and a per-lens limit (`max_turns`, `lens_wall_s`) no greater than its per-review limit (`max_calls`, `review_wall_s`). A limit cleared back to unset is refused (`LimitsUnsetError`, naming each unset field) before any model, runner or ledger work. Retries (1) and pool size (1, the only accepted value) stay the working defaults. **Not built in this decision:** triage and more than one lens (phase 4), and the baked toolchain (phase 5). The live tool-calling adapter and the Sprite keepalive, which this decision left unbuilt, are built offline in D41 (2026-11-12); the 2026-11-12 live probe is recorded in D41. The one-shot OpenRouter adapter still sends one prompt and gets text back. The one-shot path's behaviour is unchanged: `prompt._fence` is now public as `fence`, and `bridge_args` gained `arg_names` and `required_args` so the model's tool schemas are built from the bridge's own table. **Open for the owner:** whether a quote failure should get a corrective turn in the same attempt instead of a fresh attempt (cheaper; a fresh attempt is the conservative reading of "retry with the body forced"); whether natural-key reuse is right (a rerun on the same head and body reuses the settled result, including a failed one, until the body changes); and the live first-turn context (the PR conversation and CI blocks from D38) for the first live run. **Update (2026-11-12):** D43's $5 reservation and the one-token-per-byte bound stay. The agentic per-call input cap is the pinned model's catalog `context_length` minus the output reserve, not a fixed 200,000, and the output cap is the model's `max_completion_tokens`. An override can tighten either and cannot exceed the model. The reasoning value sent on the request defaults to half the output cap, so the reply has the other half; an operator's own hint is sent as given when it is at least 1 and less than the output cap. The reservation does not depend on it. A stop from any of these limits, or from the input cap or the model's context, is partial and not retried. It names the limit, the configured value, the observed or needed value, and the flag or `ATLAS_*` variable that changes it. `agentic_config.load_agentic_limits` is the reader. The one-shot ceilings stay D25's. A lens that needs more than one context window is Q26, not built here. **Update (2026-11-12, review of #637):** a turn the model ends with `finish_reason=length` is an output-cap stop (`max_output_tokens`, configured cap, observed `finish_reason=length`), not a model error. The one-shot `review` command does not accept the eight agentic limit flags. `limits_for_lens` still reads the `ATLAS_*` variables. Update (2026-11-12, D44): the agentic CLI and the multi-lens coordinator are D44. This entry's statements that nothing is wired to the CLI, and that pool size 1 is the only accepted value, describe the state when D40 was written. Update (2026-11-14, D54): a finding whose only problem is a severity string outside the four names and D54's aliases is dropped and named instead, and blocks approval; every other contract problem still refuses the whole reply. Update (2026-11-14, D55): the coordinator verdict in this entry stays as written: a verified introduced Blocker is `REQUEST_CHANGES`, and any other verified introduced defect above nit, including a Major, is `COMMENT`. See D55. Update (2026-11-14, D47): D47 amends this entry's **Quote checks** rule. A one-line or several-line quote of whole lines of at least 8 non-space characters may also match within 2 lines of the citation, and then the finding's location becomes the matched lines; whitespace is collapsed on the head and removed-line sides too; and a quote may come from an unchanged file the lens read at HEAD. The cited-lines rule above is the rule for every other quote.
**Update (2026-11-17, D76 — supersession for coverage).** The "every selected lens settled clean" clause is now per lens with supersession (#692): a lens that ran more than once in one review -- a continuation after a refused or failed run -- blocks coverage only when none of its own entries is clean; a later clean run supersedes an earlier NOT RUN, a later NOT RUN does not undo an earlier clean run, and the superseded failure stays in the draft marked recovered. The per-file rule above is unchanged.
- **D41 — Agentic live adapter and Sprite keepalive (the 2026-11-11 design; probed 2026-11-12).** *(agent, 2026-11-12; Brandon approved the design on 2026-11-11)* The 2026-11-12 probe, about 1:10–1:23 AM CST, ran against main at `ece6e34` and PR #623 at `7f955e4` with lens `hunting-silent-failures`, model `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5-20260928`, and caps of $0.90, 8 calls, 8 turns, 600 seconds per lens and 900 seconds per review (`CallLimits` max output 16000, reasoning 8000). Every call was served by Amazon Bedrock. The tests in this repo stay recorded HTTP and fakes, and nothing from the probe is on the CLI. The adapter calls `openrouter.py`'s private helpers. `_actual_cost` prices cache read and write tokens when `usage.cost` is missing; a present `usage.cost` is still settled as reported. **Adapter** (`tool_model.OpenRouterLensModel`, the phase-3 `LensModel` port): one Chat Completions request per `turn`, on the controller. The provider document matches the one-shot path: `only` the pinned providers, `allow_fallbacks` false, `require_parameters` true, `data_collection` deny, `zdr` true, and `max_price` from the catalog. The request also sends a top-level `cache_control` of `{"type": "ephemeral"}`. On the probe, still on Amazon Bedrock under the same pin and zero data retention, the cache probe's own pair, a content-part breakpoint on one prompt of about 7k tokens, wrote the cache at $0.017767 and then read it at $0.000780. Measured separately, the review's first turn had no breakpoint and cost $0.018676, and the same probe's top-level breakpoint read a warm cache at $0.000757. The adapter sends the top-level field. No plugins, no server tools, and no `tool_choice`. The tools are `lens_loop.tool_schemas()`, and only those. Cost is `_actual_cost` (so a BYOK upstream charge is included). A turn with no readable cost but usable token counts is priced from the catalog. Prompt tokens use the highest of the prompt and cache-write rates. When the response reports cache read or write counts, those counts use the catalog's cache rates: a missing read rate uses the prompt rate, a missing write rate uses the reservation's input rate, and counts that sum past the prompt tokens leave the turn unpriced. A model with a reasoning rate and a response that reports no reasoning-token count also stays unpriced. That missing-cost case is `Decimal("Infinity")`, and `Spend.settle` then keeps the worst case the loop reserved before the call, as are an unreadable upstream charge, a BYOK call that reports no upstream charge, and a turn with neither a readable cost nor usable token counts. A readable cost is used as reported, including when the reasoning-token count is missing and when the response also reports cache tokens, because a cache discount is already inside `usage.cost`. The probe's review settled `committed_usd` $0.047220, equal to `usage.cost` and to `/generation` `total_cost`; `is_byok` was false and the upstream charge was not added. The worst case reserved on those turns was $0.264380 and $0.293560. The probe spent $0.067310 in all ($0.047220 for the review and $0.020090 for the cache calls). `require_parameters` with zero data retention did route. `max_price` of $2 per million prompt tokens and $10 per million completion tokens prices out `google-vertex/us` and `google-vertex/europe` ($2.2 and $11); only `amazon-bedrock` and `google-vertex/global` can serve, the same pin as the one-shot path. The review's first turn called `diff_file` on `openrouter.py` and the second attested zero findings. That result was `APPROVE` after one file of five; D42 is the gate that closes it. Without a task hold the Sprite paused within a few seconds (one 100.7-second gap), not about 30 seconds. Under the lens hold, 129 ticks landed in 130 seconds, no gap was over 2.5 seconds, two refreshes succeeded and the task was deleted. A Tasks PUT re-stamps `started_at` and `expires_at`. An external `GET /v1/sprites` status flipped among cold, warm and running and reported cold while the ticker was running, so that status is not a liveness signal. Zero settles at zero. The adapter does not call `ReviewBudget.reserve` (that budget's ceiling is the one-shot 3 calls and $3) and does not retry: a retry would be a second billed request under one reservation, and the loop's turn, call and dollar caps would not see it. A key that does not count BYOK spend refuses a response that is not explicitly `is_byok: false`, and an unparseable response on that key is refused the same way, because `is_byok` cannot be checked. That refusal leaves the loop, and the ledger finishes the attempt as a model error, which is not retried, keeping the turns and tool calls completed before it and the committed secrets those calls found. The runner fails, so its note is quoted, and the next review of the same head does not make another call. The loop keeps the reservation because it does not settle. The catalog is fetched once per turn (D51 amends this: once per provider for the review, and reused). Tool-call arguments must be one JSON object string with no duplicate key; a list, invalid JSON, a pre-parsed object, a duplicate key, a non-finite number, nesting deep enough to recurse, or a call that is not a function comes back as arguments `validate` rejects. The loop sends that refusal, nonce-fenced, and does not execute the call. A `finish_reason` of `length` raises before any call is returned. Tool-result text is forwarded unchanged, fence included. **Keepalive** (`keepalive.py`): `coordinator.review_lens` holds the Sprite for the length of one lens's attempts. The controller execs the hold inside the Sprite, against `/.sprite/api.sock` with Host `sprite`: `POST /v1/tasks` named `atlas-lens-<digest>`, `expire` `5m`, refreshed every 60 seconds, and `DELETE` on the way out, including when the lens raises or is cancelled. The refresh thread is joined before the delete, and the join waits at most 20 seconds. A refresh stores a failure under the lock before releasing it, so the delete cannot pass until that error is stored. A refresh once the hold has stopped does not run. The Tasks API's PUT is an upsert, and the lock and the stop and held guards are what stop a stopped hold from being recreated. Each hold exec has its own 15-second budget and the guest socket call times out after 10 seconds, reading only the status line. Refresh and tool execs share the runner's exec lock so they do not write one stream, and the 15-second budget is how long a refresh can block a tool. A delete error does not replace the lens's own exception. A failed create does not start the lens and does not delete. Tests use recorded HTTP, `LocalSprites`, and a fake socket. **Snapshot and restore is not built.** Checkpoint create streams NDJSON, restore is in-place and destructive, and there is no public fork API (Fly support, 2026-11-06), so it is not a small change. The design, for when it is built, is a secret-free golden Sprite, serial restore (research option B) behind a flag that defaults off, and no fork until a public API exists. **Still unverified after that probe:** the `max_run_after_disconnect` default; the deny-all network-policy JSON (the probe used `run_session`'s deny-all and did not check the JSON); checkpoint create and restore latency; whether a pinned provider other than Amazon Bedrock accepts tools under `require_parameters`. Zero data retention does not cover OpenRouter tool backends, so server tools stay off. D40's open questions (a corrective turn on a failed quote, natural-key reuse, and the live first-turn context) are unchanged. **Update (2026-11-12):** The adapter no longer takes its per-call token bounds from the one-shot `CallLimits`. `resolve_per_call` fills them from the catalog after the model fetch. The probe's 16,000 output and 8,000 reasoning figures above are that run's `CallLimits`, not the default now. A token refusal is `INPUT_CAP`, `OUTPUT_CAP`, `REASONING_CAP`, or `CONTEXT`, not `MODEL_ERROR`. **Update (2026-11-12, D51):** the adapter sends `cache_control` and `reasoning` only when every selected ZDR endpoint lists that name in `supported_parameters`. The provider document is otherwise the one-shot pin. **Update (2026-11-14, D57):** Anthropic-family routes send `cache_control` even when no selected endpoint lists it. Other models keep that intersection. `reasoning` still follows it.

- **D42 — A partial file read does not approve, and the loop does not nudge (2026-11-12).** *(agent, 2026-11-12; Brandon approved the gate)* Cross-ref D36 and D40. An agentic lens is clean only when every changed file in its scope has a `diff_file` chain that starts at offset 0 and follows each truncated page's `next_offset` until a page that was not cut. A `read_file` at HEAD does not substitute, and a call whose offset is not the next needed page does not fill the gap. A `next_offset` that does not advance does not complete the file. When any changed file is unread, the attempt's cause is `FILES_UNREAD`, the state is partial, and that cause is not retried. The final reply is the attempt's answer. The draft names the unread paths, highest risk first and quoted, `APPROVE` becomes `COMMENT`, and the status line carries `changed_files` and `unreviewed_files` as counts, not paths. A PR whose diff is larger than the bridge's session output budget (`SessionLimits.max_output_bytes`, 1 MiB by default) cannot come clean on this path: the call past the budget is refused `OVER_SESSION_LIMIT`, its file stays unread, and the review fails closed. The loop does not send those paths back for another turn. D36 does not send the one-shot model back either, and another turn would bill another reservation (on the probe, each call's reserved worst case was $0.264380 and $0.293560, about 10 to 14 times that call's settled cost of $0.018676 and $0.028544). The model already receives the changed-file list on the first turn, and the system prompt requires every page. A ledger line written before this decision has no changed-file count, and it does not show which files were read, so the coordinator treats it as unknown coverage: the review cannot approve on it. Settled attempts are reused for the same head and body, so that line keeps the review at `COMMENT` until the body or the head changes. Nothing re-runs it to clear the flag. The attempt's own state stays clean, and only the coverage check blocks approval. The attempt's cause is `FILES_UNREAD` and not retried when a final reply skipped a changed file, even if its reply also failed to parse. **Update (2026-11-12):** A limit stop is partial and not retried in the same way. The draft's detail and the ledger name the limit, its configured value, the observed value, and where to change it. The status line carries `limit_stop` (that object, or null). The file-read gate is unchanged. Update (2026-11-12, D44): an attempt is still not retried for `FILES_UNREAD`, and the loop still does not send the unread list back for another turn of that attempt. `review_lenses` may assign a lens's own unread files to a continuation of that lens, under its own ledger key, while a model call and the review clock remain. It stops when the set of (lens, file) gaps does not shrink. The gate stays per lens, as approved here: a file is covered for a lens only when that lens's own attempts read it, and a read by another lens does not cover it. A file some lens left unread, or a lens that failed for a cause other than `FILES_UNREAD` alone, still blocks approval. **Update (2026-11-14, D58):** two rules here changed. The loop now sends the unread list back once, fenced, before accepting a final reply, so a triaging model can finish its coverage in its own conversation; a nudged reply that still leaves files unread ends the attempt partial exactly as this decision wrote, and D44's continuation then hands those files back as a scoped run. And an unread file no longer cancels an `UNPARSED` retry: the parse refusal is what threw the reply away. Every other retryable cause is still vetoed by an unread file, and the heading's "the loop does not nudge" is superseded by this update.
**Update (2026-11-17, D76 — supersession for coverage).** The per-lens, per-file gate above is unchanged; what D76 adds is the state side: within one lens's own entries, a later clean run supersedes an earlier NOT RUN (and a later NOT RUN does not undo an earlier clean run), so the lens's coverage rides on whether any of its runs settled clean, while the superseded failure stays in the draft marked recovered. A read by another lens still covers nothing.
**Update (2026-11-17, D77):** the bridge session output budget this entry cites is per lens attempt (D77 starts it over per attempt). The structural rule stands unchanged: one attempt must read the whole diff within its own budget, so a diff over 1 MiB still cannot come clean.

- **D43 — The per-review reservation stays $5, and the byte-bound worst-case check stays (2026-11-12).** *(user, 2026-11-12, 11:54 AM CT)* Cross-ref D41 and D42. The figures come from two live agentic reviews on 2026-11-12 (lens `hunting-silent-failures`), which are not the D41 probe (PR #623 at `7f955e4`, $0.90 of caps): PR #621 at `35c655c` (base `27084e9`, $0.45 reservation, 24 calls, 24 turns; `CallLimits` output 4,000, reasoning 1,500) and PR #631 at `5a691ff` (base `ece6e34`, $1.25 reservation). Their transcripts are not in this repo, so the figures rest on the owner's reports of them. With prompt caching warm, the per-turn worst-case reservation ran about 5–44× the settled cost (#621 5–34×, #631 5–44×). `budget.input_token_bound` counts one token per UTF-8 byte plus 64, and `worst_case_usd` prices that bound at `max(prompt, cache_write)` plus the full output cap and the reasoning budget. On #621, turn 9 was refused because $0.113 committed plus a $0.367 worst case passed the $0.45 reservation. The #631 read that #621 projected at about $0.8–1.0 reserved for about $0.2 was then run: 14 of 14 files, APPROVE, $0.2603556 settled, $0.929 peak held of $1.25. **Decision:** keep that strict check unchanged and give the review a bigger per-review reservation, not a cached-prefix estimate and not lower per-call output or reasoning ceilings. **Why:** the safety guarantee stays provable, with no estimate that could undercount a turn the cache misses; and lower per-call output or reasoning ceilings are not a hard bound (#631's reasoning reached 2,270 tokens against a 1,500 request limit). **Cost:** reservations sit at about 5–44× actual, so that headroom must be provisioned. The key's hard-limit preflight (at most $5, credit remaining at the start) and OpenRouter's own limit bound what a review can spend; only the one-shot path also checks each call against the key's remaining credit. A request on the one-shot path is capped at 200,000 prompt-bound tokens (`CallLimits.max_input_tokens`), where one call's worst case is about $0.84. On the #631 slope that 200,000 cap, which then ended the review as `MODEL_ERROR` and incomplete (it cannot approve), was reached at about a 135 KB diff read in full, well before $5 binds. That 200,000 figure is the one-shot cap. The agentic use of it is superseded by the Update below. `DEFAULT_RESERVATION_USD` in `atlas_reviewer/lens_loop.py` is already $5 and stays the per-review reservation. Preflight still requires a hard limit of at most $5 with credit remaining, and the one-shot path refuses a call whose worst case would take committed dollars over that credit (`ReviewBudget.reserve`, `ceiling_usd`). The agentic loop's per-call check is `Spend.reserve` against the $5 reservation. **Revisit when** a live review is stopped by the input cap (`INPUT_CAP` or `CONTEXT`) or ends on the reservation (`Cause.BUDGET`) while its settled spend is well under $5, or when a pool size above 1 is considered, since the plan says the pool serialises below about $5. No code change in this decision. **Update (2026-11-12):** The $5 reservation and the one-token-per-byte bound stay. `worst_case_usd` now prices the whole output cap at the completion rate plus the reasoning rate, so reported reasoning above the hint still fits the reservation. The agentic per-call input cap is the model's context minus the output reserve; hitting it is `INPUT_CAP` or `CONTEXT`, not `MODEL_ERROR`. The one-shot path keeps the 200,000 input cap. **Update (2026-11-13, D44):** the pool-size revisit trigger fired. `agentic-review` runs up to 16 lenses at once, 4 by default. The $5 reservation stays one per review: every pooled lens reserves through the same locked `Spend`, so a pool does not raise what a review can commit. The key preflight's ceiling is no longer fixed at $5. It defaults to $5 and is raised with `--key-ceiling-usd` or `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD`, which `review` and `agentic-review` both read. A value that is not finite and positive is refused. **Update (2026-11-14, D58):** the byte bound is superseded for a growing agentic conversation by `budget.InputTokenMeter`: after a response, the bound is that response's reported `prompt_tokens` plus the messages added since at one token per byte (the provable end of the same assumption), not the whole payload at one token per byte. The enforced input cap value is unchanged; this is a measurement change, and the basis every bound came from is recorded. A first request, a restarted conversation, and an unreadable, zero or implausibly low reported count still take the byte bound.
- **D48 — A webhook receiver queues reviews for this repository and publishes nothing (amends D24 and D37; does not close Q24).** *(user, 2026-11-12)* GitHub can deliver `pull_request` `opened`, `synchronize`, and `ready_for_review` to `python -m atlas_reviewer.service`. The receiver checks the HMAC, skips drafts, keeps an allowlist that defaults to `brandondees/code-quality-atlas`, and dedupes by repository, PR, and head SHA. A later `updated_at` supersedes an in-flight review. An older redelivery does not. The process publishes nothing to GitHub. A write path is a separate decision. **Queue.** At most 2,000 active jobs (`queued`, `running`, `post_pending`, `posting`) for pull requests that are not already in the queue. Past that, `enqueue` returns `full` and the HTTP handler answers 503. A newer head of a pull request that already has an active job takes that slot instead. Replacing a job that is still `running` or `posting` leaves it active until it settles, so the count can reach 2,000 plus those superseded jobs. That extra is at most `ATLAS_REVIEW_CONCURRENCY`, which itself is at most 32. Terminal jobs are trimmed to make room. A `FAILED` head is not a duplicate: GitHub does not redeliver a delivery that was already acknowledged, so the same SHA can be queued again. `MAX_SEEN` (20,000) is a soft cap. It drops the oldest key that is not a pull request's current head, and it keeps every current head, so the set can grow by one key per pull request. A key is about 80 bytes. At 30,000 keys one enqueue holds the queue lock for about 338 ms while it rewrites the file, which is why the cap exists. A run directory is removed when its job fails or is superseded. A finished job with no publisher keeps `draft.md` and drops the child's `stderr.txt`. **Child.** The review child gets an allowlist: the OpenRouter key, the Sprites token, a read-only installation token, and the process basics (`PATH`, `HOME`, language and CA paths). The webhook secret, the App private key, and any other parent variable, including `FLY_API_TOKEN`, stay in the parent. The parent stops the child at 1,200 seconds. The child holds an installation token the parent already minted and cannot refresh; that token lasts an hour, and 1,200 seconds is inside the hour. An equal `updated_at` with a different SHA is a newer head, not a stale one. **Bind.** The default listen address is `127.0.0.1`. `ATLAS_SERVICE_PORT` must be 8080. **Log.** Service events go to stderr and to `service.jsonl`, which rotates at 1 MB and keeps one previous file. The in-memory list keeps the newest 500 events. **Options.** Stay operator-only, which is still what the CLI does. Reuse the private webhook router. Post to GitHub from this process. None of those is this decision: this is a receiver in this repo, and it publishes nothing. **Cost.** No standing Machine and no GitHub write. The model spend stays inside the caps the review already has, and only for a review that runs. The always-on host, when it is deployed, is a later decision. **Reversibility.** Stop the process. Nothing here writes to GitHub, so there is no comment to withdraw. The state directory can be discarded. The operator CLI and the one-off Fly app are unchanged. **Revisit** when a second repository is allowlisted, or when the receiver is given a GitHub write path. Nothing here is deployed. **Update (2026-11-13, D46):** The webhook service now posts one COMMENT review. A posted COMMENT cannot be withdrawn. APPROVE and REQUEST_CHANGES are still refused. The revisit trigger for giving the receiver a GitHub write path has fired; the second-repository trigger has not. **Update (2026-11-18, D79):** under the head-change policy's default keep (#702, D79), the supersede in this entry is no longer the whole story: a newer head whose push is benign -- or that lands past the cancel threshold -- no longer supersedes the running review; it is held back as the pull request's pending follow-up and queued when the running review settles. `Board.enqueue` keeps exactly the supersede contract this entry records (it is the cancel path, and the whole behavior under `ATLAS_HEAD_CHANGE_POLICY=cancel`); the keep path is `Board.keep_head` beside it.
- **D46 — The webhook service posts one COMMENT review (amends D24, D48's no-publish rule, and the webhook service's provider default from D25; does not amend D37).** *(user, 2026-11-12)* **Choice.** The operator CLI from D24 still publishes nothing. D48 recorded the webhook receiver as publishing nothing and left the write path as a separate decision; this is that decision. This path posts one pull-request review whose event is COMMENT. `comment_payload` has no event argument, and `comment_only` raises for APPROVE and REQUEST_CHANGES. The body carries an advisory banner, the lenses run and not run, the files note, limit stops, and cost. Each untrusted field goes through `quote_untrusted`. **Options.** Leave the draft on disk for an operator to copy (D24). Post the draft's recommended event, including APPROVE. Post COMMENT only. COMMENT only is the choice, because the service is not yet a substitute for the full Claude Code protocol review. **Provider.** The webhook service defaults `ATLAS_SERVICE_PROVIDER` to `openrouter`, and it will not boot that way without `ATLAS_ALLOW_LIVE_INFERENCE=1`. `offline` is set explicitly. The operator CLI's default stays `offline`, which is what D25 records for that path. **What this does not change.** D25 stays the read path: the review child still uses a read-only installation token. D37 stays the one-off Fly app, which has no webhook and no queue. D53, on the deploy PR, is the always-on app. D45 is a separate in-flight decision and is not changed here. D48 is the receiver. This decision is the write path D48 left open. **Volume.** This decision does not create a volume and does not depend on merge order. The queue caps are D48's: 2,000 active jobs is a hard cap, and the 20,000 dedupe keys are a soft cap because current heads are kept. A key is about 80 bytes. The 1 GB volume exists only when D53's app is deployed. If this PR merges without that deploy, the state directory is `ATLAS_REVIEWER_STATE_DIR` and the caps still bound the queue file. **Marker.** `<!-- atlas-service-post: repo#pr#sha -->` counts only when `commit_id` is that SHA, `user.type` is Bot, and `user.login` is `{ATLAS_GITHUB_APP_SLUG}[bot]`. Another App's bot does not count. **Retries.** A failed review posts nothing. A failed post is tried at most five times. The waits before the later tries are 5, 15, 30, and 60 seconds, each stretched by up to 20%. The fifth attempt does not wait. A 429 waits. A 403 waits only when `Retry-After` is present, or `x-ratelimit-remaining` is 0 and `x-ratelimit-reset` is present, or the body says it is a secondary rate limit. A secondary limit waits at least 60 seconds. A wait above 15 minutes is cut to 15 minutes. A 401, a 403 that is not one of those cases, and a 422 are not retried. Each attempt's deadline is 45 seconds and includes the token mint. **Cost.** No standing Machine is created here. The model spend stays inside the caps D25 and the agentic loop already enforce. The extra cost is one GitHub write of a COMMENT. The always-on Machine's price is D53's, about $6.70 per 30 days plus the volume, and it is not spent by merging this PR. **Reversibility.** Stop the process or disable the webhook. A COMMENT already accepted by GitHub stays; that request cannot be recalled. The one-off CLI and D37's app are unchanged. There is no schema migration. **Revisit** after the first live webhook review, or if COMMENT-only is dropped. Tests use fakes. Nothing was posted to GitHub from this change.

- **D45 — Eval traces stay on self-hosted Phoenix in the Fly org `personal` (2026-11-12).** *(agent, 2026-11-12; recommendation, nothing provisioned)* Numbered D45 because D44 belongs to #646, which records the agentic picker and repair decision. D48 is #639's webhook receiver, already on main, and D46 is the open #640's. The reviewer needs a trace store before its drafts are treated as an MVP, and the traces can carry pull-request text. **Decision:** run Arize Phoenix ourselves on Fly in the same org `personal` as the controller (D37). Do not send traces to Arize AX, and do not use hosted Phoenix Cloud, until a later decision. **Why this and not AX or Phoenix Cloud:** Phoenix's own license page says self-hosting on your own infrastructure is free and permitted under the Elastic License 2.0 ([license](https://github.com/Arize-ai/phoenix/blob/main/docs/phoenix/self-hosting/license.mdx)). A Fly Machine in `personal` keeps the spans in the same org as the review Machine. AX Free and AX Pro are Arize-hosted SaaS (US, EU, or CA), so the spans would leave the org; AX Enterprise is the only AX plan that lists self-hosting, and it is custom-priced ([pricing](https://arize.com/pricing/), read 2026-11-12). Hosted Phoenix Cloud still has a sign-in, and a community reply says legacy spaces stay until they are idle or the owner asks for deletion; the pricing page publishes no Phoenix Cloud span cap or retention, so those terms are not something this decision can rely on. **Cost, as published, not a quote:** Fly's lowest regions (`iad`, `ewr`) list a `shared-cpu-1x` Machine with 1 GB at $6.70 per 30 days and a volume at $0.15 per GB-month ([Fly resource pricing](https://docs.fly.io/about/pricing), read 2026-11-12). A 1 GB Machine plus a 10 GB volume is about $8.20 per 30 days before egress ($0.02/GB to the internet in North America and Europe). Prices vary by region. AX Free is $0 with 25,000 spans/month, 1 GB ingestion, and 15 days of retention. AX Pro is $50/month with 50,000 spans, 10 GB, and 30 days. **Retention:** Phoenix's default project policy is `PHOENIX_DEFAULT_RETENTION_POLICY_DAYS=0`, which the retention doc defines as indefinite ([data retention](https://arize.com/docs/phoenix/settings/data-retention)). Set it to 30 on the Fly app. The architecture doc's storage table says SQLite is the default and the fit for a single-user deployment, and PostgreSQL is the production multi-user backend ([architecture](https://arize.com/docs/phoenix/self-hosting/architecture)). One operator starts on SQLite with `PHOENIX_WORKING_DIR` on the volume. **Auth:** Phoenix 21 turns authentication on when the process listens on a non-loopback address, and the Docker image sets `PHOENIX_HOST=::`, so the image requires an API key unless `PHOENIX_ENABLE_AUTH=false` ([release note](https://arize.com/docs/phoenix/release-notes/11-2026/11-02-2026-authentication-by-default-and-decision-annotations), 2026-10-30). Leave auth on. The API key is a Fly secret on the Phoenix app, and the controller's OTLP header is `ATLAS_OTEL_HEADERS` on the controller only. Neither value goes in the repo, a Sprite, a test, or a doc. **Redaction:** the controller scrubs span attributes before export, and prompt and completion bodies stay off unless `ATLAS_OTEL_EXPORT_BODIES=1`. Scrubbing is pattern-based. A secret in a shape the redactor does not name can still be exported, which is why the spans stay in `personal` and why bodies stay off. Phoenix itself phones home unless told not to: `PHOENIX_ALLOW_EXTERNAL_RESOURCES` defaults to true (fonts, Phoenix telemetry, the WebAssembly sandbox) and model-cost refresh fetches GitHub unless `PHOENIX_DISABLE_MODEL_COST_AUTO_UPDATE=true` ([configuration](https://arize.com/docs/phoenix/self-hosting/configuration)). Set both so the Phoenix app does not open those connections. **Not done:** no Fly app, no volume, no secret, no Phoenix process. The parity gate for submitting `APPROVE` is [`plans/2026-11-12-reviewer-parity-gate.md`](plans/2026-11-12-reviewer-parity-gate.md). **Addendum (2026-11-12, Brandon, 3:14 PM CT):** an extra finding never fails that gate, and the verdict and the severity do not have to match. It fails when a selected lens was NOT RUN or failed, a lens named by a reference finding was not run, file coverage is incomplete, or recall of the reference findings (same path, line within five lines) is below 1. Revisit when the span volume outgrows one SQLite file, or when someone asks to put spans on AX. **Update (2026-11-14):** #640 has since merged, so D46 is on main.

- **D44 — Agentic review runs the picker's ranking, one repair turn, and a continuation for the files a lens left unread (2026-11-12).** *(agent, 2026-11-12)* Offline-tested. No live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made for this decision. The one-shot `review` command still uses `select_lenses` and the 60,000-character lens budget, so a review-depth one-shot run still skips the picker and still runs the two critical lenses that fit. **Selection:** `select_agentic_lenses` does not consult that budget. At review depth it calls the picker and plans the critical tier plus every ranked lens that has a body. A lens with no body is still NOT RUN. A picked lens that did not run blocks approval: D34 counts it as not run, and D42 lets only complete coverage approve, so `coverage_complete` is false and the review event is not APPROVE. **Several lenses:** `review_lenses` runs the planned lenses, up to `pool_size` at once. `pool_size` is an integer from 1 to 16. The dataclass default stays 1, so `review_lens` is unchanged. `agentic-review` uses 4 when neither `--pool-size` nor `ATLAS_POOL_SIZE` is set. Each lens gets `lens_runner_name`, which adds a digest of the lens to `event_runner_name`, and its own bridge directory. `Spend.reserve` and `settle`, and the attestation ledger's append, take a lock, because calls and dollars are review-wide. The runner ledger's `record` and `clear` share a lock too: `clear` rewrites the file from what it read, so a runner recorded by another lens between that read and the replace would drop out of the crash-recovery record. A Sprites create that returns HTTP 429 is a typed `RateLimitedError`, waited out (1s, then 2s, then 4s, each scaled by a random 0.5 to 1.0 so pooled lenses do not retry in step) and tried again, but only while the runner deadline has time left; after three waits it is final. The #636 live run refused two of ten creates before a Sprite existed, and a later create of the same name succeeded. Any other refusal is final and still deletes nothing. A lens still queued when the review deadline passes is not started, and each runner's deadline is the review time left, not a fresh `review_wall_s`. The lenses' results are one `synthesize`. **CLI:** `agentic-review` is the command that applies the agentic flags. The one-shot `review` command does not accept those flags and does not read the eight agentic `ATLAS_*` limit variables; it does read `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD`. D40's "nothing is wired to the CLI" is the state when D40 was written. A live run builds the picker's OpenRouter provider from the agentic limits, at most `max_calls` calls (and the one-shot ceiling of 3) and at most `reservation_usd` (and the one-shot ceiling of $3), not from the one-shot `--max-model-calls`, which `agentic-review` does not accept. The picker's calls and committed dollars start the review's `Spend`, so they count toward `max_calls` and `reservation_usd` and appear in the draft's totals. The PR title the picker sees is redacted. The PR head is checked against `event.head_sha` before and after the diff fetch, as in the one-shot controller; a mismatch ends the run with no model call and no draft. **A reply outside the contract:** inside the attempt, the loop sends the rejected reply back as the model's own turn, then one user turn that quotes the validation error and asks for one JSON object. A second failure is the attempt's result (`UNPARSED`). That is not a new attempt, and `FILES_UNREAD` is still not retried. **Coverage:** after the parallel lenses, each lens's own unread files are assigned back to that same lens as a continuation, for up to `MAX_CONTINUATIONS` (4) rounds, while a call and the review clock remain. A lens with no body is not continued. The continuation has its own ledger key, so a settled `FILES_UNREAD` attempt is not reused. A round that does not shrink the set of (lens, file) gaps ends the loop early. A file is covered for a lens only when that lens's own attempts read it; a read by a different lens does not cover it. This is the per-lens gate D42 approved. Every lens is held to every changed path, not only the scope it was assigned, so a lens given too narrow a scope cannot approve. A coverage stop condition, the gap name in the headline and the unread list come from one rule. **Runners:** the draft's Runner line shows a runner that did not complete, else one whose delete was not verified. A delete that was not verified does not block approval, as with one lens, but the draft names every such runner for `sweep --runner NAME --force-live`. A lens runner's name adds 12 hex digits of the lens and its index to `event_runner_name`, so one lens's cleanup cannot match a sibling's Sprite. **Selection in the draft:** a `## Lens selection` section carries the picker's note, including why it fell back to the critical tier, and each picked name that did not run with its reason. The one-shot and agentic selections share one fallback path, so they fall back for the same reasons. **Update (2026-11-12, protocol review of #646):** the earlier rule let any lens's read cover a file for every lens, and that loosened D42. It is withdrawn. A lens that failed for a cause other than `FILES_UNREAD` alone still blocks approval, and so does a gap that no continuation closed. **Bodies:** `LensLibrary` on the default change-review bundle reads a body that is not in that bundle from another collapsed entrypoint. An empty or blank `body.md` is no body, so that lens is NOT RUN. `auditing-documentation-health` stays `shape: repo` and is generated under the audit entrypoint. `names()` still lists only the change-review bundle, so the one-shot "smallest other lens" check is unchanged. A library pointed at another root does not search siblings. **BYOK and fatal errors:** a `ByokRefusedError` still ends its lens as a failed runner report, as before, and it now also halts `Spend`: no lens that has not started is started, the other lenses' next model call is not sent, and a lens that was not started reports why. The halt is checked again after a call is priced, because pricing can wait on the catalog. A fatal error in any lens halts the review the same way. **Key ceiling:** a value that is not a finite positive number is refused, so `Infinity` and `NaN` are no longer accepted. `preflight` still refuses a key whose own limit is above the ceiling. The ceiling defaults to $5. `--key-ceiling-usd` and `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD` raise it, and both `review` and `agentic-review` read them, because a key above the ceiling fails either command's preflight. The refusal names the observed limit, the configured ceiling, and those two settings. **Not in this decision:** lenses running tests or mutants, reading CI, or seeing commits, and any change to severity calibration. Revisit when a live `agentic-review` is stopped by the pool, the continuation, or the key ceiling. **Update (2026-11-14, round 4):** a review appends one round record (D52), after the final synthesize, from the verdict over every lens and continuation. The record's lens field names each lens that ran. Before this, each pooled lens and each continuation appended its own one-lens record, so one review could store APPROVE and COMMENT side by side. With one write per review, the in-process lock that serialized those writes is gone. The coordinator no longer sets `atlas.review_event` on a span the caller opened; the caller sets it.
**Update (2026-11-17, D76 — supersession for coverage).** "A picked lens that did not run blocks approval" and "a lens that failed for another cause still blocks" now carry the supersession rule: they block unless a run of the same lens in the same review came back clean, in which case the earlier NOT RUN or failure is superseded for coverage and stays in the draft marked recovered (#692). An unsuperseded failure blocks exactly as stated here.

- **D51 — The live model and its provider pin are configurable, and a request stays on pure zero data retention (2026-11-12).** *(agent, 2026-11-12; Brandon, the same day: cheaper models for testing, ZDR non-negotiable, one-provider pins for implicit-cache hosts)* Amends D25 and D41. Offline only: the tests use recorded HTTP, and this change makes no live model call and reads no key. **Choice.** `ATLAS_MODEL` or `--model` selects the OpenRouter model. Unset stays `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`. A response is accepted only from that id or one dated revision (`<id>-YYYYMMDD`). `ATLAS_PROVIDERS` or `--providers` replaces `provider.only`. Unset stays `amazon-bedrock`, `google-vertex/europe`, `google-vertex/global`, and `google-vertex/us`. One slug is a valid pin, so a trial can stay on one implicit-cache host (DeepInfra, Fireworks, and Moonshot, and the other exact tags the ZDR list names (a bare `deepinfra` is not a tag; variants such as `deepinfra/fp8` are)). `allow_fallbacks` stays false, `require_parameters` stays true, `zdr` stays true, `data_collection` stays `deny`, and `max_price` stays the catalog's highest prompt and completion prices. None of those five is a setting. Before a completion the adapter reads `GET /api/v1/endpoints/zdr` with no key. A slug is eligible only when every row for this model and slug has `safety_retention: false`. A missing row, a row whose flag is not a boolean, or any Safety Retention row refuses the slug and no completion is sent. `only` names a slug, so it cannot keep one endpoint of that slug and drop another, and OpenRouter's ZDR guide (fetched 2026-11-12, https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/features/zdr) says `zdr: true` still follows the account's Safety Retention choice. `ATLAS_ALLOW_SAFETY_RETENTION=true` or `--allow-safety-retention` opts in. The default is off. `reasoning` is sent, with the configured token hint and `exclude: true`, only when every eligible row lists `reasoning`. `cache_control` is sent only when every eligible row lists `cache_control`. The output cap stays the configured limit: the agentic path's `ATLAS_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (or the model's completion limit when that is unset) and the one-shot path's `CallLimits` cap. **Options.** Leaving the model and the four slugs hardcoded keeps today's pin and blocks the trial models. Relaxing `require_parameters` would let the request carry `cache_control` and `reasoning.max_tokens` onto endpoints that do not advertise them, which is the refusal this payload rule avoids. Treating Safety Retention as pure ZDR would send, for example, Gemini on Vertex to endpoints that may retain flagged prompts. **Cost.** Each review adds one unauthenticated GET of the ZDR list per provider. A one-slug pin does not fail over to another host; the default pin stays four slugs, so an unset configuration does not take that outage. Sonnet stays the default until a cheaper model passes the parity gate on #643: an extra finding does not fail that gate, and it fails when a selected lens was NOT RUN or failed, a lens named by a reference finding was not run, file coverage is incomplete, or recall of the reference findings is below 1 (that PR's D45 addendum). **Reversibility.** Unset the three variables and drop the flags. The defaults are the previous model and pin, and Safety Retention stays off. Nothing is migrated. A parent slug is refused when any child tag (`<slug>/...`) for that model is Safety Retention or has no boolean `safety_retention`, because `provider.only` of the parent can expand to that child. A slug with no exact row is refused and the error names child tags when the list has them. `max_price` stays the model's highest catalog prompt and completion rates, including overrides, which can be above the eligible rows and so cannot admit a row the reservation did not price. A required prompt or completion rate of zero is refused, and a per-million price that is not finite is refused. The catalog and the ZDR list are read once per provider and reused for later completions in that review; `configure` drops that snapshot. **Revisit when** a cheaper model passes that parity gate and should become the default, or when `GET /api/v1/endpoints/zdr` stops exposing a boolean `safety_retention` on each row. **Update (2026-11-13, D57):** `max_price`, the reservation rates, the context length and the completion limit now come from the selected endpoint rows, not the catalog headline. `zdr: true` stays the default and can be turned off. Anthropic-family routes always send `cache_control`, because no endpoint lists it and a ZDR Bedrock Sonnet endpoint still honors it. See D57. **Update (2026-11-14, D57):** those endpoint rates include each row's `pricing.overrides`, the same maximum the catalog tiers used. A base-only read leaves `max_price`, the reservation and the fallback settlement short of a long-context tier.
- **D54 — A finding whose severity is unknown is dropped, and the review cannot approve (2026-11-12).** Main's `parse_reply` refused a lens's whole final reply when one finding's severity was not exactly `blocker`, `major`, `minor` or `nit`; case and surrounding whitespace already matched. **Decision:** severity is matched after case, surrounding whitespace, one pair of surrounding quotes, and `.,:;!` at either end, and then a closed alias set: `critical`, `crit` and `blocking` are Blocker, `high` is Major, `medium` and `moderate` are Minor, and `low` and `trivial` are Nit. A finding that meets every other rule of the contract but whose severity string still matches none of those is dropped from the reply. The other findings stand, and no repair turn is sent for it. Every other per-finding problem (a key outside the contract or a missing one, a non-string value, a bad location, valence, route or attribution, empty text, evidence over 40 lines, or the improvement and pre-existing rules) still refuses the whole reply, which is sent back once and is then `UNPARSED`, as D44 says. A finding with an unknown severity and any other problem is refused, not dropped. The drop line names the raw severity, whitespace collapsed and cut to 80 characters. The draft lists those lines under **Dropped findings**, each scrubbed and quoted as inert text, and the lens line counts them. A dropped finding blocks approval: the coverage rule reads "a finding was dropped", so the review event is at most COMMENT, because the dropped finding could have been a Blocker. The lens system prompt lists the allowed values next to the severity, valence, attribution and route keys, says a finding whose severity cannot be read as one of the four names is dropped and the review cannot approve, and says any other value outside the contract sends the whole reply back. The one-shot `review` command parses no per-lens reply, so this is the agentic loop only. Offline-tested. No live model call was made for this decision.

- **D52 — Each agentic round reviews the whole pull request, and prior findings are input, not scope (2026-11-12).** *(agent, 2026-11-12)* Amends D40 and D42. Offline only: no live model, Sprite or GitHub call. **Choice.** `review_lens` puts two fenced blocks ahead of the current changed-file list. `conversation.review_context` is the PR description, comments and review threads the one-shot path already assembles, with every omission that assembly recorded. `rounds.RoundLog` is an append-only JSONL file, mode 0600 on every write, keyed by repository and pull request number. The repository key is compared case-insensitively. Records for another pull request in the same file are counted and are not omissions. A file that cannot be read is its own omission, "the round log file could not be read", not an unparsed line. A superseded record that does not fit is skipped so a current-head record behind it can still be shown. A record stores the head SHA, the review event and the verified finding lines. A new head still runs the lens, because the attestation ledger stays keyed by head SHA. The older record is labelled as a superseded head and is not the scope of the review. The changed-file list is every file in the current diff. Prior findings do not remove one. A continuation may later list a subset of files. The first turn does not. A round record over 8,192 characters stores no finding text. Prior blocks are kept whole, newest first, inside 16,384 characters. A record at the head under review that does not fit, and a line for this pull request that does not parse, is an `Omission`. A superseded head is not this review's scope, so leaving it out does not block approval. A line that names another pull request, or that names no pull request, does not block this one. A future record version that still names a pull request blocks only that pull request. `coverage_complete` is false while `context_omissions` is non-empty, so the review cannot approve, the same rule as an unread file (D42). The status line carries the omission count and how many other pull requests were in the file. A failure to append the new record returns the review that was already paid for. The draft quotes the full error. The status line names the error class and its strerror, not the path. It is not an omission. **Options.** Reviewing only the commits since the last round would let a bad change land as several harmless diffs. Keeping prior findings only in the ledger, which is keyed by head SHA, would hide them on the next push. Silently truncating the conversation or a prior round would let the model approve on a partial picture. **Cost.** The first turn carries the conversation block (D38's budget, about 64 KiB) plus up to 16 KiB of prior rounds, on top of the changed-file list. A partial context cannot approve, so a pull request whose conversation does not fit stays at `COMMENT` until the budget holds it or the omissions are empty. **Reversibility.** Pass no round log and no context omissions. The first turn is then the target and the changed-file list, as before. The log file can be deleted. Nothing is migrated. **Revisit when** #646's continuations land and a continuation lists a subset: the first turn stays every file in the current diff. Also when #639's webhook queue starts a review without passing `review_context` and the round log. The log path is chosen when that caller is wired. No shipped caller builds one yet. The plan is [`plans/2026-11-12-full-pr-context-rounds.md`](plans/2026-11-12-full-pr-context-rounds.md).
- **D53 — The webhook receiver is a second, always-on Fly app, and it posts COMMENT only (amends D24's "no webhooks" and D37's "no standing Machine" for this path; the one-off app is unchanged; does not close Q24).** *(agent, 2026-11-12, from Brandon's direction the same day: enable the reviewer on this repo early, COMMENT only, until it matches the Claude Code routine)* **Shape.** GitHub closes a webhook that has no 2xx within 10 seconds and does not redeliver a timeout on its own (https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/best-practices-for-using-webhooks and https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/handling-failed-webhook-deliveries, read 2026-11-12). Fly Proxy can stop an idle Machine and start it on the next request. Fly's configuration reference says `auto_stop_machines = "off"` means the proxy does not stop the Machine, and that auto-stop and auto-start should both be on or both off; it does not bound a cold start under 10 seconds (https://fly.io/docs/reference/configuration/, `http_service`, read 2026-11-12). The Fly app sets `ATLAS_SERVICE_HOST=0.0.0.0` because the proxy is not on loopback; the process default stays `127.0.0.1`. The listen port stays 8080. The receiver is therefore one standing Machine, not an auto-start Machine. It is a second app, `atlas-reviewer-service`, so [`fly.toml`](../fly.toml) stays without an HTTP service.
