Region `iad`, org `personal`, `shared-cpu-1x`, 1024 MB, the same memory as the one-off review Machine. **Cost.** Fly's pricing page prices `shared-cpu-1x` with 1 GB of RAM at $6.70 per 30 days of continuous runtime in `iad` and `ewr` (https://fly.io/docs/about/pricing, read 2026-11-12). That is the compute cost of leaving this Machine up. The 1 GB volume at `/data` is $0.15 per month on that same page. A stopped Machine is $0.15 per GB of root filesystem per month; this image's root filesystem was not measured, so that rate is not turned into a second total. **Behavior.** `pull_request` `opened`, `synchronize`, and `ready_for_review` only, drafts ignored, HMAC `X-Hub-Signature-256` with `ATLAS_GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, allowlist defaulting to `brandondees/code-quality-atlas`. Dedupe is repository + PR + head SHA. A newer SHA cancels the older review and that review is not posted. The review is the existing one-shot `review` command. Exit 0, 4, or 5 posts one pull-request review with event COMMENT. The body says the review is advisory and not a substitute for the full Claude Code protocol review, then lists lenses run and not run, the file-coverage note, limit stops, and cost. APPROVE and REQUEST_CHANGES cannot be sent: the payload builder has no event argument. A failed review posts nothing and records telemetry. The idempotency key is `repo#pr#sha`, stored after GitHub accepts the review and also matched against reviews already on the PR. The posting token may request `pull_requests: write` and no other write. The parent process holds the App private key and the webhook secret and mints the tokens. The review child does not receive the App private key, the App id, the installation id, or `FLY_API_TOKEN`. The parent passes it a read-only installation token instead. The child does receive `ATLAS_OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, because that process calls the model, and `ATLAS_SPRITES_TOKEN`, because that process creates and destroys the Sprite, plus `PATH`, `HOME`, the CA and locale variables it needs to start, and the non-secret settings `ATLAS_MODEL`, `ATLAS_PROVIDERS`, `ATLAS_ALLOW_SAFETY_RETENTION`, `ATLAS_REQUIRE_ZDR`, and `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD`. The child is the one-shot review, so the agentic limit variables are not passed and D25's ceilings stay. Neither the model key nor the Sprites token is sent into the Sprite. **State.** The queue, the post ledger, and `service.jsonl` live on a 1 GB Fly volume mounted at `/data`. The queue refuses a new job when 2,000 active jobs (`queued`, `running`, `post_pending`, `posting`) belong to other pull requests. A newer head of a pull request that already has an active job takes that slot. Replacing a job that is still `running` or `posting` can leave the count at 2,000 plus those superseded jobs, and that extra is at most the worker concurrency. The dedupe list keeps every current head, so it can grow past 20,000 by one key per pull request. A run directory is removed when its job ends. A key is about 80 bytes, so 20,000 keys are about 1.6 MB, which fits that volume. The Machine's root disk does not keep this state. **Not done here.** This repository does not create the app, set the secret, or enable the webhook. Runbook: [`atlas_reviewer/deploy-service.md`](../atlas_reviewer/deploy-service.md). Plan: [`plans/2026-11-12-webhook-service-mode.md`](plans/2026-11-12-webhook-service-mode.md). The entrypoint chowns the state directory to uid 10001 before the process drops privileges, so a fresh root-owned volume does not need a manual chown. **Reversibility.** Disable the repository webhook, stop the Machine and let the drain finish, sweep a leftover runner with `sweep --runner NAME --force-live`, and destroy the app, which deletes the volume. The one-off app is a different app. **Open:** a finished SHA stays a duplicate, and a failed SHA can be queued again because GitHub does not redeliver an acknowledged delivery. The token is still one installation id, so the allowlist cannot cross accounts. **Revisit** when a second always-on app is added, or when the webhook's event set or repository allowlist changes. Q24 stays open. **Update (2026-11-18, D79):** the blanket supersede this entry describes is now the cancel side of the head-change policy: under the default keep, a newer head is decided per push (a merge of the base branch, docs or tests only, or no overlap with what the review is still reading keeps the running review, which then posts at the head it reviewed; the new head is queued as its follow-up), and `ATLAS_HEAD_CHANGE_POLICY=cancel` restores this entry's behavior whole.

- **D55 — A verified introduced Major stays a comment (2026-11-14).** *(user, 2026-11-14, 2:37 PM CT)* Live runs #639, #640 and #642 left verified Majors as `COMMENT`, because `decide` requested changes only for a Blocker. An earlier draft of this decision made a verified introduced Major `REQUEST_CHANGES`. Brandon reversed that: keep Majors as `COMMENT` in agentic reviews, and let a repo opt in to blocking through the `REVIEW.md` threshold. **Decision:** a verified introduced Blocker is `Verdict.BLOCK` and the review event is `REQUEST_CHANGES`. A verified introduced Major or Minor is approve-with-changes and `COMMENT`. Only nits, or nothing, approves. An unverified finding and a pre-existing finding still do not set the verdict. This matches `skills/synthesizing-review-findings/SKILL.md`, which says a single Blocker is enough to block and only nits left means approve, and `templates/REVIEW.md`'s *GitHub review state vs. severity* section. That section calls Blocker-only `REQUEST_CHANGES` a deliberate choice, because the state does not clear itself, so a stale one holds an otherwise mergeable pull request until someone dismisses it. The same section says a repo that wants Majors to hold the review opts in with `unresolved_findings: block` and `unresolved_threshold: Major`. The agentic coordinator does not read `REVIEW.md`. Reading it means fetching the file from the pull request's base ref — the merge-base tarball already fetched is not that tip — parsing those two lines, failing closed when the fetch fails, and passing the result into `decide`. `docs/runbooks/pr-review-automation.md` still describes the same Blocker-only rule for the human-posted review. Those texts were not rewritten here. Offline-tested. No live model call was made for this decision. **Correction (2026-11-15, D68).** The multi-lens agentic coordinator now reads the reviewed repo's merge-base `REVIEW.md` for the derived review-wide dollar ceiling (`review_wide.review_md_ceiling`), which this entry's no-read clause ruled out; the one-shot review command still does not read it.

- **D57 — Pinned-endpoint terms, a model-aware output cap, and an optional ZDR requirement (2026-11-13).** *(Brandon, 2026-11-13: the ZDR requirement can be dropped for flexibility; limits guard only against runaway loops and costly failures, stay configurable, model-aware and diagnosable, and are never arbitrarily low. A same-day probe: ZDR Bedrock honors `cache_control` when it is sent, and OpenRouter never lists that parameter.)* Amends D51. D52 is unchanged: prior findings stay input, not scope. Offline only: the tests use recorded HTTP, and this change makes no live model call and reads no key. **Choice.** The catalog headline is the cheapest published price, so `max_price` taken from it filtered out a pinned provider priced above that headline (GLM-5.3 on Fireworks, $1.40/$4.40 per million against a $0.084/$3.60 headline; Kimi K2.7-Code on Moonshot, $0.95/$4.00 against $0.60/$3.50) and the request returned HTTP 404. Context, the completion limit and the reservation were the catalog's too, so Qwen3.8-27B on DeepInfra was asked for more context than that endpoint serves. The request now takes those terms from the rows the route itself selects. Prompt and completion prices are the highest on those rows, which only raises `max_price` and the reservation. Context and the completion limit are the smallest. A selected row with no pricing object, a zero required rate, or a missing limit refuses the call before it is sent. The catalog is still fetched once, to confirm the model id, and its prices and limits are not used. **Output cap.** When no `ATLAS_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (or `--max-output-tokens`) is set, the cap is the selected endpoints' completion limit, unless that limit is larger than a quarter of their context. Then the default is that quarter, at least 2 tokens and never above the completion limit. A review turn needs the diff, the lens and tool results in the input. On the 262,144-token models that left 26,215 input tokens (262,144 − 235,929), the default is 65,536 output tokens and 196,608 input tokens. On a context near 1,048,576 whose completion limit is about 943,718, the default is 262,144 instead of 943,718, which is what priced one GLM call at about $6.29 and above the $5 per-lens reservation. Sonnet's 64,000 completion limit on a 1,000,000 context is already under a quarter, so it stays 64,000. An override may be larger than the quarter, up to the completion limit, as long as one input token remains. Hitting the cap is still named on the limit stop. **Caching.** D51 sends `cache_control` only when every selected row lists it in `supported_parameters`. OpenRouter lists that name on no endpoint, ZDR or not, so the intersection never sends it. A live probe on 2026-11-13, outside this repo, showed that a ZDR Bedrock endpoint for Sonnet does honor `cache_control` when the request sends it with `require_parameters: true`. The repeat call was 24 times cheaper. Anthropic-family routes, model ids under `anthropic/`, therefore always send `cache_control: {"type": "ephemeral"}`, whether or not any row lists the parameter and whether or not ZDR is required. Other models keep D51's intersection. `reasoning` still follows the intersection. **ZDR.** The switch is for flexibility. It is not how caching comes back. `ATLAS_REQUIRE_ZDR` defaults to on, and `--no-require-zdr` forces it off and wins over a true variable. On, the adapter reads `GET /api/v1/endpoints/zdr` and sends `zdr: true`, as D51. Off, it reads `GET /api/v1/models/{author}/{slug}/endpoints` and does not send `zdr`. `data_collection: deny` stays on, so a request is not sent to an endpoint that trains on data. `allow_fallbacks` stays false and `provider.only` stays the pin. A missing `safety_retention` on the full endpoint list is not a refusal. A row that sets the flag true is still refused unless `ATLAS_ALLOW_SAFETY_RETENTION` is on. The usage line and the completion span record `zdr required` and whether `cache_control` was sent. The webhook review child receives this variable and the other non-secret route and limit variables. It still does not receive the webhook secret or the App private key. **Options.** Keep reading the catalog headline, which is the failure. Special-case Fireworks, Moonshot or DeepInfra, which misses the next provider priced above the headline. Cap every model at a fixed 64,000 output tokens, which is arbitrarily low for a model whose completion limit is already under a quarter of a million-token context and is the kind of ceiling the standing rule rejects. Leave D51's intersection in place for Anthropic, which is what dropped the breakpoint. Drop ZDR in order to find an endpoint that lists `cache_control`, which none do. **Cost.** The default still requires ZDR. Anthropic routes send `cache_control` either way, so an unset deploy is not left on the uncached rate by the ZDR flag. The pilot can still opt out of ZDR through config. One unauthenticated GET per provider remains; it is the ZDR list or the model endpoint list, not both. A dearer pin raises the reservation. The quarter cap lowers a reservation that the full completion limit had pushed over $5, and it does not lower a model whose completion limit was already the smaller number. **Reversibility.** Unset `ATLAS_REQUIRE_ZDR` or drop `--no-require-zdr`. Unset `ATLAS_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` to return to the quarter rule. Nothing is migrated. **Revisit when** a live Anthropic review shows the cached share, when a non-Anthropic host needs the same breakpoint, or when a model needs an input/output split other than a quarter. **Update (2026-11-14):** "which only raises" is incomplete. The highest rate includes each selected row's `pricing.overrides`, so a long-context tier can be several times the base. Reading the base alone leaves `max_price`, the reservation and the fallback settlement short of that tier. With the ZDR requirement off, a tag the ZDR list marks `safety_retention: true` is still refused unless `ATLAS_ALLOW_SAFETY_RETENTION` is on, and an unreadable ZDR list refuses the call. Sonnet 5.5's completion limit is 128,000 on a 1,000,000 context, still under a quarter, so the unset output cap stays 128,000. The Cost sentence's "one GET, not both" is no longer the whole rule: with ZDR off the provider reads both `GET /api/v1/models/{id}/endpoints` and `GET /api/v1/endpoints/zdr`. With ZDR on it still reads only the ZDR list. **Update (2026-11-14, D53):** The webhook review child does not receive the agentic limit variables. The one-shot `review` command never read `ATLAS_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` or those variables. The child receives exactly `ATLAS_MODEL`, `ATLAS_PROVIDERS`, `ATLAS_ALLOW_SAFETY_RETENTION`, `ATLAS_REQUIRE_ZDR`, and `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD`.
- **D58 — The lens harness stops throwing away and cutting short lens results: prompt, parser, repair turn, usage-based input bound, transport retry, picker prompt and parse, decision-bundle load, exporter timeout, the unread nudge, the reasoning-hint cap, and the no-progress detector (2026-11-13).** *(agent, 2026-11-13; Brandon approved the last four at 4:55 PM CT the same day, after the first seven came out of the trial diagnosis)* Offline only: the tests use recorded HTTP and scripted models, and no live model call is made anywhere in the change. The evidence is the 2026-11-13 model-trial run log (12 drafts, `atlas_reviewer`'s agentic lens harness on main at `d10fc746`, PRs #661 and #640): even Sonnet produced 1 clean lens of 9, and the diagnosis shows nearly every partial or failed lens was the harness or the prompt, not the model. The lenses are benchmarked against 4B models, which is the basis for that attribution. **No default limit value changes in this decision; D57 does that.** The routing fixes (`max_price` from the catalog headline, per-call bounds from the catalog instead of the pinned endpoints, the output cap defaulting to the full completion limit, the ZDR switch) and the `cache_control` intersection rule are D57's, on a separate PR touching `openrouter.py` provider preferences and request bodies; they are not here. **1. The finding schema is stated in the lens prompt.** `INSTRUCTIONS` now says severity is exactly one of blocker, major, minor or nit, never a level such as high, medium or low — the trials lost 9 severity attempts (raw values "medium" and "low") because only synthesis.md's "one of the levels above" reached the model, and the levels section was never sent. **2. The per-finding `lens` mismatch is resolved in the parser.** The Finding contract text lists `lens` among the per-finding fields; `lens_contract` refused any key outside its required and optional sets, and 5 attempts died on it. The parser now accepts a per-finding `lens` and drops it: the reply's top-level `lens` is the one that counts. **3. One repair turn on a contract refusal.** A final reply that breaks the finding contract earns exactly one repair turn in the same conversation, carrying the validator's exact message (which quotes nothing the model wrote); the next final reply is the one that counts; the refused reply's causes do not stick to the attempt unless the repaired reply fails too. `retry_reasons` now carries the validator's message into a retry attempt's system prompt, where only the cause name travelled before. The parser also accepts a reply whose last fenced block is one JSON object after prose (4 attempts lost to prose before the fence). **4. The input bound is measured from data, not bytes.** D43's one-token-per-UTF-8-byte bound stays for a first request, which has no reported usage to build on; after a response, the bound is that response's reported `prompt_tokens` plus the messages added since at 2.4 bytes per token (`BYTES_PER_TOKEN_BOUND`), rounded up, plus the 64-token overhead. 2.4 sits under the lowest bytes-per-token ratio the trials measured (2.47 minimum, 2.80 median, 771 calls), so the estimate stays an upper bound on that data; it is an assumption about tokenizers, not a proof, which is why the basis is recorded: the completion span carries `atlas.input_bound`, and an input-cap refusal's observed value names the basis it came from. The enforced input cap itself stays configurable and unchanged — this is a measurement fix, not a cap change; DeepSeek as shipped had `configured 104858, observed 119861` against a real prompt of 22.9k–26.4k tokens. **5. Transport failures are retried.** A read timeout is retried once at the call level (`OpenRouterLensModel.turn`), inside the reservation the loop already placed — the timed-out request produced no response to settle, and the reservation prices the turn's worst case. The transport's timeout classifier (`http._transport_error`) raises `TransportTimeoutError`, and the adapter wraps every transport failure as `TransportError`, a new `BoundaryError` subclass; the loop maps it to a new `Cause.TRANSPORT_ERROR` ("a model call failed on the transport"), which is in `_RETRYABLE`. A model error — a response the provider produced, an HTTP 4xx/5xx, a served-by mismatch, an unparseable body — is still one request, no retry, cause `MODEL_ERROR`, not retryable. The 300 s timeout stays. The trials lost 10 lens attempts to 300 s read timeouts that arrived in two 2-second bursts. **6. The picker prompt and parse.** The instructions now say the critical tier always runs before the picks and does not spend the slots (naming the four lenses), and that extra names are dropped, not an error — "do not correct yourself with a second list", the exact failure shape Sonnet produced on #640 (a list of 9, then prose, then a corrected list of 8, refused whole). `parse_picks` reads the last JSON object in the reply when the whole reply is not one object, skipping a decoded object's own braces. `MAX_LENSES` stays 8, and the always-include-pr-hygiene rule was initially deferred as a policy call — see 10. **7. Bundle gaps.** `LensLibrary` reads the reviewing-a-decision bundle after reviewing-a-change, so `reviewing-decision-lifecycle` (10,638 characters) is loadable and counted; three trial runs picked it and lost it, and #640 m3 was structurally unreachable. A picked name with no body in any bundle is still reported NOT RUN with its reason in the Lenses line — `plan_lenses` never dropped it, and a test pins that. docs-health still has no lens body anywhere; that stays open. **8. The Phoenix exporter timeout.** `OTLPSpanExporter` was built with a 1 s HTTP timeout; every batch export to the busy collector failed on long reviews and dropped its spans (Haiku #661 lost its review span and a lens span, Haiku #640 five lens spans, DeepSeek-fix two). The exporter timeout is 30 s. The flush wait stays at 1 s (its own test pins that, so a shutdown cannot hang on a dead collector). **9. The unread nudge (reverses part of D42).** When a final reply leaves any changed file's diff unread, the loop sends the unread list back once — fenced as untrusted data — before accepting a final reply, so a triaging model can finish its coverage; a second unread final reply is accepted as the attempt's answer and stays partial. D42's rule that an unread file vetoes a retry is narrowed, not removed: `FILES_UNREAD` no longer cancels an `UNPARSED` retry (the parse refusal is what threw the reply away), and it still vetoes every other retryable cause, including `TRANSPORT_ERROR`. The D42 entry itself is not edited in place; this paragraph is its update. **10. The reasoning hint is capped for open-weight models.** `default_reasoning_tokens` returns half the output cap for an `anthropic/` model id and `min(half, 8192)` for every other id: the trials measured the open-weight candidates spending up to 96% of each turn's completion tokens on reasoning at the half-output hint, at 31–46 s a turn, until the lens clock ran out. An `ATLAS_REASONING_MAX_TOKENS` override still wins, and the resolved hint is recorded on the completion span as `atlas.reasoning_hint` (`CallFacts.reasoning_hint`), so a wrong derivation is visible. **11. The no-progress detector.** Once every changed file's diff is read, 6 more turns without a final reply inject one "finalize now with what you have" turn; independently, 3 identical (tool, validated-args) calls inject the same nudge at any coverage. One nudge per attempt, from whichever trigger fires first. The thresholds are limits like the others: `no_progress_turns` (6) and `duplicate_calls` (3), overridable with `ATLAS_NO_PROGRESS_TURNS` and `ATLAS_DUPLICATE_CALLS`, refused when cleared to unset or not a positive integer. When one fires, the attempt's ledger line carries it in `nudges` and the completion span answers it with `atlas.nudge`. **12. The policy half of the picker rule.** `reviewing-pr-and-process-hygiene` is included ahead of the picks, on every selection path including the picker fallbacks, when the pull request body states verification results — a test count, a coverage figure, or a CI or check-run claim (`body_states_verification`, a heuristic over untrusted text that only decides lens inclusion). This closes the deferred policy call from 6; a body claiming "2105 passed" is exactly the surface that lens checks against what actually happened. **What did not change:** the five review limits' defaults, the per-call token caps, `MAX_LENSES`, the 300 s timeout, the 64-call bridge session cap, D42's veto except as stated in 9, and D43's byte bound except as stated in 4. **Tests.** Each change names a test that fails when it is reverted: the severity wording and repair turn (`test_the_instructions_state_the_severity_words`, `test_a_contract_refusal_earns_one_repair_turn_and_the_next_reply_counts`, `test_the_retry_reasons_carry_the_validators_message`), the `lens` key and the last-fenced parse (`test_a_per_finding_lens_key_is_accepted_and_ignored`, `test_a_reply_whose_last_fenced_block_is_one_json_object_parses`), the meter and its basis (`test_the_input_bound_comes_from_the_reported_usage_plus_new_messages`, `test_the_first_turn_keeps_the_byte_bound`, `test_a_conversation_restart_falls_back_to_the_byte_bound`, `test_an_input_cap_refusal_names_the_usage_basis`, `test_an_unreadable_usage_count_falls_back_to_the_byte_bound`), the transport retry and cause (`test_a_read_timeout_is_retried_once_at_the_call_level`, `test_a_second_timeout_is_a_transport_error_and_no_third_request`, `test_a_transport_error_other_than_a_timeout_is_not_retried`, `test_a_read_timeout_is_a_transport_timeout`, the attestation retryability table), the picker (`test_the_last_json_object_in_a_reply_is_parsed`, `test_instructions_say_the_critical_tier_always_runs_and_extras_are_dropped`, `test_a_body_stating_verification_results_adds_pr_hygiene_ahead_of_the_picks`, `test_verification_detection`, `test_the_pull_body_stating_verification_results_reaches_the_selection`), the bundle (`test_the_decision_bundles_lenses_are_loadable`, `test_a_picked_decision_bundle_lens_runs_whole`), the exporter (`test_the_exporter_timeout_is_the_export_setting_not_the_flush_wait`), the nudge and detector (`test_an_unread_final_reply_is_nudged_once_and_the_next_reply_counts`, `test_the_unread_nudge_is_sent_once`, `test_no_progress_is_nudged_after_the_configured_turns`, `test_five_turns_past_coverage_are_not_nudged`, `test_three_identical_calls_are_nudged_even_before_coverage`, `test_the_no_progress_thresholds_are_configurable_and_bad_ones_are_refused`), and the hint cap (`test_the_reasoning_hint_is_capped_outside_anthropic`, `test_the_span_names_the_input_bound_the_hint_and_any_nudge`). **Cost.** No live call is made by the change. The effect on the next live run is the point: fixes 1–5 here are the ones the diagnosis projected would turn mostly-clean runs at trial cost; D57's routing and output-cap work is what the open-weight models additionally need. **Reversibility.** Every new threshold is a limit; the parser leniencies are additive; the nudge and repair turns spend existing turn and call budgets; the exporter timeout is a constant. **Revisit** after the next live trial: if a lens still ends UNPARSED or INPUT_CAP at the measured usage, the 2.4 divisor or the repair-once rule is the first suspect; and if a nudged lens produces findings only under the nudge, the detector's thresholds are tuning inputs, not fixed constants. The execution and CI-evidence work that reaches the remaining protocol Minors is planned, not built: [`plans/2026-11-13-agentic-execution-and-ci-evidence.md`](plans/2026-11-13-agentic-execution-and-ci-evidence.md). **Update (2026-11-14, after #646/D44 merged first):** Brandon directed that #677 adopt D44's contract-repair turn and drop this decision's own duplicate. Item 3's separate repair turn is withdrawn: a reply outside the finding contract is now sent back by D44's `_repair` — the rejected reply travels as the model's own `AssistantText` turn, and the one user turn quotes the validation error through `quote_untrusted`. D58's three schema facts moved into that same turn, which D44's original message lacked: the four severity words (blocker, major, minor, nit — never high, medium or low), that a per-finding `lens` key is accepted and dropped, and that prose before the reply is fine because the last fenced JSON object is the one read. `retry_reasons` carrying the validator's message into a retry attempt's prompt (item 3) is unchanged, as are the parser leniencies in item 2. The cause-restore mechanism item 3 needed is gone with it: D44's `_final` runs only on the accepted or finally-failed reply, so a refused reply never pollutes the attempt's causes. The unread-list nudge (item 9) stays and now composes with D44's continuation: the nudge is the in-conversation chance to finish coverage cheaply; a nudged reply that still leaves files unread ends the attempt partial, and D44's continuation then hands those same files back to the lens as its own scoped run — the continuation tests were re-pointed for a model that ignores the nudge. Everything else in this decision is unchanged. **Correction (2026-11-14, protocol review round 2 of #677):** four claims in the frozen text above were wrong and are corrected here. "No default limit value changes" is false as written: this decision adds two new limit defaults (`no_progress_turns` 6, `duplicate_calls` 3) and changes a derived default (the reasoning hint outside `anthropic/` is now capped at 8,192, not half the output cap); what it does not change is any *existing* limit's value. The "the lenses are benchmarked against 4B models" sentence is withdrawn: it was the owner's rationale, not something the 2026-11-13 trials establish. "see 10" for the deferred pr-hygiene rule meant item 12. The approval note meant items 4, 5, 7 and item 12's policy half, approved at 4:55 PM CT on top of the seven the trial diagnosis proposed. Item 8's "so a shutdown cannot hang" is also narrowed: the flush *wait* is bounded by a thread join (`_FLUSH_MS`), but on OpenTelemetry SDK 1.45.2 `force_flush` ignores its timeout, so an export against a dead collector can keep running in the background after the wait. Two additions the same review required: a follow-up turn that can never run (turn cap, clock, budget) no longer discards the reply the harness already judged — the judged outcome is held and returned with the stop's cause, so a valid reply's findings survive; and a timed-out request's retry is now the loop's own reserved, counted call (`atlas.timeout_retry` on the span), skipped when less than one timeout fits the remaining clocks, instead of a second possibly-billed request inside one reservation. **Correction (2026-11-14, protocol review round 3 of #677), continued:** item 4's frozen text says the 2.4 bytes-per-token divisor "stays an upper bound" on the trial data; the divisor is gone — the delta estimate is one token per byte, the provable end of the byte-bound assumption, because dense content (base64, minified fixtures) can tokenize under any ordinary ratio. Item 5's frozen text says "the adapter wraps every transport failure as `TransportError`"; the wrapping now excludes a response that arrived (`ResponseTooLargeError` is a model-level error) and the timeout, which propagates as `TransportTimeoutError` for the loop's retry. Item 12's "a CI or check-run claim" is now "a CI or check-run result": a body that only mentions CI, as a flake fix does, is not a verification claim. The trial run log's figures are not in this repo; they rest on the operator's reports. The frozen test list names adapter-level retry tests that the loop-level rework superseded; the live names are `test_a_timeout_raises_for_the_loop_to_retry`, `test_the_timeout_retry_is_its_own_reserved_call`, `test_a_second_timeout_at_the_loop_is_not_retried_again` and `test_the_timeout_retry_is_skipped_when_no_clock_room_remains`. **Update (2026-11-14, D54 alignment):** item 1's "never a level such as high, medium or low" is withdrawn: D54 maps a closed alias set (critical, crit and blocking are blocker; high is major; medium and moderate are minor; low and trivial are nit) and DROPS a finding whose severity still matches nothing, naming the raw severity in the ledger's `dropped` field and the draft's Dropped findings, and a dropped finding blocks approval. INSTRUCTIONS and D44's repair turn now state that rule instead of the refusal claim; the repair turn is no longer spent on an unreadable severity alone, because a drop parses. The outcome and ledger carry both `dropped` (D54) and `nudges` (this decision).
- **D59 — The agentic dry run reaches and runs the lenses end to end on the shipped fixture (2026-11-14).** *(agent, 2026-11-14, from the day's two protocol reviews, #650 round 2 and #651 round 1, which both found the shipped dry run never reaches a lens)* `agentic-review --dry-run` staged the fixture as GitHub but left the runner as the one-shot's rules-only fake (`runner-script.json`), which has no trusted-git step, so `run_bridge` failed at bridge staging ("no trusted git in the runner", exit 127) and every lens was NOT RUN; and the dry-run lens model never called a tool, so even a staged runner would have left the file-coverage gate closed. Every review of an entry-point change therefore had to hand-build fakes for `run_bridge`, `lens_hold` and `run_attempt` to reach synthesis, decide, the draft and the exit code. **Choice.** The offline pieces the test suite already trusts move into the package, and the dry-run wiring uses them. `atlas_reviewer/local_runner.py` holds `LocalSprites` — the exec wrapper run as a real local subprocess, moved out of the test helpers so there is one implementation — and `LocalTasks`, the Tasks API on a unix socket under the state directory, so the keepalive hold is created, held and deleted for real. The model is `atlas_reviewer/lens_script.py`'s scripted reviewer, driven by a JSON lens script in the fixture directory (`--lens-script`, default the fixture's `scenarios/blocker.json`). The script's reading behavior is fixed — the order the lens instructions themselves ask for, every changed file's diff page by page, then a HEAD `read_file` of every file a finding cites — and what varies is data: which findings each lens reports, each entry validated by the real finding-contract parser at load (an unknown severity loads, because dropping it is a scenario, D54), and which changed files no lens ever reads (`unread`), for a coverage-gap scenario. A retry reusing the model instance starts its coverage over, so a replayed attempt reads again instead of answering from memory. **What runs as real code.** The runner lifecycle with its deny-all set and read-back — a safety hook is never skipped on this path, and the end-to-end test asserts the read-back event from the telemetry. Bridge staging under real git, `diff_file` and `read_file` against the fixture's head and merge-base trees (the fixture gains a `source-<first 7 hex of the SHA>/` tree the fixture GitHub serves at that SHA, beside the head tree in `source/`), the quote checks against the head tree, the attestation ledger, the unread-list and no-progress nudges, the synthesis, the mechanical verdict (D55), the draft, and the exit code. **What stays impossible.** Nothing is read from the environment — the shipped command runs with an empty one, and its test proves that with a subprocess — no network client is constructed, no model key is named, and `--lens-script` is refused without `--dry-run`, so a canned reply can never stand in for a live model. **Ledger.** A dry run's attestation ledger is a per-run file under a temporary directory in the state directory, so re-running or editing a scenario replays from scratch instead of reusing an attempt settled for the same head and lens body; the runners ledger and the review lock behave as live. **Tests.** `test_dry_run_end_to_end.py` runs in CI and drives the real CLI entry against the shipped fixture with one scenario per verdict class: the default Blocker (`REQUEST_CHANGES`, exit 5, one verified finding `L1-F1`, the committed-secrets section for the fixture's own planted key with its values scrubbed), a verified Major and a verified removed-line Minor (`COMMENT`, exit 0), an approve (`APPROVE`, exit 0), a coverage gap that leaves `settings.ini` unread (exit 4, the file named), and a dropped unknown-severity finding (exit 4, named under Dropped findings). The unit suite covers the loader and the turn policy without a runner. **What changed for the operator.** The agentic dry run can now exit 0 or 5 where it previously always exited 4 with every lens NOT RUN; the one-shot `review --dry-run` is unchanged and still always exits 4, because its offline provider reviews no file. Offline only: no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made for this decision.

- **D47 — A whole-line quote may match within two lines, and internal whitespace is collapsed (2026-11-12).** *(user, 2026-11-12)* **Amends D40's quote rule** (its **Quote checks** text says every quote must be the cited lines of the head file; see the update at the end of D40). A live run rejected a finding whose evidence was an exit-code issue the owner read as a protocol Major. The rejected quote itself is not in the comments on #639, #640 or #642, so this decision follows the two examples the owner named rather than replaying that quote. **Decision:** comparison collapses internal whitespace, so a doubled space does not fail a line that is otherwise the cited line. A quote of whole lines whose combined text is at least 8 non-space characters may match the same number of lines starting within 2 of the citation. That match is verified and tagged `head within 2 lines of the citation` in the draft, and the finding's location becomes the matched head lines, so the location names the lines that hold the quote. The cited range is not kept on the finding. A shorter quote, including a one-character line such as `}`, still has to be the cited line and does not get the 2-line search. A long fragment of a nearby line does not match either, because the drifted window has to be those whole lines. A wider cited range is still not searched. Removed lines also collapse internal whitespace, and a removed-line match is checked before the drifted match, so a quote of a removed line is tagged `removed lines` even when the same text sits near the citation. **Context files:** a quote may also come from a file the pull request does not change, when the lens read that file at HEAD with `read_file` in the same attempt. Only that file's head lines are added to what quotes are checked against, and the same span, fragment and 2-line rules apply. A file read only at BASE or DEFAULT, or not read, still does not verify. **A context-file finding can set the verdict.** A verified quote from such a file is an ordinary verified finding: when the lens marks it `introduced`, an introduced Blocker is `REQUEST_CHANGES` and an introduced Major is `COMMENT`, the same as for a changed file (D55). This is kept on purpose, because a pull request can break a caller in a file it does not touch. It also means a lens that mislabels such a finding `introduced` reaches the verdict; the quote must still verify against the file as read at HEAD in that attempt. Offline-tested. No live model call was made for this decision.

- **D56 — Every lens the picker names has a body, and a missing one is listed under Lens selection (2026-11-12).** *(user, 2026-11-12)* On a Fly config pull request the picker ranked `auditing-infrastructure-as-code` first, and that lens had no body in the change-review bundle. D44 already reads a body that lives in another collapsed entrypoint, and that lens's body is the one generated for the audit entrypoint. **Decision:** a test takes every review-lens name in `skills/choosing-review-lenses/SKILL.md` and requires `LensLibrary().body` to return it. `auditing-infrastructure-as-code` is one of those names. The synthesizer and `grounding-review-in-tool-output` are not review lenses and are not in that set. A lens that still has no body is not handed to the review loop. The draft lists that pick as NOT RUN under **Lens selection**, and the review does not approve. `names()` still lists only the change-review bundle. The test reads names as backtick spans that the picker's own name pattern accepts; a single-word backtick is prose, not a lens, and a name cited without backticks is not read at all, so the test's completeness half -- every lens directory except the picker's own must be cited -- fails when the generated skill stops backticking a lens name. A name the picker cites that has no `skills/` directory fails the test rather than being skipped. Offline-tested. No live model call was made for this decision.

- **D60 — Redaction and refusal bookkeeping hardening from issues #663 and #664 (2026-11-14).** *(agent, 2026-11-14; corrected after review rounds 1 and 2 of PR #687; numbered D60 here because #688's dry-run decision took D59 on main first)* Offline only: recorded HTTP and synthetic values; no live model call was made, and no real secret is in any fixture. **Redaction (#663).** `redact` gains a `sprites-token` pattern: four slash-separated segments `<org slug>/<numeric org id>/<token id>/<secret>`, every segment `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, only the second all digits, and the secret at least eight characters and carrying at least one digit. The shape is the one the operator confirmed the deployed token has, checked locally without printing the value; the CLI docs' example (`my-org/token-id/secret`) shows the same shape without the numeric id. The first draft anchored on the literal `org/`, which matches no real token and flags the `w3.org` XML-schema namespace in every Maven `pom.xml`, XSD or WSDL diff; the review's false-positive cases (`w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance`, `/org/<id>/repositories`, an `api.example.com/org/<id>/…` URL, `repos/<id>/issues/comments`, `api/<year>/users/list`) are tests now, and a `pom.xml` diff through `scan_diff` gets no finding. Round 2 added the boundary: the match may not start after a `/`, `.`, `-` or `_` — where a URL path's segments begin — so commit and job links, `api.github.com` paths, GitLab merge-request paths, `files.pythonhosted.org` wheel URLs (a `uv.lock` update) and dated paths are not flagged, in `redact` or as committed-secret findings; the token positions that remain covered are pinned (bare, after a `Bearer` prefix, after `ATLAS_SPRITES_TOKEN=`, in JSON, in single quotes, after a `--token` flag). A bare relative four-segment path at the start of a line, such as a dated blog permalink `blog/2024/03/my-post-2`, still matches: no boundary distinguishes it from a token without a scheme or host. `detect_secrets` reports a matching token under the label. The known-value scrub replaces the value's standard and URL-safe base64 (padded and unpadded), lower- and upper-case hex, its percent-encoded form, and its JSON string body with `/` escaped as `\/`, under the same label (`controller-credential`, or `possible-committed-secret` for a `flagged` value), every form longest first, so a value that contains another is not cut short by the shorter one. Bytes re-encode with `surrogateescape`, so a value read from the environment with undecodable bytes scrubs instead of crashing redaction. Not covered, stated here rather than overclaimed: base64 of a blob that carries the value at a shifted 3-byte alignment (`Bearer <secret>` encoded whole), a value split across lines, hex in mixed case, and lower-case percent escapes (`%2f` against the upper-case `%2F` this builds). The exact value stays the primary defense; the pattern and the encodings are defense in depth. **Refusal recording (#664).** `OpenRouterProvider.complete` records every way the one-shot path fails closed before a request in `budget.refusals` — a ZDR-list or `/model` catalog GET whose transport failed was previously not recorded there. The agentic adapter reaches `load_terms` directly (`tool_model.OpenRouterLensModel._ready`), not through `complete`, so it records the same failures in the provider's budget itself, one entry per failed read; the picker and the lenses share one provider instance. What reads those lines: `usage_lines` (`ReviewBudget.report`) on the one-shot path. The agentic draft does not print the provider's budget refusals as a list — round 2 corrected the earlier claim that they reach the draft's budget totals; what the operator reads in the agentic draft is the lens line's cause detail, which carries the refusal text. The exception still propagates unchanged and no call is sent. **Refusal labeling (#664).** A ZDR or pricing refusal (`BudgetRefusedError` or `PricingUnknownError`) raised while pricing or placing a turn is cause `MODEL_REFUSED` ("a model call was refused"), not `MODEL_ERROR`. It still fails the lens and is still not retried (`_FAILING`, not `_RETRYABLE`); the draft's lens line and the ledger's `causes` name a policy refusal instead of a model failure. The parity gate reads lens state, which a refusal does not distinguish; the claim is about the report's labels, not the gate's inputs. A 5xx on those GETs is also `MODEL_REFUSED` (round-1 Nit 1, left as a follow-up). **Tests.** Each change names a test that fails when it is reverted (the redaction bounds and boundary were also checked against mutants): `test_a_sprites_token_shape_is_redacted_and_reported`, `test_sprites_token_ids_and_slugs_of_any_length_are_redacted`, `test_a_seven_character_secret_segment_is_not_a_sprites_token`, `test_a_token_is_redacted_in_every_position_it_appears_in`, `test_text_merely_shaped_like_a_sprites_token_survives`, `test_a_url_or_path_is_not_a_sprites_token`, `test_a_diff_with_url_shapes_gets_no_committed_secret_finding`, `test_a_pom_xml_diff_with_the_schema_namespace_gets_no_finding`, `test_a_seven_character_value_is_below_the_scrub_floor_and_an_eight_is_not`, `test_every_encoding_form_of_a_known_value_is_scrubbed`, `test_overlapping_known_values_replace_longest_first`, `test_a_value_with_undecodable_bytes_is_scrubbed_not_fatal`, `test_known_credentials_are_scrubbed_in_base64_and_hex`, `test_a_zdr_list_transport_failure_is_recorded_as_a_refusal`, `test_a_catalog_transport_timeout_is_recorded_as_a_refusal`, `test_a_zdr_list_timeout_on_the_lens_path_is_recorded_as_a_refusal`, `test_a_zdr_list_http_failure_on_the_lens_path_is_recorded_as_a_refusal`, `test_a_catalog_timeout_on_the_lens_path_is_recorded_as_a_refusal`, `test_a_refusals_detail_reaches_the_drafts_lens_line`, `test_a_zdr_refusal_while_pricing_is_its_own_cause_and_not_retried`, `test_a_pricing_refusal_on_the_turn_is_its_own_cause`, and the `MODEL_REFUSED` row in the attestation retryability table. **Reversibility.** Remove the pattern, the encoding scrub and the cause mapping; no setting, stored format or ledger version changes. A ledger line already written stores causes by name, so code older than this decision reading such a ledger fails on `Cause[...]` — that matters only on a rollback with a ledger left in place. **Revisit** when a live review ends in a refusal whose label or recorded refusal text an operator cannot act on, when a real token shape departs from the four segments, or when the agentic draft should print the provider's budget refusals as a list.

- **D61 — A lens retry keeps the earlier attempts' verified findings (2026-11-15).** *(agent, 2026-11-15, from the Nov 14 model trials on main at `10b86d85`: across 12 trial runs the retry path lost 21 verified findings, including a verified catch of a real Minor on PR #661)* **The defect.** `QUOTES_FAILED` is retryable (`attestation.py`), so one unverifiable quote re-ran the whole lens, and `next_step` let the last attempt's outcome stand: an empty or weaker retry replaced findings the quote checker had already verified. **Decision.** A settled lens's outcome is the last attempt's with every earlier attested attempt's verified finding merged into it, deduplicated, computed from the ledger alone in `next_step` so every reader of a settle sees the same merge. The merge is the retry's job scoped to what failed: a finding the retry restates stays the retry's copy (the retry's reply is the one that counts); an earlier verified finding the retry could not verify replaces the retry's unverified copy at the same citation; an earlier verified finding the retry does not restate is appended after the retry's own. The identity of "the same finding" is the location, the severity, the valence and the attribution, plus the whitespace-collapsed, case-folded finding and evidence text; the route and the fix are not the identity, because a retry may refile the same issue with either changed. Only verified findings are carried forward, and only from an attempt whose own reply was attested — the mint rule that an unattested attempt contributes nothing is unchanged, so an outcome whose last attempt never attested keeps its carried findings on the ledger but reports none. **Honesty.** The merged outcome keeps the last attempt's attestation, causes, detail, unread list and changed-file count, so a retry that still failed is still partial or failed and never clean just because it kept earlier findings, and coverage (`coverage_complete`, the unread list, the per-file gate) reads the last attempt exactly as before. A retry that comes back clean settles clean while carrying the earlier verified findings; a retry that never attests (a second contract failure, a misacknowledgement) leaves the lens failed. The retry prompt is unchanged: the body is still applied whole (D40), and the merge — not a narrowed retry prompt — is what keeps the earlier work. **Tests.** `test_attestation.py` pins the merge at the policy level (an empty retry, a weaker retry, a restated finding, a non-restated one, no carrying of unverified findings, the attested-only skip pinned against a ledger line that carries findings on an unattested attempt, every key part — location, severity, valence, attribution — pinned by two verified findings that differ only in that part, the normalized finding and evidence text, and the last attempt's own duplicates kept); `test_coordinator.py` pins it end to end over the real bridge with the scripted model: a verified finding survives an empty retry with the lens clean, and survives a retry that verifies less with the lens still partial and escalated. Offline only: no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made for this decision.

- **D62 — A Sprites capacity refusal degrades only its lens; Sprite creation waits longer on a bounded schedule that honors Retry-After, and is capped in concurrency (2026-11-15).** *(agent, 2026-11-15, from issue #690: the 2026-11-14 model trials lost three reviews — about $2.05 — when about 12 pooled lenses created Sprites at once and the three waits of 1, 2 and 4s that #636 left behind gave up, so `CreateRefusedError` escaped `future.result()` and crashed the review with every other lens's result lost; a 502 during a tree upload left its lens NOT RUN)* **Choice.** A 429'd create now waits a schedule that doubles from 2s and is capped at 60s a wait, `create_retries` waits long (default 6, about two minutes; 0 to 24, `ATLAS_CREATE_RETRIES` / `--create-retries`), against the 7s the old three waits spent. A Retry-After header the 429 carries is parsed by the Sprites adapter (read case-insensitively, as the repo's other Retry-After readers read their headers; delay-seconds or HTTP-date; the parser never raises, so an unreadable value — including a non-ASCII digit look-alike such as `²`, which `isdigit` accepts and `float` refuses — is None, and the schedule stands in) and honored up to a 120s per-wait cap, so one huge value cannot park a lens's deadline in a single wait; each wait is also truncated to what the runner's deadline has left, and the deadline still bounds the whole loop. The coordinator hands its pooled lenses and continuations a per-review gate sized `create_concurrency` (1 to 64, default 4, `ATLAS_CREATE_CONCURRENCY` / `--create-concurrency`), so at most that many Sprites create at once; a slot wait past 0.1s is recorded in telemetry, and a slot that never frees before the deadline degrades that runner before any create call is sent — its `_Create` outcome stays `not started`, so cleanup deletes nothing and the ledger entry clears, and the same holds for a deadline that fires during a 429 wait, because the 429 is definitive: the server did not act, so the outcome returns to `not started` for the wait and the run degrades with cleanup `NOT_CREATED` and no ledger entry left rather than `DESTROY_UNVERIFIED` for a Sprite that never existed. Every wait and the final give-up are telemetry events, so hitting the limits is diagnosable. Folding the plain positive-integer limits' parser into the ranged one to share one message shape also changed what those nine limits say when they refuse — a non-integer now reads “must be an integer, not X” and a below-one value “must be an integer of at least 1, not X”, where each said “must be a positive integer, not X” — both new texts pinned by test. A create still refused after the schedule raises `CreateRefusedError` as before, but `review_lenses` now catches it per lens: that lens is NOT RUN with why in the draft, the other lenses' results and continuations run on, the spend is not halted, and coverage stays incomplete so the review cannot approve. The 502-during-a-tree-upload path needed no code change — a `BoundaryError` there already fails only that runner, and its lens is NOT RUN with the note — so the new test pins it. **Not touched:** the lens-retry logic another change owns (`attestation.next_step` and `coordinator._result`). **Tests.** The runner suite pins the default schedule's shape (the literal default retry count and cap, and the exact waits the schedule produces at both jitter extremes), Retry-After honored and capped, a Retry-After past the deadline starting no create and degrading that runner with `NOT_CREATED` and no ledger entry, the waits and give-up named in telemetry, a gate of one serializing two creates with the second's wait recorded and the first's not, and a slot that never frees degrading before any create; the coordinator suite pins a 429 refused past the backoff degrading only that lens while the other lens's result reaches the draft, the gate capping concurrent creates at the configured size, the review's own `create_retries` reaching the runner, a garbage Retry-After degrading instead of crashing, and the 502 leaving that lens NOT RUN the same way; the limits suite pins the env ranges edge to edge and refuses a bool, like D58's thresholds, and pins the plain positive-integer limits' new refusal texts (kills the mutant that reverts either). All offline: no live Sprite, model or GitHub call was made for this decision.

- **D63 — Anthropic routes pin one endpoint, and the run summary counts prompt-cache misses (2026-11-15).** *(agent, 2026-11-15, from issue #695 and Brandon's constraints there; closes #695; corrected after protocol review round 1 on #699 the same day)* Amends D51 and D57's default pin. Offline only: the tests use recorded HTTP and fakes, and this change makes no live model call and reads no key. **Evidence (the 2026-11-14 model trials, issue #695).** Sonnet's cache writes summed to 1.73x (#661) and 1.66x (#640) the sessions' final contexts; cache writes were 60-64% of a Sonnet review; 72 of 146 turns on #661 and 46 of 179 on #640 missed the previous turn's cache. All 358 Sonnet calls were served by Amazon Bedrock: the default pin's bare `amazon-bedrock` slug matches every eligible Bedrock endpoint, regions included, and each endpoint keeps its own prompt cache, so consecutive turns of one lens conversation alternated between independent caches (a turn's hit was often the context from two turns back). Haiku on Google Vertex missed none of its 217 turns, and its writes equalled its final contexts. **Choice.** An Anthropic-family model id (one under `anthropic/`) now defaults `provider.only` to the single endpoint `google-vertex/global` (`ANTHROPIC_PINNED_PROVIDERS`), so a lens's consecutive turns share that endpoint's cache. On the public ZDR endpoint list (fetched 2026-11-15, no key, and re-fetched during #699's round-1 review), exactly three tags carry a `safety_retention: false` ZDR row for both `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5` and `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`: `google-vertex/global`, `google-vertex/europe`, and `amazon-bedrock/eu-west-1`. `google-vertex/global` is the cheapest of the three (cache read $0.10 and cache write $2.50 per million against $0.11/$2.75 on both others) and the only region-unbound one, which is the whole tie-break: `google-vertex/europe` also served both models and already sat in the pin, and it loses on both price and residency. A cheaper or equal row outside that three-tag set does not qualify: the bare `amazon-bedrock` tag prices the same as `google-vertex/global` for Sonnet, but it has no Haiku row at all, and a bare slug expands to every eligible regional endpoint of that provider — the spreading this pin removes. Every other model keeps the four-slug pin (`PINNED_PROVIDERS`). `ATLAS_PROVIDERS` or `--providers` replaces either default, and one slug remains a valid pin. **What the old default could not do.** Against that same list the four-slug default refused Haiku outright before any request: the bare `amazon-bedrock` and `google-vertex/us` slugs have no ZDR row for `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5` (its rows are `amazon-bedrock/global`, `amazon-bedrock/us`, `amazon-bedrock/eu-west-1`, `google-vertex/global`, `google-vertex/europe`, `google-vertex/us-east5`). The single-endpoint default is therefore also what makes a default-config Haiku route runnable, and the reversibility note below is worded for that. **Constraints kept.** `data_collection: deny` stays; the pinned endpoint is ZDR with `safety_retention: false`, and Safety Retention stays opt-in. On failover: `allow_fallbacks: false` kept requests off providers outside `only` (per the 2026-11-07 routing doc), but what routing did among several pinned slugs — and whether one pinned endpoint's failure reroutes to a sibling — was not verified against live behavior, so this decision does not claim the single pin gave up nothing; what it trades is the old pin's within-list spread for cache locality, and an unavailable pinned endpoint fails the call loudly (the refusal below) or errors, fixed by repinning. **The deployed one-shot route moves too (for Brandon).** The webhook service's review child is `python -m atlas_reviewer review` (`atlas_reviewer/deploy-fly.md`), which reads the same default through `model_route`. No deployed setting is changed by this PR and no `ATLAS_PROVIDERS` is set on the Machine, so with the merge the deployed Anthropic one-shot review's effective route changes from the four-slug pin to `google-vertex/global` — a path with one model call per review, which gains no caching benefit from the pin. **Residency.** `global` is not region-bound; the previous default named EU (`google-vertex/europe`) and US tags. A workload that needs EU or US residency must pin it explicitly with `ATLAS_PROVIDERS` (for example `google-vertex/europe`, which also serves both models). A pin the endpoint list does not serve is refused before any request, with the tag named — it does not silently spread (that refusal behavior predates this decision; a test now pins it for this default). **The diagnostic.** `cache_stats.CacheLedger` records, per session, each turn's reported prompt, cached and cache-write counts; a session is one conversation (the turns between one system prompt and the next, the same boundary the input meter already uses), a turn is a cache hit only when its reported cached tokens are at least the previous turn's reported prompt tokens, and anything else — shorter, or unreported — is a miss; a session's first turn is never a miss. `OpenRouterLensModel` feeds it, and the agentic draft gains a `## Prompt cache` section: one line per session (turns, misses, cache writes, final context) plus a run total of the cache writes against the sum of the sessions' final contexts. The sessions are ordered by lens name in the draft, so the pool's completion order does not shuffle them. The tracker is model-agnostic; any model implementation that reports the same usage fields can feed it. The one-shot `review` path is not given the section: its calls are one turn each, so a miss count there is structurally zero. **Cost (the issue's estimate, not a measurement).** About $1.67 per Sonnet review ($6.63 to about $4.96), a repricing of the logged turns as if each had hit the previous turn's cache. **Tests.** Each behavior names a test that fails when the behavior is reverted (mutation-confirmed where marked): the Anthropic default and the four-slug default elsewhere (`test_the_anthropic_default_pin_is_one_endpoint_and_the_others_keep_four`, `test_the_provider_default_pin_follows_the_model`), the `anthropic/` prefix boundary against lookalike ids (mutation-confirmed: `test_the_pin_boundary_is_the_anthropic_prefix_not_a_substring`), the request carrying the single slug (`test_request_is_pinned_to_one_model_with_no_fallbacks_or_plugins`), an explicit pin overriding the default (`test_an_explicit_provider_list_wins_over_the_anthropic_default`), a missing pinned endpoint refusing (`test_a_pinned_endpoint_the_list_lacks_is_refused_not_spread`), the miss rule's four edges and its hit case (`test_cache_stats.py`: first turn never a miss, hit at equality, short read, unreported read, unreported previous prompt), the session split at a system change and its marker label (`test_a_changed_system_prompt_opens_a_new_session_named_by_the_body_marker`), the draft section with its total and the blank line that keeps the runner and round-log lines out of the cache list (mutation-confirmed: `test_the_draft_renders_the_prompt_cache_sessions`, `test_a_draft_without_cache_sessions_has_no_prompt_cache_section`), the sessions' order in the draft — by lens name, with one lens's own attempts kept in run order whatever order the pool created the adapters in (mutation-confirmed: `test_the_cache_section_orders_sessions_by_lens_and_keeps_attempt_order`, and `by_label`'s unit tests in `test_cache_stats.py`), and the live path's wiring from the per-lens adapters to that section, through the real CLI entry with faked OpenRouter, GitHub and Sprites (mutation-confirmed: `test_a_live_agentic_review_renders_the_prompt_cache_section`). **Reversibility.** Set `ATLAS_PROVIDERS` to a tag with a ZDR row for the model at hand and the pin moves; the previous four-slug list restores the old Sonnet behavior but is not a working Haiku rollback (it refuses Haiku against the current list), so a Haiku rollback names a tag with a Haiku row, such as `google-vertex/europe`. Unset `ATLAS_PROVIDERS` and the single-endpoint default stands. Nothing is migrated. **Revisit** after the first live Sonnet review on the pinned endpoint: the run summary should show the total cache writes close to the total final context and near-zero misses; a clear refusal because the pinned endpoint left the ZDR list is fixed by pinning another ZDR row explicitly, not by widening the default.

- **D64 — A rejected quote earns a bounded repair turn in the same session, and the checker searches a ±3-line window whose span the quote's line count sets (fixes #697; amends D40's span rule and D47's drift search) (2026-11-15).** *(agent, 2026-11-15, from the Nov 14 cost breakdown's fix 3 and that day's model trials on PRs #661 and #640: all 7 Sonnet retries — 5 on #661, 2 on #640 — were `QUOTES_FAILED`, one failed quote re-ran the whole lens from turn 1, and the retries cost $2.00 and $0.69 as logged. The per-review saving below is the issue's own estimate, repriced from the logged calls, not a measurement this change made.)* **The defect.** Every failed quote was text that really is in the head file: 2 were one line off, 2 were span mismatches (the checker required the cited span to equal the quote's line count), and 3 were 9–62 lines off. The first two classes are citation errors the model can fix where it sits; the third stays unverified — a window wide enough to reach 9 lines would let a quote verify far from anything it cites. **1. The quote-repair turn.** When a final reply the harness has otherwise accepted carries a finding whose quote did not verify, the loop sends one more turn in the same conversation, bounded and configurable: `quote_repairs` repair turns per attempt, default 1, an integer from 0 to 3, over `ATLAS_QUOTE_REPAIRS`. The turn names each rejected finding's location, the checker's reason and the rejected quote text (all model-authored, so fenced as untrusted data with the `failed-quotes` label, exactly as the unread nudge fences PR-chosen paths), and instructs the model to give the line range where that text really sits or drop the finding; it may read the head file again. The reply that answers it is the one that counts, and its findings are re-checked by the same checker. The repair never loops: when the bound is spent and quotes still fail, the attempt keeps `QUOTES_FAILED` exactly as before — partial, retryable, so the attempt-level retry (D61) remains the fallback — and the outcome's detail names that the bound was reached. A stop before the repair turn is placed keeps the rejected reply's own judgment, as D58's review M1 holds for every queued turn. **2. The window.** The checker's drift search is generalized: a quote of whole lines whose text is at least 8 non-space characters may now match a window of the quote's own line count starting within `quote_tolerance` lines of the citation's first line — default 3, an integer from 0 to 8, over `ATLAS_QUOTE_TOLERANCE` — instead of D47's same-span, ±2 search. This amends D40's span rule (the cited span equaling the quote's line count still decides the exact match at the citation; inside the window, the quote's real line count sets the searched span, so a 3-line quote cited as 4 lines, or a 4-line quote cited at one line, verifies where its lines actually sit) and D47 (the drift window widens from 2 to 3 and no longer requires the cited span to equal the quote's length; a fragment still never drifts, a removed-line match is still checked before a drifted one, and a match whose start is the citation's first line is still tagged drifted, not head-exact). The verified finding's location becomes the matched lines, so the posted citation points at the lines that hold the quote; nearest match wins (deltas tried 0, +1, −1, +2, −2, +3, −3); nothing outside the window counts as verified, and a window of 0 keeps only the span correction at the citation's own start. **The merge.** A repair or a window correction keeps the attempt's other verified findings: the reply that answers a quote repair merges the held reply's verified findings in through D61's own `_merged_findings` — a verified finding the repaired reply restates stays the repaired reply's copy, one it could not verify replaces the repaired reply's unverified copy at the same citation, one it does not restate is appended — so #694's merge is reused, not duplicated. **Telemetry.** The attempt's ledger line and the completion span record the repair turn (the `nudges` field and `atlas.nudge` carry `quote-repair`, as they already do for `unread-files`, `no-progress` and `duplicate-calls`), the bound hit is named in the lens line's detail, the draft's lens line counts the repair turns and the citations the window corrected, and each corrected finding carries the source tag built from its own distance (`drift_label`: "head within N line(s) of the citation", or "head at the citation's first line, span set by the quote" at distance 0). **Tests.** Each change names a test that fails when it is reverted: the window (`test_a_whole_line_quote_within_the_window_is_verified_as_drifted`, `test_a_whole_line_quote_exactly_three_lines_off_is_the_window_edge`, `test_a_span_mismatch_lets_the_quote_set_the_span`, `test_a_multi_line_quote_cited_over_a_wider_span_is_corrected`, `test_the_window_is_configurable`, `test_the_window_zero_still_corrects_a_span_mismatch`, `test_a_one_line_quote_cited_across_the_file_verifies_when_it_starts_the_range`, the fragment and far-line refusals), the repair turn (`test_a_failed_quote_earns_one_repair_turn_and_the_next_reply_counts`, `test_a_repair_that_drops_the_finding_keeps_the_other_verified_ones`, `test_a_repair_that_still_fails_keeps_the_quote_failure_and_names_the_bound`, `test_the_repair_bound_is_configurable_and_zero_disables_the_turn`, `test_a_quote_repair_turn_that_never_runs_keeps_the_rejected_reply`, `test_the_configured_window_reaches_the_quote_checker`), the limits (`test_the_quote_repair_bound_and_window_are_configurable_and_bounded`), and the draft (`test_a_window_correction_is_counted_in_the_lens_line`, the end-to-end repair in `test_a_failed_quote_is_repaired_in_the_session_and_the_lens_settles_clean`). **Mutants.** Each behavior was checked against a mutant the tests kill: the window bound removed (searching the whole file) dies on the three-lines-off edge and far-line refusals; dropping the −1 direction dies on the drifted-window set; keeping D40's span equality inside the window dies on the span-mismatch tests; returning the cited location instead of the matched one dies on `test_a_drifted_quote_takes_the_location_of_the_matched_lines`; letting fragments drift dies on the short-fragment and minimum-length tests; ignoring the repair bound dies on the bound tests; skipping the merge dies on the drop-the-finding test; never sending the repair turn dies on `test_a_failed_quote_earns_one_repair_turn_and_the_next_reply_counts`; dropping the bound detail dies on the still-fails test; not recording the nudge dies on the `nudges` assertion; and not passing the configured window to the checker dies on `test_the_configured_window_reaches_the_quote_checker`. **Cost.** No live call is made by the change. The issue estimates about $0.64 per Sonnet review after the caching fixes, and cuts retries for GLM where retries are 25% of spend; a repair turn is an ordinary reserved, counted call under the existing turn, call, dollar and clock limits. **Reversibility.** Set `ATLAS_QUOTE_REPAIRS=0` to disable the repair turn and `ATLAS_QUOTE_TOLERANCE=0` to keep only the span correction at the citation itself; both restore the pre-D64 refusal behavior for offset quotes. Stored format: the ledger's new `nudges` label is a string value, `QuoteSource.HEAD_NEAR`'s value is now "head near the citation" (the posted tag comes from `drift_label`), and a checked finding gains a `drift` field that `_checked_from` defaults to 0, so a ledger line written before D64 reads back. **Revisit** after the next live trial: if repaired quotes still fail, the repair message's contents are the first suspect; if the window's corrections disagree with the model's own second answer, the window is the tuning input, not a fixed constant. **Update (2026-11-15, round-1 review of #703).** Four failure paths and four small ones, all fixed offline. (a) A reply that answers the quote repair and never attests — a second contract refusal, or one naming another lens — no longer becomes the attempt's recorded outcome: the held reply's attested judgment is what the attempt records, with the failed reply's causes and detail folded on, so its verified findings report, block approval, and carry to the attempt-level retry under D61's attested-only rule. (The traced defect: a held reply verified one finding, the repair reply and its contract repair were both prose, an attested empty retry settled the lens clean with nothing to report, and the review approved.) (b) A contract repair queued after a quote repair no longer overwrites the held judgment with the unparsed one — the hold stays the quote hold's, with `UNPARSED` and the validator's message folded in — so a stop before the contract repair is sent returns the held findings, not an empty hold. (c) The repair turn is counted, in `nudges`, on the completion span and in the draft's lens line, only when its call is actually sent; a turn cap, budget or clock stop in the gap leaves it uncounted. The unread and no-progress labels keep D58's queue-time recording, which their own tests pin. (d) The posted source tag is built from the match's own distance — `drift_label`: "head within N line(s) of the citation", or "head at the citation's first line, span set by the quote" at distance 0 — not from the configured window, so it stays true for every setting; `Verified` and `CheckedFinding` carry the distance, the ledger round-trips it, and `QuoteSource.HEAD_NEAR` no longer hardcodes the window's number in its value. (e) `_check_quote_limits` rejects a non-int — a float passes `in range(...)` and would have broken the window search mid-review — and both bound layers are pinned by their own messages (the env parser names the raw value, `resolve` does not). (f) The repair message marks a quote cut at 120 characters with " … [cut]". (g) The equal-distance tie-break (positive before negative), removed-lines-wins over an in-window head match, and the `resolve()`-layer rejection of `quote_repairs=4` are pinned by tests; the three mutants that survived round 1 now die. New tests: `test_an_unattested_repair_reply_keeps_the_verified_findings_on_the_retry`, `test_a_repair_reply_that_names_another_lens_keeps_the_verified_findings`, `test_a_contract_repair_after_the_quote_repair_keeps_the_held_findings`, `test_a_repair_turn_queued_but_never_sent_is_not_counted`, `test_the_drift_is_the_distance_the_match_moved`, `test_a_match_at_the_configured_window_is_tagged_with_its_distance`, `test_the_nearest_match_wins_and_the_positive_direction_breaks_a_tie`, `test_a_removed_line_match_wins_over_a_head_line_within_the_window`, `test_the_repair_message_marks_a_cut_quote`, and the float and layer rows in `test_the_quote_repair_bound_and_window_are_configurable_and_bounded`. **Update (2026-11-15, round-2 review of #703).** A repair reply that never attests because it names another lens folds its attestation's fixed controller text — "the reply named another lens or body" — as the detail: a misacknowledged outcome has no causes and no detail of its own, so without it the recorded attempt read exactly like a repair that attested and still failed its quote, and the draft could not tell a confused or steered model from a wrong citation. The attempt's unread list now flows through one `_Progress.unread()` accessor, the spot #704's shared-prefix bookkeeping will land on; it takes no different value today.

- **D65 — The review's lenses share one cached prefix: the tools, the common contract and the fenced whole diff, ahead of the lens skill (2026-11-15).** *(agent, 2026-11-15, from issue #696 and the 2026-11-14 model trials it cites: on PRs #661 and #640 every Sonnet lens session read every changed file's diff through `diff_file` — 32k and 44k tokens, 60–65% of a lens's final context — and no two lenses could share a cached block, because the lens-specific system prompt came first and only the automatic top-level `cache_control` was sent; every turn 1 reported `cached_tokens: 0`)* Offline only: the tests use recorded HTTP, fakes and the shipped fixture, and this change makes no live model call and reads no key. **Choice.** When the diff fits, each lens request is laid out so a byte-identical block comes first and carries its own cache breakpoint: the tools, a shared system text (the prefix instructions, the review's one fence nonce, the Finding contract) and the fenced whole diff of the files the lens was given; the lens skill (the retry preamble, then the attested body) and the turn-1 message follow inside the same user message, so the first session writes the block and the rest read it at the cache-read price. The fence nonce is minted once per review (`secrets.token_hex(8)`), which is what makes the prefixes byte-identical across lenses and across a lens's retry; the nonce never survives inside fenced content (`prompt.fence` scrubs it), so diff text cannot close the shared block early or forge controller fences, and the nonce is not derived from PR content. **Grounding.** OpenRouter's prompt-caching page (fetched 2026-11-15) documents explicit per-block `cache_control` breakpoints on Chat Completions content parts as working "across all Anthropic-compatible providers including Bedrock and Vertex", with a limit of four explicit breakpoints (this change sends one), and documents the top-level automatic mode as applying its breakpoint to the last cacheable block and advancing it as the conversation grows — the automatic top-level field stays on every request (D57), so a prefix turn's tail still caches as it did; the combination of the two on one request was not probed against a live endpoint, and the page does not forbid it (verify). The same page's sticky-routing section says OpenRouter hashes the first system message and the first non-system message into the conversation's sticky key; the first system message is shared, but the first non-system message is not — it carries the lens skill and the turn-1 text after the diff — so the routing hash differs per lens, and no `session_id` is sent: the locality comes from D63's single-endpoint pin, and the cache sharing from the explicit breakpoint over the byte-identical first part, which is what the first-turn cache reads will confirm. **Threshold.** `diff_prefix_share` (default 0.5, `ATLAS_DIFF_PREFIX_SHARE` / `--diff-prefix-share`) is the share of the model's resolved per-call input cap — its context minus the output reserve — the shared block's bytes may take; the complement is the lens's work. Bytes against a token budget is the conservative direction (a token never encodes less than one byte), so a block that passes is one the model can hold, and the per-call token check still prices every turn. A block that does not fit, a model that cannot name an input room, a scope any of whose paths has no section in the diff — the empty scope is that case taken whole — and share 0 all page through `diff_file` exactly as before, and each names its reason in the attempt's `diff_prefix` note, which the ledger records and the draft's lens line shows; the paging keeps the paged path's honest coverage, so a path the fence does not hold is never attested as read. **Coverage.** On the prefix path the whole scope diff was delivered in turn 1, so coverage is attested from what the prefix held: a lens that calls no tool is clean, `FILES_UNREAD` cannot fire, and the unread-list nudge and the `diff_file` page bookkeeping are retired there. On the paged path everything is exactly as before: the page bookkeeping, the one unread-list nudge (D58), the honest coverage rule and the parity gate are unchanged — a lens never attests coverage it did not get. The prefix path and the paged path keep separate instruction texts composed from shared parts (the opening, the fence paragraph and the reply contract are one source), so the two cannot drift on the sentences they share. **First wave.** No warming: the lenses fan out at once, and each may write the prefix — measured, at the trials' prices, at about $0.24–$0.33 per review — the issue's own "about $0.30 less saving" estimate — because the `agentic-review` command defaults its pool to 4 (`AGENTIC_POOL` in `cli.py`, not the `AgenticLimits` default of 1), so a flagless first wave starts its lenses together and up to `min(pool, lenses) − 1` of them write the prefix at the $2.50/M write price; a pool of 1 serializes the lenses, so lens *n* starts after lens *n−1* finished and reads the block warm, and a raised pool costs more writes, not less thoroughness. Warming would serialize the first lens ahead of every other lens and spend review wall clock on ordering the pool does not need; the run summary's per-session first-turn cache reads show which sessions warmed and which wrote, so an operator who prefers warming has the number to justify it. Concurrency stays as configured. **Diagnostic.** `cache_stats` gains each session's opening-turn cache read (`prefix_read_tokens`): nothing else in a session's first prompt can have been cached, so on the prefix path that count is the shared prefix's read for the session, and the run total sums them — the confirmation the issue asks for, that turn 1 of every lens after the first shows the shared prefix as cached tokens, reads straight off the draft. **Constraints kept.** `data_collection: deny` and D63's endpoint pin are untouched; the prefix carries no new endpoint setting; nothing is sent to Safety Retention, Gemini, or any endpoint that trains on data. The dry run keeps the paged path (its scripted reviewer names no input room), which is also what keeps the `gap` scenario's coverage gap reachable. **Tests.** Each behavior names a test that fails when the behavior is reverted: the prefix layout and its attachment to every turn (`test_the_prefix_request_carries_the_shared_prefix_and_the_skill_after_it`), byte identity across lenses and a retry over serialized request bytes, not structure (mutation-confirmed, mutant M1: `test_the_shared_prefix_is_byte_identical_across_lenses_and_a_retry`), the explicit breakpoint on the diff part with the automatic top-level field kept (mutant M2: `test_the_explicit_breakpoint_sits_on_the_diff_part_of_the_prefix`), the no-marker layout on a route whose endpoints do not list `cache_control` (mutant M3: `test_a_route_without_cache_control_support_gets_no_marker`), the threshold paging and naming its reason (mutant P6: `test_a_diff_over_the_prefix_budget_pages_with_the_paged_rules`), a model that names no room (mutant P7: `test_a_model_that_names_no_room_pages_and_says_so`), share 0 (`test_a_zero_share_turns_the_prefix_off_and_says_nothing`), a scope whose diff has no section (`test_a_job_whose_diff_holds_no_scope_section_pages_and_says_so`), prefix-path coverage from the prefix alone (mutant P4: `test_the_prefix_path_attests_coverage_from_what_the_prefix_held`, and end to end through the parity gate: `test_a_prefix_review_is_clean_and_complete_with_no_tool_calls`), the unread nudge retired on the prefix path and standing on the paged path
