(`test_the_prefix_path_never_sends_the_unread_files_nudge`, killed by mutant P4 with the coverage test), the prefix fencing exactly the scope it attests (mutant P10: `test_the_prefix_fences_exactly_the_scope_it_attests`), the stall nudge surviving on the prefix path (mutant P8: `test_the_prefix_path_still_nudges_a_stall`), the fencing guarantees over a poisoned diff (mutant P9: `test_diff_content_cannot_close_its_fence_or_pose_as_instructions`), the one CSPRNG mint per review (mutant N1: `test_one_nonce_is_minted_per_review_and_every_lens_fences_with_it`), the shared block byte-identical across two live lenses end to end (`test_both_lenses_share_one_byte_identical_prefix`), the shared instruction sentences agreeing across both layouts (`test_the_prefix_instructions_and_the_paged_ones_agree_where_they_share`), the limit's parsing and bounds (mutant S1: `test_the_diff_prefix_share_is_configurable_and_zero_turns_it_off`, the CLI's `test_agentic_review_validates_the_diff_prefix_share_flag`), the ledger round-trip of the prefix note (`test_the_diff_prefix_note_round_trips_through_the_ledger`), and the per-session prefix read in the summary (mutant C1: `test_a_session_that_read_a_prefix_shows_its_first_turn_cache_read`), the turn-cap stop on the prefix path reporting no unread files (mutant P12: `test_a_prefix_path_turn_cap_reports_no_unread_files`), the threshold's edge — equal inlines, one byte over pages (mutant P13: `test_the_prefix_budget_boundary_inlines_at_equal_and_pages_one_byte_over`) — and the block's byte count including the diff, so a diff that alone pushes over the budget pages (mutant P14: `test_a_diff_alone_over_the_budget_pages`), byte identity carrying the real tools, not empty lists (mutant P15, an assertion in `test_the_shared_prefix_is_byte_identical_across_lenses_and_a_retry`), the nonce's literal 8-byte width (mutant P16: `test_the_fence_nonce_is_eight_bytes`), the prefix path's no-progress window starting at turn 0 (mutant P17: `test_the_prefix_path_no_progress_window_starts_at_turn_zero`), and a scope mixing a real section with a path the diff has no section for, which pages and is never attested (mutant P18: `test_a_scope_with_a_path_without_a_section_pages_and_is_not_attested`). Mutants left equivalent on purpose, and said so: the `if not p.prefixed` second guards on the page bookkeeping and on the unread nudge, and `_inject_stall_nudge`'s accessor bypass — `covered_at_turn` is already 0 on the prefix path, so the bypassed branch cannot run. **Reversibility.** `ATLAS_DIFF_PREFIX_SHARE=0` restores the paged review exactly: layout, coverage, nudge and bookkeeping all take the pre-D65 path when the share is 0, and the note field is empty there. No ledger, fixture or deployed setting is migrated. **Revisit** after the first live Sonnet review with the prefix: the draft's first-turn cache reads should show every session after the first in run order reading a block near the prefix's size — the draft orders the sessions by lens label, so "after the first" is run order, not the draft's order; a run whose reads sit far below it, or whose writes did not drop by roughly (lenses − 1) × the diff, is evidence the endpoint ignored the breakpoint or the route spread, and the D63 revisit's endpoint diagnostics apply. **Correction (2026-11-15, round-1 review of #707).** The "mutants left equivalent on purpose" sentence's `_inject_stall_nudge` accessor-bypass clause and this entry's P17 test reference describe code D66 removed — `_Progress.covered_at_turn` no longer exists and the no-progress window is no longer seeded per path — so both are superseded by D66, whose shared-window tests replace P17's; see D66 before reading either here.

- **D66 — The no-progress window counts consecutive turns that brought the lens nothing new, by the same rule on both paths (fixes #705; amends D58's detector item 11 and D65's prefix-path seeding) (2026-11-15).** *(agent, 2026-11-15, from the Nov 15 Sonnet measurement on #640 at main `616d980c` that Brandon approved this fix on: 5 of 12 lenses — tracing, silent-failures, resilience, observability and test-quality — got the "finalize now" nudge at about turn 6, where none did on the Nov 14 baseline; lens sessions averaged about 4 turns against about 13; verified findings fell to 10 (8 Minor, 2 Nit) from 20 with one Major, and the Major's lens was not picked that run, so the cap's share of the drop is not isolated; total cost was $1.70 against $6.87, most of that the caching work's, but some possibly the shortened exploration. Re-measuring against both runs is done outside the repo after merge.)* Offline only: the tests use fakes and the shipped fixture; no live model call, network call or secret is involved, and no deployed service setting changes. **The defect.** D65 seeded `_Progress.covered_at_turn` at 0 on the shared-prefix path (pinned by mutant P17), so D58's window counted from the lens's first turn: a lens whose diff arrived inline got about 6 turns of exploration in total, where the paged path gave it 6 turns *after* finishing its diff reads — and on neither path did the window reset when the lens was in fact still making progress. **Progress, defined once for both paths.** A turn brings new content when one of its bridge calls succeeded with a (tool, validated-args) pair the attempt has not successfully run before — a new file, a new diff page, a new search, a new HEAD read; an empty result still counts, because it teaches the lens that the search came up empty — or when a judged final reply carried a verified finding the attempt has not recorded before (the finding's `repr` is the signature, so a repeated finding is not progress). A failed call returns nothing and is never progress. Every other turn is a stalled turn — a tool turn whose calls returned nothing new, and a nonterminal final reply that queues a follow-up (the contract repair, the unread-list nudge, a quote repair) without a new verified finding, checked before the follow-up is queued so its nudge and the follow-up compose in one conversation the way the nudge composes after a tool turn's results — and `no_progress_turns` consecutive stalled turns inject the one finalize nudge — the same count, the same one-nudge-per-attempt rule, the same trigger precedence as D58 gave the detector. A transport-timed-out turn is not counted — the loop continues before any turn or stall accounting — and its one retry counts once when it lands (D58's retry rule, unchanged). **What stays.** The duplicate-call trigger (`duplicate_calls` identical calls, at any coverage) is still checked first and is unchanged; the max-turn, max-call, dollar and wall-clock guards are unchanged; the limit stays configurable (`ATLAS_NO_PROGRESS_TURNS`) and is still refused when cleared to unset or set to a non-positive value; the ledger's `nudges` labels and the `atlas.nudge` span attribute keep D58's closed vocabulary (`unread-files`, `no-progress`, `duplicate-calls`, `quote-repair`). **What the nudge now says.** The injected text names why the trigger fired and the turns it counted — for the window, "No turn since turn *N* has brought new content or a new finding: *k* turns without progress, as of turn *M*" (or "since the attempt began" when nothing has progressed yet); for `duplicate-calls`, the repeated call's count and the turn it reached it on — so the conversation, the spans and the ledger show which lenses hit the limit and on which turn. **Per Brandon's standing rule** the limit still guards runaway loops and costly failures only; it is never sized to make reviews cheaper than thoroughness needs, and this change resizes no default. What it removes is the two paths' unequal starts: the paged path's head start (the window began only at full diff coverage) and the prefix path's penalty (the window began at turn 0) are both gone — neither path seeds the window, and only progress resets it, so a lens that reads something new every turn is never nudged by this limit on either path, and a lens that stalls is nudged at the same count as before. `_Progress.covered_at_turn` is deleted; D65's "mutants left equivalent on purpose" sentence about `_inject_stall_nudge`'s accessor bypass describes code that no longer exists. **Tests.** Each behavior names a test that fails when it is reverted, with its mutant: steady progress is never nudged, twelve turns of new reads on the prefix path (mutant NP1: `test_an_inline_diff_lens_still_making_new_reads_is_not_nudged`); a stalling lens is nudged on exactly the `no_progress_turns`th stalled turn, with the nudge naming the turns (mutant NP2: `test_a_stalling_lens_is_nudged_after_exactly_no_progress_turns`, prefix path — P17's replacement, since the window is no longer seeded per path); the same on the paged path, where the old window waited for full coverage before counting and this lens's pre-coverage fumbling was never nudged by it (mutant NP3: `test_the_paged_path_nudges_a_stall_after_exactly_no_progress_turns`); new progress resetting the count mid-attempt, so the nudge moves to the second stalled turn after the reset (mutant NP4: `test_new_progress_resets_the_no_progress_count`); a new verified finding resetting it across a quote-repair turn (mutant NP5: `test_a_new_verified_finding_resets_the_no_progress_count`); the configured window's boundary (`test_no_progress_is_nudged_after_the_configured_turns`, and one under it: `test_five_stalled_turns_are_not_nudged`, renamed from `test_five_turns_past_coverage_are_not_nudged`); and the duplicate trigger unchanged (`test_three_identical_calls_are_nudged_even_before_coverage`, `test_two_identical_calls_earn_the_unread_nudge_instead`, and the prefix path's `test_the_prefix_path_still_nudges_a_stall`). A stalled turn in the round's first tests is a turn that calls nothing (`NOTHING`, a `ToolCalls` with no calls); the round-1 review of this PR found the production stall shapes untested — five predicate mutants survived the whole suite — so the stall tests now also run the shapes the live adapter produces: a turn of rejected calls, an empty `grep` pattern (mutant M2: `test_a_rejected_call_is_not_progress`), a turn repeating one successful call with `duplicate_calls` raised above the window to isolate the no-progress trigger (mutants M1 and M8: `test_a_repeated_successful_call_is_not_progress`), a judged reply whose every finding failed the quote check (mutant M5: `test_an_unverified_finding_is_not_progress`), the attempt scope of `seen_calls` across an intervening call (mutant M8 again: `test_seen_calls_reach_across_turns`), and the co-fire where the duplicate trigger and the window both hold on one turn (mutant M9: `test_the_duplicate_trigger_wins_when_both_hold`); a nonterminal final reply that queues a follow-up with no new verified finding is counted and checked like a stalled tool turn (round-1 Minor 1: `test_a_nonterminal_final_reply_counts_as_a_stalled_turn`); and the `atlas.nudge` span naming is pinned end to end through the production trace wrapper on a nudged attempt (round-2 review, mutant R5 — dropping the loop's `note_nudge` from the detector had passed the whole suite: `test_a_nudged_attempt_names_the_nudge_on_the_completion_span`). Each mutant was applied by hand against its test and killed. **Reversibility.** The old window is one line away — counting `p.turns - p.covered_at_turn` against the limit, seeded per path — but nothing restores it by configuration; the behavior, not a setting, is what changed, and no ledger, fixture or deployed setting is migrated (the `nudges` vocabulary and the limit's parsing are untouched). **Revisit** after the next live Sonnet review: if a lens now runs long and ends at a cap instead of the nudge, the stall accounting missed a progress signal the lens counted on — argument variation is the first suspect (ever-increasing `diff_file` offsets, near-identical `grep` patterns, a path re-read under a different spelling that still validates), bounded by the unchanged caps (`max_calls` 50, `max_turns` 50, the $5 reservation, the wall clocks) — and the nudge text and the lens lines will show where the turns went; if lenses still finish at about 4 turns with fewer findings, the cap was not the only cause of the Nov 15 drop, and the measurement stands for the other causes.

- **D67 — The agentic review passes the PR conversation and the round log into every lens, and the picker gets the PR body (fixes #709; wires D52 into the `agentic-review` CLI) (2026-11-15).** *(agent, 2026-11-15, from the lens-budget plan's S1; #717 keeps D68 and is the collision this slice plans around)* Offline only: the tests use the shipped fixture, the scripted reviewer and fakes; no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, and no deployed service setting changed. **Choice.** `_cmd_agentic_review` now builds the same `Conversation` the one-shot `review` command builds — `parse_pull`, the PR's commits through the new `gather.pr_commits` (the CI gatherer's own `_pr_commits`, unchanged for it), the remarks through `fetch_remarks`, `build_conversation` under `ConversationBudget` — and passes `conversation.review_context` into `review_lenses` as `context=` with the assembly's omissions as `context_omissions=`; a pull whose fields do not parse yields no block and one omission, not a crash. It also passes a `RoundLog` at `<state dir>/rounds.jsonl` — the caller-chosen path D52 left open; the webhook queue will choose its own — so the review reads the prior rounds into turn 1 and appends its own record. The picker's `pull-request` block is the new `conversation.picker_block` — title and body under D38's 16,384-character body cap, redacted, fenced, never instructions; the title-only agentic picker is gone, and the one-shot path's `_pull_block` now calls the same helper, so the two pickers' views cannot drift. **Input room (the part D52 left to the caller).** `review_lens` has accepted `context=` and `round_log=` since D52; what was missing is sizing them to the model. The model's resolved per-call input room (`prefix_room_tokens`, the same figure D65's share budgets against) minus the share the shared diff prefix may take (D65), minus the largest lens body the plan loaded, minus the turn-1 message's own framing measured from the diff (`first_turn` with no context blocks: the subject, the fence markers and the changed-file listing), minus an 8,192-character reserve (`cli.CONTEXT_RESERVE_CHARS`) for the controller text the measurement cannot see — the instruction text, the reply contract, the tool schemas, the nudge and repair texts, measured at under 6 KiB on main — is the characters the context blocks may take. Bytes against a token room is the conservative direction (D65). A model that names no room — the dry run's scripted reviewer never does — keeps D52's fixed caps and the per-call token check stays the only bound, exactly as before. Under a fit, the conversation block is rebuilt smaller until its UTF-8 bytes fit (the same recorded-omission trail a too-long conversation leaves under D38), the prior rounds take what is left inside D52's 16,384, and a block that still does not fit is left out whole and named — never silently truncated, never sent to overflow the per-call check. The conversation keeps its D38 budget before the prior rounds take the remainder. A partial context cannot approve and the review stays COMMENT until the budget holds the blocks or the omissions are empty — D52's rule, unchanged, as is the parity gate: a missing lens, missing coverage or a missed protocol finding fails; extra findings are fine. **Options.** Fitting inside the coordinator's `_review_context` would have put the room where the blocks are assembled, but it would have put this slice's diff inside the files #717 is editing; a fixed byte cap alone would overflow small-room models, whose turn 1 the per-call check then refuses — the lens fails whole instead of running on a recorded-partial context. **Cost.** Per review, the reads the one-shot path already makes: one more commits listing and the three remark listings; no new CLI flag, and no budget number changed. **Reversibility.** The wiring passes only what `review_lens` already accepted; drop the arguments and the first turn is exactly the pre-D67 one. The round log stays an append-only JSONL file the caller chose; deleting it loses only prior-round context. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_agentic_context.py`, each behavior a test that fails when reverted, each mutant applied by hand and killed): the conversation block reaches every lens's fenced first turn (`test_the_first_turn_carries_the_conversation_block_to_every_lens`, kills mutant M1, dropping `context=`); the picker sees the body inside the fence and never as instructions (`test_the_picker_receives_the_pr_body_not_only_the_title`, kills M2, the title-only picker, and M3, the body spliced into the trusted instructions); a conversation the room cannot hold next to the prefix share and the lens body is omitted whole and named (`test_a_conversation_bigger_than_the_room_is_omitted_whole_by_name`, kills M4, sizing against the room without the prefix share, and M6, building the block and slicing it silently); the round log reaches the next round's first turn and the review appends its record (`test_the_round_log_reaches_the_next_rounds_first_turn`, kills M5, `round_log=None`); prior rounds that no longer fit are omitted by name while the record still appends (`test_prior_rounds_that_no_longer_fit_the_room_are_omitted_by_name`); and a conversation over D38's budget shrinks with recorded omissions and still reaches every lens (`test_a_conversation_over_its_d38_budget_shrinks_with_recorded_omissions`). The live confirmation that #661 m1 is reachable stays with S0 (#708), not this PR. **Correction (2026-11-15, round-1 review of #718).** Four claims above were corrected or completed by the round-1 fixes. (1) The omits-whole mechanism is the fit < 1 gate, not the refit loop the Choice paragraph described: the loop it described was bounded at four rebuilds, and its exhaustion branch was unreachable -- `build_conversation` caps its text at the attempt's character count and `_fit`'s final fallback truncates the fixed part from its start, which begins in ASCII, so the smallest build's bytes never exceeded a room of fit >= 1. The loop is gone: the block is first built at the room's own size (`min(budget.total_chars, fit)`), and when its UTF-8 bytes still overflow -- UTF-8 encodes one to four bytes per character, so a character count that fits the room can overflow it in bytes -- one rebuild at `fit // 4` follows, whose byte bound holds by construction (4 * (fit // 4) <= fit), with no branch after it. Killed there: N7 (first build sized to D38's cap alone, `test_a_room_that_fits_a_shrunken_conversation_sizes_the_first_build_to_it`), M7 (the multi-byte rebuild skipped, `test_multi_byte_conversation_content_is_rebuilt_to_its_byte_budget`, a direct unit call at fit 56,000 over thirty multi-byte remarks), and the review's M6' (a silent slice inside the old loop, unreachable there, unreachable now by the same construction). (2) The unparseable-pull omission is pinned: a fixture whose pull carries no `head.ref` (the reachable unparseable shape -- a pull with no head SHA is refused by `_require_head` before the assembly runs) leaves exactly one `pull request fields` omission, keeps the review COMMENT, and the picker falls back to its title-only view (`test_an_unparseable_pull_is_one_named_omission_and_the_picker_falls_back`, kills N1, the deleted guard that survived the whole suite). (3) The same-assembly claim is now exact about two differences the first wording papered over: the remarks' fetch errors are quoted with the one-shot's scrubber -- the redactor augmented with `scan_diff`'s flagged secret values, flagged before anything is quoted, as the one-shot orders it -- but the one-shot's controller-notes channel (the quoted fetch error as a draft note) has no agentic equivalent; the omission still carries the fact of every cut, and the note's loss is recorded here rather than fixed by growing `AgenticReview` inside #717's collision surface. (4) m-1: the README's two `no shipped caller builds the path yet` sentences for `rounds.py` are fixed in this PR (the module row and the prompt paragraph's sentence); `docs/agentic-reviewer.md`'s matching sentence is the follow-up for whichever PR merges second, because that file is in #717's edit set. The room accessor is now public (`lens_loop.input_room_tokens`), replacing the private `_prefix_room` the first wording reached into. **Correction (2026-11-15, round-2 review of #718).** The round-1 Correction above is wrong on two points, and the sizing it describes crashed and overflowed in a band no test covered; all fixed on the PR branch. (1) The unreachability claim was false: the deleted loop's exhaustion branch WAS reachable for fit 1 through 39, because a truncated build appends `redact.TRUNCATION_MARKER` (39 characters) after its cap -- the smallest build was 40 characters, not 1 -- so the old code omitted whole in that band, which was the correct behavior; this Correction's predecessor argued from a marker-free model of `_truncate` and produced a rebuild at `fit // 4` that asked `build_conversation` for a zero-character budget at fit 1 to 3 (a `ValueError` no CLI handler catches) and shipped blocks up to 39 bytes over the room at fit 4 to 42. (2) The by-construction bound was false as written for the same reason: a truncated build's bytes are at most four per content character PLUS the marker's 39, so `4 * (fit // 4) <= fit` is not the bound. The sizing is now marker-aware: the omit-whole gate fires below one worst-case character plus the marker (`fit < 4 + 39 = 43` characters -- a room that small cannot hold any block next to the shared prefix and the lens body); the first build runs at `fit - 39` characters, so an ASCII build truncated to the room ships at exactly the room's bytes in one build; and a multi-byte build that still overflows is rebuilt once at `(fit - 39) // 4`, whose bound holds by construction (`4 * ((fit - 39) // 4) + 39 <= fit`). The band is pinned by a small-fit unit sweep over fit 1 to 60 in both byte widths (ASCII and four-byte content), asserting no exception and either an omitted-whole omission or block bytes within the room (`test_the_small_fit_band_never_crashes_and_never_ships_oversized_block` shape: `test_the_small_fit_band_never_crashes_and_never_ships_an_oversized_block`), the multi-byte rebuild test now runs both a three-byte and a four-byte variant, and a marker test pins the first build's marker subtraction (`test_the_first_build_leaves_room_for_the_truncation_marker`). Killed there: the round-2 scheme as written (gate at fit < 1, rebuild at `fit // 4` -- crashes at fit 1 to 3), M8 (the same scheme with only the `max(1, fit // 4)` clamp -- ships a 40-byte block at fit 1), the skipped-marker first build (builds at `fit` and burns the rebuild), and a gate at 39 instead of 43. **Correction (2026-11-15, the merge follow-up this entry records for #717, the PR that merges second).** (1) The four-byte rebuild test's kill claim for the marker-blind `fit // 4` rebuild is withdrawn: that mutant is equivalent-safe, not killed -- by the ASCII-prefix argument, the assembled conversation block's leading framing is ASCII, so a build's bytes sit below the four-bytes-per-character bound by more than the few extra characters the marker-blind size can add, and the mutant ships a block that fits wherever the marker-aware rebuild's does; run on the merged tree, the whole of `test_agentic_context.py` passes with the mutant in place. (2) The round-2 oversize band the ASCII variant exercised ran to fit 50, not the 42 this entry's earlier sentence gives. (3) The 43-character omit-whole gate is a conservative margin, not the minimum: one content character plus the marker needs only `fit >= 40`, so rooms of 40 to 42 characters could hold a one-character block and the gate omits them anyway; `_MIN_FIT_CHARS`'s docstring now states the margin as a margin.
**Update (2026-11-17, issue #728 — the webhook's round-log path chosen).** *(agent, 2026-11-17, on the branch that closes #728; the same offline rules as above)* The path this entry deferred — "the caller-chosen path D52 left open; the webhook queue will choose its own" — now exists: `agentic-review` takes `--round-log`, and the service passes `<state dir>/rounds/<repo>-<pr>.jsonl`, one file per pull request rather than per head, so a later head reads the earlier rounds (D74 records the storage, retention and concurrency decision). The entry's "no new CLI flag" sentence described the #709 wiring, where the state directory was the only path; the unflagged default stays `<state dir>/rounds.jsonl`, so a local run keeps the path it always had.

- **D68 — A multi-lens agentic review derives its review-wide limits from the planned lens count, under one absolute per-review dollar ceiling (amends D55's 'the agentic coordinator does not read REVIEW.md' clause) (2026-11-15).** *(agent, 2026-11-15, issue #710; Brandon settled the ceiling on Nov 15, 2026, from 3:11 PM CT: about $10, configurable per repo, and structured so a later per-run policy can adjust it; Sonnet cost of about $1.70-4.80 per review is accepted until coverage parity)* Offline only: the tests use fakes, the shipped fixture bridge and scripted models, and this change makes no live model call, network call or secret read. **The failure.** The flat review-wide defaults - 50 calls and a $5 reservation shared by every lens, the picker and the continuations (D44) - stopped a 12-lens review near 50 calls with lenses unrun, which is a missing-lens failure, not a cheap one: the parity gate counts a lens that never ran as coverage not complete. **Choice.** `review_lenses` now derives its review-wide limits from the planned lens count (`review_wide.derive_review_wide`), and the per-lens values stay the knobs: `max_calls` is the per-lens call budget (`ATLAS_PER_LENS_CALLS` / `--per-lens-calls`, default 50) times the planned lenses plus the picker's calls; `reservation_usd` is the per-lens dollar budget (`ATLAS_PER_LENS_USD` / `--per-lens-usd`, default $5) times the planned lenses plus the picker's committed dollars, clamped to the absolute per-review ceiling; and `review_wall_s` is the configured review wall raised to the lens wall times the planned waves (a wave is one pool-full of planned lenses) - raised, never lowered, so no review loses wall clock it ran on before. The per-lens defaults are the 2026-11-11 review-wide defaults re-scoped per lens, so an unconfigured one-lens review keeps the numbers it always had, and a review whose planned lenses fit one wave of the default pool keeps its 60-minute wall. **The ceiling.** The default is $10 (`DEFAULT_REVIEW_CEILING_USD`); `ATLAS_REVIEW_CEILING_USD` or `--review-ceiling-usd` sets it for the deployment, and the reviewed repo's own `REVIEW.md` overrides both with a `review_ceiling_usd:` line read from the **merge-base** tree the review already fetches - the tree the PR branched from, not the base-ref tip (D55's fetch boundary); a PR cannot raise or lower its own ceiling either way, and a ceiling the repo changes on the base tip takes effect for pull requests branched after it. A line that is present but not a positive dollar amount fails open to the default with a note the draft carries, so 'line present but unusable' is distinguishable from 'no line'. Precedence is repo, then configured, then default, resolved by one pure function (`resolve_ceiling`): that function is the seam a later per-run policy adjusts the ceiling through, by computing the value it passes - no policy engine is built here. The ceiling is the runaway guard, not a budget - with the clamp's one qualification (round-1 review of #717, M-1): the reservation bounds worst-case projections, and a pool of concurrent first turns projects several at once, so a clamped reservation can be smaller than one wave's worst-case reservations. It does not stop a lens for that: `Spend.reserve` waits, bounded by the review wall, for an in-flight call to settle -- the wait's real-time backstop arms only when the review clock itself stops moving across wakeups (round-2 review of #717, M-1R), so on a healthy clock the wall is the bound however long a turn takes -- and stops only when the settled spend plus the call's own worst case cannot fit even with nothing in flight, or the wall runs out; spend never passes the ceiling. What the limits never do is size a review cheaper than thoroughness needs. **Overrides.** An operator-set `--max-calls`, `--reservation-usd` or `--review-wall-seconds` keeps the review-wide meaning it always had and wins for that one limit; `agentic_config.load_agentic_limits` records which in the new `AgenticLimits.explicit` field. The ceiling clamps the reservation even when it is explicit, so raising it past the ceiling needs the ceiling raised too; the clamp flag the stop text reads is taken from the value the clamp bit on - the formula's value when the reservation is derived, the configured reservation when it is not - so an explicit reservation under the ceiling never reads as clamped. A derived call budget below the per-lens turn cap is refused before any lens runs. The per-lens-against-review-wide relations (a per-lens limit no greater than its per-review limit) are now checked against the totals the review actually runs on: the derivation re-checks them against the derived values, so a per-lens turn budget or lens wall above the flat review-wide defaults is a valid multi-lens configuration the old configured comparison would have refused, while an explicit review-wide value below its per-lens budget is still refused, and the single-lens `review_lens` path still checks the relations against its configured totals. **Diagnosability.** A stop on a derived limit names the derived values, the absolute ceiling, the observed value and the knob (`lens_loop.Derivation` builds the `LimitHit` texts; the ledger stores the same fields), and the picker's calls no longer eat the lens budget - they are added on top of it, so the flat cap's other failure mode (one busy picker starving the lenses) is gone by construction. The single-lens `review_lens` path and the one-shot `review` command are unchanged; the service switch onto `agentic-review` is #712 and waits on this. **Acceptance.** The derived call budget is review-wide with no per-lens call enforcement: one lens can consume up to `(1 + retries) x max_turns` calls, about twice its per-lens share today and up to about four with #654's D49 retry composed - the x-lenses scaling shrinks the flat cap's starvation mode without eliminating it, consistent with limits guarding runaway rather than thoroughness, and the #654 composition is a conscious acceptance recorded here (round-1 review of #717, n-3). **Scope note.** D66 is #707's (it merged into main while this PR was open; this entry was written as D68 while both were open) and D67 is #709's, so this entry is D68; if D68 is taken by push time, the next free number after the highest on main and in the open PRs applies. **Tests.** The derivation, the clamp, the ceiling precedence, the `REVIEW.md` line's parsing (fail-open to the default on anything unusable) and the two limit stops' texts are pinned in `atlas_reviewer/tests/test_review_wide.py`, including the mutants this kills (dropping either x-lenses factor fails the formula tests); a 12-lens scripted review with 15 turns per lens finishes with no limit stop and complete coverage, and reviews pushed past the derived calls or the ceiling stop with the derivation named. A `REVIEW.md` over the read bound is unparseable rather than parsed cut, so a truncation inside the value cannot read as a number it is not. The clamp's pool edge has its own acceptance variant - 12 lenses at pool 4 with the scripted worst case at the recorded catalog's about-$2.90 - which completes with full coverage, and the revert-to-stop mutant dies on the deterministic fourth-reservation wait pin and on that run; the merge-base read is pinned against a head copy naming a different ceiling; an unusable ceiling line's note is pinned in the draft; the wave count's non-multiple case kills the ceil-division mutant; and the CLI's pre-picker validation pins the EXIT_USAGE exit path. The backstop's arming condition is pinned both ways: a freeing settle arriving after 22 seconds of clock advance (beyond the backstop's reading, shrunk to a quarter second for the run) still reserves, and a wait on a moving clock stops at the wall; the exception-path settles are pinned per exit (a turn stopped at the output cap, a transport timeout and its retry, a transport failure, a model error each settle at their reservation, killing the dropped-settle mutants C4 and C5); the tight `review_ceiling_usd:15` form parses and a no-space unusable value still notes; parked-waiter timing pins cover the halt wake and the halt-shaped outcome. Offline only.
- **D69 — The picker chooses any number of lenses: no fixed cap, hygiene off the slot economy, and a repo preference that overrides the guideline by include and exclude (fixes #711) (2026-11-16).** *(agent, 2026-11-16, issue #711; Brandon settled on Nov 15, 2026, CT: the picker can choose any number of lenses, with no fixed cap like the current 8; the skill bundle provides the guideline — default selection or auto-includes — and repo preferences can override it, for example in REVIEW.md; the $10 review ceiling (D68), not a lens-count cap, is the runaway guard; #717 keeps D68 and #712–#714 keep D70–D72, so this slice takes the reserved D69)* Offline only: the tests use fakes, the shipped `sample-pr` fixture and the real CLI's dry run, with the picker's reply scripted or monkeypatched; no live model call, network call or secret read was made, and no deployed service setting, Fly config value or secret changed. **The failure.** The picker was hard-capped at 8 names (`Depth.REVIEW.limit = MAX_LENSES = 8`, read as the top of review mode's 3-8 breadth) and at 40 one-shot at comprehensive, and the instructions said "at most 8 names … the extra names are dropped" — contradicting the guideline's own "not a strict cap". Hygiene is added ahead of the picks (D58), but the picker was not told it was already in, so it could spend one of the 8 slots naming it; auto-includes depended on the model under that slot economy, which is how the #640 module-design Major went missing. **Choice.** `Depth.limit` is now `int | None`: triage keeps its critical-tier cap — its depth mode, intended — and review and comprehensive return None; `lenses.plan_lenses` treats None as uncapped, so the one-shot 60,000-character body budget, not a lens count, decides what a one-shot review inlines, and an agentic review stays bounded by D68's derived limits under the per-review dollar ceiling. `MAX_LENSES` and `COMPREHENSIVE_LIMIT` are removed, not left dangling. The picker instructions now carry the guideline's breadth as a default, name the auto-includes the guideline says ride along additively (a decision-record or decisions-log change names `reviewing-decision-lifecycle`; a docs-only change names `auditing-documentation-health`; a rename or restructuring names `reviewing-module-design` and `reviewing-naming-and-readability`), and say there is no fixed cap on how many lenses are named. Hygiene stays a guideline include that spends no slot: a pick whose guideline would have included hygiene still yields the full guideline set of non-hygiene picks plus hygiene — the ranked-name dedupe D58 introduced is unchanged, and with no cap nothing displaces anything by count. **The repo preference.** The reviewed repo's merge-base `REVIEW.md` — the same tree D68's ceiling line is read from, so a PR cannot move its own override either — may carry `review_lens_include:` and `review_lens_exclude:` lines, each a comma-separated list of lens-shaped names. The controller applies the override to the picks itself (`picker._apply_preferences`): an exclude drops a ranked pick; an include appends a choice and runs even when the picker fell back or made no call, but never widens triage (a preference re-ranks within its depth mode's breadth and never widens the mode, the guideline's own team-preferences rule). The critical tier is the controller's own floor, not the guideline's, so neither direction touches it. The selection note records the override — both lists and what actually changed ("dropped from the picks: …; added: …") — and the draft carries it; a line present but not a usable lens-name list, a repeated key, or a file over the 64 KiB read bound fails open to the guideline with a note, the way the ceiling's unusable line does (D68); a name that is lens-shaped but matches no bundle lens is surfaced, not guessed — its include shows up as its own NOT RUN line. The picker's instructions state the directive so the model does not fight it; every name is validated lens-shaped at parse, so the trusted instructions cannot be injected into from `REVIEW.md`. The auto-includes themselves stay model-applied per the guideline: this slice removes every mechanism that forced the picker to drop one (the cap, the hygiene slot, the "extras are dropped" instruction), and the parity gate's missing-lens failure stays scoped to a leftover hard cap or a hygiene-spent slot. `agentic-review` reads the preference, because it is the path that fetches the merge-base tree; the one-shot `review` command fetches no merge-base tree and runs on the guideline alone — recorded here rather than given a second preference source. **Options.** Encoding the auto-include shapes as a controller-side path table was rejected: it would duplicate the guideline the picker already carries in its prompt and drift from it — the copied-parallel-way the standing authoring rules warn against — while the include/exclude lists are repo-authored data, not a re-derivation, so code carries only their application. An override that lived only in the prompt was rejected: a preference the model can ignore under injection is the same "depends on the model" failure the issue diagnoses. The parse lives in `review_wide._lens_preferences` beside the ceiling's, over one extracted `_review_md_text` walk, so the tarball walk exists once. **Cost.** None offline; live, a larger plan scales D68's derived call cap, reservation and wall, and no number changed. The documented pool-1 edge (plans of 13 or more lenses derive more than the service's 14,400-second parent backstop) predates this change and is now stated in `atlas_reviewer/README.md` as the ordinary shape of a many-lens plan to cover with `ATLAS_POOL_SIZE` or `ATLAS_LENS_WALL_S` — documented, not capped. **Reversibility.** `plan_lenses(max_lenses=None)` is the default and every caller passes its depth's value explicitly; drop the preference read and its argument and the picker is exactly the pre-D69 uncapped picker. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_picker.py`, `test_lenses.py`, `test_review_wide.py`, `test_cli.py`; each behavior a test that fails when reverted, each mutant applied by hand and killed): more than 8 picks survive both paths (`test_more_than_eight_picks_survive_review_depth`, kills M1, `Depth.REVIEW.limit` reverted to 8; `test_more_than_eight_picks_survive_the_agentic_path`, kills M2, a lens-count cap on `plan_agentic_lenses`; `test_an_uncapped_plan_runs_every_choice_the_budget_fits`, kills M3, the `max_lenses` default reverted to 8), with no "below the cap" reason anywhere at review depth; hygiene spends no slot (`test_hygiene_does_not_use_a_guideline_slot` and its agentic twin — twelve picks plus hygiene is exactly the shape the old cap broke, so M1 kills these too — and `test_the_picker_naming_hygiene_displaces_no_pick`, killed by the undeduplicated-ranked-hygiene mutant); the preference honored and recorded (`test_a_repo_preference_exclude_drops_a_ranked_pick_and_is_recorded`, kills M4, the exclude ignored; `test_a_repo_preference_include_runs_even_when_the_picker_missed_it`, `…_when_the_picker_falls_back` and `…_when_no_call_is_available`, kill M5, the includes silently dropped; `test_a_repo_preference_never_touches_the_critical_tier`, kills M6, the exclude reaching the critical tier; `test_triage_is_not_widened_by_a_repo_preference`, kills M7, the fallback applying includes at triage; `test_a_repo_preference_include_of_an_unbundled_lens_is_not_run_not_dropped`); the auto-includes and the no-cap sentence pinned at the instructions (`test_instructions_ask_for_the_guidelines_auto_includes`, kills M8, the sentences removed; `test_instructions_name_the_operator_depth_and_the_guidelines_breadth`, kills M9, a numeric cap back in the text — it asserts "at most" is gone); the preference parse (`test_the_reviewed_repos_review_md_overrides_the_lens_guideline`, `test_a_no_space_lens_preference_line_is_honored`, `test_an_unusable_lens_preference_line_is_ignored_not_refused` and `test_lens_preference_names_never_carry_markup`, which kills M10, the lens-shape validation dropped; `test_an_oversized_review_md_is_not_parsed_cut_for_lens_lines_either`); and the CLI dry run shows the full plan (`test_a_dry_run_shows_the_full_plan_for_more_than_eight_picks` — fourteen lenses clean on the status line and the no-cap sentence in the instructions the picker received; `test_a_dry_run_honors_the_reviewed_repos_lens_preference` — the override recorded in the draft and stated in the instructions; `test_a_dry_run_records_an_unusable_lens_preference_line`, kills M11, the note dropped from the draft). **Overlap.** #652 touches `coordinator.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_lenses.py`, `docs/open-questions.md` and `docs/session-log.md`; drafts #654/#655 touch `cli.py`, `lens_loop.py`, `agentic_config.py`, `tool_model.py`, the README and the docs logs. This slice edits only `atlas_reviewer/picker.py`, `lenses.py`, `review_wide.py` and `cli.py`, confined to picker breadth, guideline slots and the repo-preference override, and appends only to the docs logs. **Update (2026-11-16, round-1 review of #729).** Eight fixes on the PR branch, appended here; the verdict was COMMENT (one Major, five Minors, two Nits; the reviewer re-ran mutants M4 and M9 by hand and confirmed both killed). (1, Major) `_apply_preferences` did not know the D58 controller-added hygiene choice `_with_critical` inserts after it, so `review_lens_include: reviewing-pr-and-process-hygiene` with a verification-stating body ran hygiene twice (the body inlined twice one-shot, two conversations and a doubled reservation agentic), and `review_lens_exclude:` naming hygiene silently did nothing. The hygiene choices now reach `_apply_preferences`, join the covered set, and an exclude naming `PR_HYGIENE` removes the controller-added lens — hygiene is neither critical-tier nor a cap, so the repo may decline it — with the note recording it; `plan_lenses` refuses a repeated name structurally (NOT RUN, "already chosen earlier in this plan"), so no path can plan a lens twice; and a loop test holds the selection duplicate-free across the hygiene/preference/fallback combinations. (2) Exclude wins over include on every path: `_preferenced_fallback` applies the exclude filter too (on the fallback paths the only excludable choice is the controller-added hygiene lens), and a name in both lists does not run on either path; stated in the README. (3) The README's exclude contract now says what the code does — the F3 carve-out was resolved by making hygiene excludable, not by documenting an exemption. (4) Cheap hardening, not a redesign: the controller computes the auto-include shapes from the changed paths it already holds (`picker.guideline_shapes` — a decision record or the decisions log touched, an all-documentation diff with at least one non-record path, a git rename record) and states them in the picker's instructions and in the draft's selection note, each path quoted as data, so the request is auditable even when the model leaves a shape out; the shapes are **stated, not enforced** — the auto-includes stay model-applied per the guideline, the recorded owner decision stands, and force-adding the lenses in the controller remains the owner's call, not this slice's. (5) The `_BREADTH` enumeration no longer claims to be every auto-include ("its auto-includes include"), names the decision route's second lens (`auditing-decision-record-currency` when an existing record is edited rather than authored), and attributes the rename pair to the guideline's restructuring route, where the guideline carries it. (6) A repo preference is recorded in the selection note whenever one is present — "nothing needed changing" when the picks already satisfy it — so an operator can tell applied-and-satisfied from ignored. (7) The fallback note no longer says "only the critical tier was considered" once the preference has added to it or dropped from it. (8) `LensPreferences.source` is dropped; the one source is the module constant the directive and the note both read. Killed there, by hand: the covered-set mutant omitting the hygiene choice (the include-duplicate and the exclude-no-op tests both fail without the fix), the structural dedupe removed (the repeated-choice test), the fallback exclude filter removed (the fallback exclude-wins test), the record suppressed when nothing changed (the F6 test), and the "only" reword removed (the F7 test). **Overlap note.** #652's `test_lenses.py` imports `_NAME` from `atlas_reviewer.picker`, which this PR renamed to `LENS_NAME` and deleted; no deprecated alias ships — whoever merges second updates the import to `LENS_NAME`. Offline only: fakes, the shipped fixture and the real CLI's dry run; no live model call, network call or secret read; no deployed setting, Fly config value or secret changed. **Correction (2026-11-16, round-2 review of #729).** The kill list above over-attributes two tests to one mutant — the exact killed-mutant-claim class D67's correction withdrew a claim from: under the covered-set mutant omitting the controller-added hygiene choice, only `test_a_repo_preference_include_of_hygiene_does_not_run_it_twice` fails; the exclude-no-op tests still pass, because exclusion flows through `hygiene_dropped`/`kept_hygiene`, not through the covered set, and the exclude direction dies under the separate mutant that hardcodes `hygiene_dropped` false (round-2's R2), on `test_a_repo_preference_exclude_removes_the_controller_added_hygiene`. The coverage itself is real — every direction is guarded, each by its own mutant. Four smaller round-2 findings shipped with this correction: the fallback's hygiene drop is recorded once, as the controller-added drop, never also as a dropped pick (`test_a_fallback_hygiene_exclude_is_recorded_once`); the rename sentences in the instructions and the shape facts are marked "among others", so a two-lens sample does not read exhaustive (the route also carries `checking-restraint` and `checking-idioms-and-consistency`); the `_DECISION_DIRS` comment no longer claims coverage it does not have (a root-level log under another name matches no heuristic, and a missed shape costs one sentence, as the function's docstring already says); and the `_pick_lenses` comment's #624 claim is stated as its current truth — the branch's `select_lenses` call never reads `scrub`, while `guideline_shapes` does, before the branch, and only through the note. The round-3 guards carry their mutants, applied by hand: the fallback-record mutant (reverting the hygiene drop to a dropped pick) dies on `test_a_fallback_hygiene_exclude_is_recorded_once`, and the two "among others" mutants (removing the qualifier from the instructions sentence or from the shape-fact sentence) die on `test_instructions_ask_for_the_guidelines_auto_includes` and `test_the_rename_shape_names_the_restructuring_route_lenses`.

- **D70 — The webhook service's review child follows a service mode: `agentic-review` by default, the one-shot `review` kept as the selectable oneshot mode (fixes #712; wires D68's limits into the service child) (2026-11-16).** *(agent, 2026-11-16, issue #712; Brandon decided on Nov 15, 2026 to move the service onto `agentic-review` and keep one-shot as a selectable mode for low-budget runs or runs with different parameters)* Offline only: the tests use fakes, the shipped `sample-pr` fixture and the real CLI's dry run; no live model call, network call or secret read was made, and no deployed setting, Fly config value or secret changed — flipping the running app is a separate, approved deploy step, out of scope here. **The failure.** The deployed webhook app started the one-shot `review` command (D53), so every review it posted ran only tracing and security: migration, concurrency and pr-hygiene were skipped and the picker was never called, and the coverage parity the agentic path exists for never reached a posted comment. **Choice.** `service_run.RunConfig` carries a `mode` resolved from `ATLAS_SERVICE_MODE` (`agentic` when unset; an unknown value raises, and `service.config_from_env` turns the raise into the startup refusal, so a typo stops the process instead of silently picking a command). `review_argv` builds `agentic-review`'s argv in agentic mode and the `review` argv in oneshot mode — both with the same `--event`/`--out`/`--events`/`--provider` wiring — and the one-shot mode stays a complete, callable command; nothing is stubbed. The agentic `ATLAS_*` limit variables (`agentic_config.ENV`, D68's `ATLAS_PER_LENS_CALLS` and `ATLAS_PER_LENS_USD` and the $10 absolute `ATLAS_REVIEW_CEILING_USD` included) join `_CHILD_ALLOWED`; they are settings, not credentials, and the one-shot child ignores variables it does not read, so the shared allowlist costs it nothing, while a token-like name (`FLY_API_TOKEN`, the webhook secret, the App key) still stays in the parent. No lens cap is hard-coded in the service path — the picker may plan any number of lenses (#711 is not blocked by this), and the parent's wall clock stays the only service-side bound. That wall, a hard 1,200 seconds since D53, becomes `ATLAS_SERVICE_DEADLINE_S`, 60 to 14,400 seconds, because a slower model needs more time than the one-shot budget and a value below a minute cannot even start a runner; a value outside the bounds or not a number refuses startup. The posted body stays COMMENT-only (D46) and now reads the agentic draft: `summarize_draft` dispatches on the status line's `"mode": "agentic"` and maps the selection's `- NOT RUN:` bullets and the attestation-ledger lens lines (a lens counts as run when its line says `clean` or `partial`; `failed` and `NOT RUN` do not, the way the parity gate reads them), builds the file-coverage note from the status line's controller counts (`changed_files`, `unreviewed_files`, `coverage_complete`; an incomplete note with no unread file names lens coverage as the gap), and carries the status line's first limit stop (`LimitHit.message()` from the stored four fields), the context omissions, and the ceiling note into Limit stops, with the `Model calls:` line as Cost. The runner and round-log prose that sits in the coverage section when no prompt-cache section follows is not a limit and is excluded. **Options.** Reading the mode inside `make_runner` from the raw environment would have kept `service.py` untouched but left a misconfigured mode discoverable only at the first webhook; parsing it once in `config_from_env` fails at boot, where the runbook's readiness checks see it. A per-mode allowlist was rejected: `child_env` has no mode argument and a static allowlist is one invariant to test, not two. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_service_run.py`, each behavior a test that fails when reverted; mutants applied by hand): the mode selects the child command and both modes carry the same wiring (`test_the_mode_selects_the_child_command`, kills the mutant swapping the two command names, which fails both the agentic and the oneshot membership assertions); unset defaults to agentic and an unknown value fails closed with the variable named (`test_the_default_mode_is_agentic_and_an_unknown_mode_fails_closed`, and in `test_service_receive.py` the config and `ServiceConfig` refusals); the D68 limit and ceiling variables reach the child while a non-allowlisted token-like name does not (`test_the_child_receives_the_route_settings_and_the_agentic_limits` — the loop over every `agentic_config.ENV` name kills the mutant dropping the `set(AGENTIC_LIMIT_ENV.values())` union — and `test_the_agentic_runner_runs_the_agentic_command_with_the_limits` through `make_runner`); the deadline is honored, bounded and defaulted (`test_the_service_deadline_is_configurable_and_bounded`); and the posted COMMENT from a fixture PR run through the real `agentic-review` dry run lists every planned lens as run with `Lenses not run: None.` and the coverage note, as COMMENT (`test_an_agentic_fixture_run_posts_a_comment_listing_the_planned_lenses` — the parity gate: `lenses_run` must equal the draft ledger's run set exactly, so a dropped lens or a missed NOT RUN fails); the agentic summary's parsing is pinned section by section (`test_an_agentic_draft_reports_selection_stops_and_cost`, killing the mutants that drop the selection-bullet parse, split run from not-run states, or skip the limit-stop insertion); the one-shot summary is unchanged (`test_a_draft_reports_lenses_limits_cost_and_files`). **Cost.** None offline; on the deployed app, an agentic review's Sonnet cost is the $1.70-$4.80 D68 accepts, under the $10 ceiling, against the one-shot's cents — that step is the deploy decision this PR does not make. **Correction (2026-11-16, round-1 review of #727).** Six fixes on the PR branch, appended here. (1) The deadline Major: the shipped default left the parent's wall at the D53 one-shot 1,200 s — a third of the agentic child's own default derived review wall (`lens_loop.DEFAULT_REVIEW_WALL_S`, 3,600 s) — so a healthy multi-lens review was SIGTERMed mid-run and posted nothing; the failure lived only in service telemetry, and every later push requeued into the same kill. `service_deadline` is now mode-aware: oneshot keeps 1,200 s; agentic derives its default from the child's own configured review wall (`agentic_child_wall_s` reads `ATLAS_REVIEW_WALL_S` through `agentic_config.load_agentic_limits` — the same configuration the child reads; D68's derivation raises the multi-lens wall above it, never below it) plus the SIGTERM teardown wait (`CLEANUP_WAIT_S`), 3,690 s at defaults, and an operator-raised `ATLAS_REVIEW_WALL_S` raises the default with it. An explicit `ATLAS_SERVICE_DEADLINE_S` below the child's wall refuses at boot, and so does a child wall the 14,400 s ceiling cannot cover. **The refusal choice, not a loud warning:** the service already refuses to start on a bad port, secret, concurrency or provider (D53's precedent); a boot refusal is visible in the deploy's readiness checks, while a warning scrolls away and the service then starts into a configuration that silently kills every review it queues — the exact outcome the round-1 review flagged. Tests: `test_the_agentic_deadline_defaults_to_the_childs_wall_plus_teardown` (kills the mutant reverting the agentic default to 1,200) and `test_an_agentic_deadline_below_the_child_wall_refuses_at_boot` (kills the mutant accepting a below-wall value), plus the config refusals in `test_service_receive.py`; `README.md` and `deploy-service.md` document the pairing, and say plainly what a deadline kill posts today: nothing — the job fails, the failure is a telemetry event, the child's own stop has already destroyed its Sprite, and a later push of the same head can queue again. Changing that no-post outcome is out of scope. (2) The runner's failure note (`Runner reported, quoted:`, a shape the real `coordinator.render_draft` emits when a runner did not complete) is added to `_AGENTIC_NON_LIMITS`, so it is no longer posted as a limit stop; the test builds its draft from the real renderer in that state (`test_the_runners_failure_note_is_not_a_limit_stop`), killing the mutant that drops the prefix. (3) The service's agentic round log is per-head — the service passes `--state-dir <run dir>/state`, keyed by head SHA, and the CLI offers no other path — so prior-round context cannot accumulate in the deployed service; that needs a CLI flag or a service-side path plus a per-PR storage decision, and is tracked as issue #728, not fixed here. (4) Issue #712's `REVIEW_DEADLINE_S` is implemented as the env var `ATLAS_SERVICE_DEADLINE_S`; the module constant keeps its name and no alias is added — recorded here and in the PR body so the issue closes accurately. (5) The incomplete-coverage note with no unread file no longer attributes the gap to lens coverage (the status counts do not say which gap it is): the wording is cause-neutral — "Agentic coverage incomplete; see the draft's coverage and omission notes." — tested from the real renderer in that state (`test_an_incomplete_coverage_with_no_unread_file_names_no_cause`), killing the revert-to-cause mutant. (6) The nits: a draft `summarize_draft` cannot read — including a status line that will not parse — is a labeled `draft parse failed` failure in `make_runner`, still posting nothing (`test_an_unreadable_draft_fails_with_a_label`, killing the dropped-wrap mutant), and a draft whose status line disagrees with the service's mode (the version-skew shape: only a mismatched parent/child pair produces it, and one deploy ships them together) is the same labeled failure instead of a silent parse as the other mode's shape (`summarize_draft` grew an `expect=` keyword; `test_a_draft_from_the_other_mode_is_a_labeled_parse_failure`, killing the dropped-check mutant). The session-log rotation nit is pre-existing debt (#467/#520) and deliberately untouched here. **Correction (2026-11-16, round-2 review of #727).** Two fixes, appended here. (1) The deadline Major, narrowed: the round-1 mode-aware default tracked only the *configured* wall, and D68's waves raise — `max(configured, ATLAS_LENS_WALL_S x ceil(planned/pool))`, applied inside the child from the plan the picker produces, where the parent cannot see it — still undercut the parent at the shipped defaults: at pool 1 (`lens_loop.POOL_SIZE`, Brandon's 2026-11-11 provisional default), a plan of 4 or more lenses derives 4,800 s or more against the 3,690 s default, and the parity fixture's own 4-lens plan was the boundary case. The unset agentic default is now the deadline bound's ceiling itself (`MAX_SERVICE_DEADLINE_S`, 14,400 s, the same constant the bound check uses — not a copied number), because in agentic mode the parent deadline is a backstop, not the real limit: the child's own derived wall and D68's $10 ceiling are what stop a review. The boot refusal stays, and stays scoped to the *configured* wall, which is the only part of the child's wall the parent can see; the residual — a derived wall up to `ATLAS_LENS_WALL_S x ceil(N/pool)` — is documented in `README.md` and `deploy-service.md` as the operator's to cover with an explicit `ATLAS_SERVICE_DEADLINE_S`, with the pool-1 edge named: plans of 13 or more lenses derive more than even 14,400 s, so those raise `ATLAS_POOL_SIZE` or lower `ATLAS_LENS_WALL_S`. Tests: `test_the_agentic_deadline_defaults_to_the_bound_ceiling` (kills the mutant reverting the default to configured-wall-plus-margin; also pins that the child's limits are still validated at boot and that an operator-raised wall does not move the ceiling), `test_an_agentic_deadline_below_the_child_wall_refuses_at_boot` (unchanged refusal, minus the removed default-past-ceiling case), and `test_the_review_deadline_is_read_and_bounded` still pins the oneshot 1,200 s default. (2) The label nit: a limit variable the child's loader refuses is re-raised naming the environment variable, not the loader's internal field name (`agentic_child_wall_s` wraps `load_agentic_limits` through `_named_limit_error`, which maps the leading field name through `agentic_config.ENV`), so an invalid `ATLAS_REVIEW_WALL_S` refuses at boot saying `ATLAS_REVIEW_WALL_S`; `test_an_invalid_child_wall_refusal_names_the_variable` kills the mutant dropping the wrap. **Correction (2026-11-16, round-3 review of #727).** The round-2 change removed the round-1 refusal of a configured child wall above the deadline ceiling along with its now-unneeded default-path branch — but the undercut it guarded stayed reachable on the default path: with `ATLAS_REVIEW_WALL_S` above 14,400 s and no explicit `ATLAS_SERVICE_DEADLINE_S`, the service booted with a 14,400 s deadline below the child's configured wall, the guaranteed silent kill the refusal exists to prevent. The comparison is restored on both paths (`wall > MAX_SERVICE_DEADLINE_S` refuses, naming `ATLAS_REVIEW_WALL_S` and saying to lower it); `test_an_agentic_deadline_below_the_child_wall_refuses_at_boot` carries the restored assertions for the default path and an explicit value, killing the mutant dropping the comparison, and the docs name the case in one line each.
**Update (2026-11-17, D76 — recovered NOT RUN lines).** The mapping above gains one carve-out: a coverage-section line the coordinator marked recovered -- a NOT RUN or failed entry of a lens whose later run in the same review came back clean (#692) -- counts as run (the lens's clean line is in the same section), and the failure itself is carried among the limit stops, so "Lenses not run" stays truthful and the parity gate does not fail a lens that ran. A NOT RUN with no clean run parses exactly as stated here.

- **D73 — The agentic review passes the head SHA's CI evidence into every lens (issue #715 Part A; wires the 2026-11-13 plan's phase 3) (2026-11-16).** *(agent, 2026-11-16, from the 2026-11-13 model trials' diagnosis §3: the protocol Minors the live models missed included #661 m1 — the PR body's stale CI and stack lines — not because the owning lens failed to run, but because the agentic lenses can only read, and `review_lens` receives no CI evidence to check a body's claims against; #717 keeps D68 and #711–#714 keep D69–D72, so this slice takes D73; corrected after rounds 1, 2 and an owner decision recorded during PR #724, all on the PR branch before landing)* Offline only: the tests use the shipped fixture, the scripted reviewer and fakes; no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, no secret was read, and no deployed service setting changed. Part B of the plan (the sandboxed `run_tests`/`mutate` tools) is untouched and stays out. **Choice.** `gather.py` gains `gather_head_ci`: the check runs at the head SHA, the workflow runs at the head SHA, and the jobs of those runs, under the one-shot path's own hard caps (`IngestConfig`) and the same fetch/parse/cap bookkeeping `gather_ci` uses, with nothing else fetched — no other commit's checks, no listing under the head branch's name, no job-log or artifact download (D25's evidence scoping; the plan scopes Part A to a summary of what ran at the head, and what was never fetched is simply not in the block rather than an omission for it). The new `ci_block.ci_block` turns that into the agentic first turn's block: `classify` against the head SHA with no expected keys, then the one-shot path's own `render_evidence`, prefixed with the one controller-written line in the block, which names the head SHA the evidence was gathered at, so the block cannot be read against another commit later in the same conversation. `_cmd_agentic_review` passes it into `review_lenses` as one more `context=` block, after the conversation block, and merges its omissions into `context_omissions` — the first turn's D52 order is now prior rounds, the conversation, the CI evidence, the changed-files listing. **The block is data.** Job names, check-run summaries and workflow paths are PR-written text: the fence and the nonce are `first_turn`'s one fence (D65's per-review nonce), no second fence is built here, and the block's values keep the same encoding the one-shot's evidence block uses. **Failures are omissions.** Every gather failure — a listing that could not be fetched, an entry that does not parse, a page cap cut — becomes an omission beside the assembly's, so `coverage_complete` goes false and a review the lenses would approve is held at COMMENT (D42); CI evidence can never make a review approvable the omission gate would not. **Sizing is D67's, and the invariant is by construction.** The room `ci_block` is handed is the fit minus the conversation block's UTF-8 bytes (None-preserving), so the two context blocks together cannot exceed the fit — the first turn's budget order is conversation, then CI, then the prior rounds, and the prior rounds take what remains. Within that room the build runs at `min(16,384, room − 39)` characters (`ci_block.CI_EVIDENCE_CHARS`, the size D52 gives the prior-round blocks; the 39 is the truncation marker), truncation at that cap is named, and a build whose UTF-8 bytes still overflow the room is omitted whole and named — never silently truncated, never sent to overflow the per-call token check; the omit-whole gate fires below 43 characters, the same figure D67's round-2 review derived. A model that names no room keeps the 16,384 cap with the per-call check as the only bound. The marker and minimum-fit figures live beside `redact.TRUNCATION_MARKER` (`TRUNCATION_MARKER_CHARS`, `MIN_FIT_CHARS`), the shared home `ci_block` imports; `cli.py` keeps its own main-side derivation of the same two figures — the lines #717's in-flight edit rewrites — and a test holds the two copies equal (`test_the_cli_sizing_copies_agree_with_their_shared_home`, kills M10, a copy drifting from the other). **The one asymmetry with the conversation block, named.** When a truncated build's bytes still overflow the room, the conversation block rebuilds once smaller — `build_conversation` can produce a smaller complete block by dropping the oldest remarks — while the CI block omits whole: `render_evidence` has no refit, so a smaller build here would only be a shorter truncated table, and omitting is the conservative direction; either outcome holds the review at COMMENT anyway. **What does not change.** The picker keeps D38's view; the status line gains no field; the draft shows CI evidence only through the existing quote and scrub paths (the omissions section and the lenses' quote-verified findings). **The reuse-with-newer-evidence decision (Brandon, 2026-11-15 8:32 PM CT).** The owner's call on the settled-lens question this block raises — a settled lens is reused for the same head and body without a runner, and the CI block is not part of that reuse key, so a reused verdict never sees the CI evidence gathered for the later review (a check still pending when the lens settled and failed afterwards is invisible to it; the same gap exists for the conversation block since D67, whose new PR comments after settlement are equally invisible): "it's a little of both. we shouldn't toss out findings just because more information comes along, but the agent should re-assess their judgement and follow up accordingly if the new information changes anything." So a reused settled result keeps its findings — newer CI evidence or newer comments never discard them — and, when newer CI evidence or comments exist since the reused result was produced, the lens re-assesses its judgment with that new evidence and adds, revises or follows up on findings where the new information changes anything. The re-assessment behavior is **not implemented in this PR** — the reuse key and the reuse code are unchanged here; it is the follow-up this decision records (#726), and it covers the conversation block's identical gap. **Options.** Reusing `gather_ci` wholesale was rejected: it also fetches the other commits' check runs (D38's stale-evidence visibility), the head branch's workflow runs, job logs and artifacts — outside the plan's scoping, and ten 5-MB logs would eat the room the conversation budgeted. Building a second, agentic-only summary format was rejected in favor of the one-shot's `render_evidence`, so the two paths' CI evidence cannot drift apart. Sizing the CI block against the full fit was the first cut and is rejected here: the reviewer's worktree arithmetic showed a conversation near its cap plus a near-cap CI block overrunning the fit by more than the 8,192-character reserve, starving the prior rounds to one character and risking a per-call refusal — hence the remainder the block is now handed. **Cost.** Per review, reads at the one-shot's own scope: two listings (check runs and workflow runs at the head SHA) plus one jobs listing per head-SHA run, capped at five runs; no new CLI flag and no budget number changed. **Reversibility.** Drop the `ci_block` call and its `context` entry and the first turn is exactly the pre-D73 one; `gather_head_ci` is additive and `gather_ci`'s behavior is unchanged (its `_gather_run`'s jobs half was extracted into `_gather_jobs` and calls back into it). **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_agentic_context.py`, each behavior a test that fails when reverted, each mutant applied by hand and killed): the block reaches every lens's fenced first turn, tagged with the head SHA, and carries only that SHA's runs (`test_the_ci_block_reaches_every_lens_tagged_with_the_head_sha`, kills M1, dropping the block from `context=`, and M7, dropping the tag line); only the head SHA's own results are fetched and gathered (`test_only_the_head_shas_results_are_fetched`, kills M4, the mutant that also asks for another commit's check runs and the branch-name listing); a failed check-run listing is an omission that holds an approve-shaped review at COMMENT while the unread evidence stays UNKNOWN inside the block (`test_a_ci_fetch_failure_is_an_omission_and_holds_the_review_at_comment`, kills M2, swallowing the gather's failures); an injected job name stays inside the fence and out of the trusted system text (`test_an_injected_job_name_stays_fenced`, kills M3, the fence-stripping mutant that ships block text unfenced); the block is sized against the room the conversation left, by construction, whichever way the conversation's slack falls (`test_the_ci_block_takes_the_room_the_conversation_leaves`, kills M8, reverting to full-fit sizing, and — on its four-byte parametrization — M9, measuring the conversation block in characters instead of UTF-8 bytes, which only a multi-byte conversation separates); a block the room cannot hold is omitted whole and named, at the unit level (`test_a_ci_block_that_does_not_fit_the_room_is_omitted_whole_by_name`, kills M5, removing the omit-whole gate) and end to end under the D67 room budget (`test_a_ci_block_bigger_than_what_the_room_has_left_is_omitted_by_name`, kills M6, the wiring that hands the block no room); a truncated build fits the room in one build with the cut named (`test_a_ci_truncated_build_fits_the_room_in_one_build`, kills M5b, building at the room without the marker subtraction); and the small-fit band 1–60 in both byte widths never crashes and never ships an oversized block (`test_the_ci_small_fit_band_never_crashes_and_never_ships_an_oversized_block`). **Overlap.** The only #717 file touched is `atlas_reviewer/cli.py`, confined to the wiring beside D67's blocks and the `_input_room_chars` docstring; `lens_loop.py`, `review_wide.py` and `agentic_config.py` are untouched, there is no coordinator change at all, and the docs logs are appended only.
**Update (2026-11-16, issue #726 — the re-assessment implemented).** *(agent, 2026-11-16, on the branch that closes #726; the same offline rules as above: the tests use the scripted reviewer, the fakes and the shipped fixture; no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, no secret was read, and no deployed service setting, Fly config value or secret changed)* The reuse path now does what the decision above records, in `atlas_reviewer/reassess.py` (new) and the pieces it wires. **Detection is a digest, not a clock.** `agentic-review` keeps the data its blocks are built from: the CI gather now fills a caller-owned `Collected` (`ci_block`'s new optional `collected` parameter), and the conversation's remarks are gathered by the command before `_conversation_block` builds — so a review whose conversation block is omitted still holds them — and `reassess.snapshot_digest` digests the snapshot: each CI item's source id, head SHA, tool, conclusion and redacted detail (its fetch time excluded, because it differs on every review of unchanged evidence and says when *we* read, not what *they* did), each remark's source id, GitHub timestamp, author, context and raw body, and the gathers' omissions, failures and notes, so a listing that now fails or now succeeds moves the digest too. `AttemptLedger.finish` stores the digest with the finished attempt (`evidence_digest=`), and `AttemptLedger.evidence_digest(key)` reads the last finished attempt's. **The comparison and the call.** In `coordinator._review_one`'s settled branch, a caller that passes a snapshot digest compares it against the stored one; a result whose digest differs — or that carries none, because it settled before this mechanism — earns **one bounded re-assessment call**: no tools (the head SHA is unchanged, so the code the findings cite is the code that is there), no repair turn, reserved at its worst case against the review's shared `Spend` (D68's derived limits and dollar ceiling; a refusal is recorded as the failed attempt it is and stores no digest, so the next review re-runs the call) and settled like any turn, and no lens-count bound (D69) — the shared call cap, dollar ceiling and wall clock are the only bounds. A caller that passes no snapshot keeps today's reuse unchanged, with no extra calls. **The turn** (`reassess.reassess_system`/`reassess_turn`) carries the prior findings — the effective set, labeled `P1`, `P2`, … with their quotes — plus the review's own context blocks, the conversation and CI evidence this review's first turn would have carried: the digest says that something changed, not what, so the delta is delivered as the complete current picture, bounded by the same D38/D52/D67/D73 budgets the first turn is and fenced with the review's one nonce; no second rendering path exists. **The reply** has its own shape (`reassess.parse_reassess_reply`): the Finding contract's findings, each optionally `revises`-ing a prior label with a stated `reason`; and `withdrawn` entries of exactly `{id, reason}` — a withdrawal or revision without a reason is refused, because the reason is the output a reader relies on. Silence keeps: a prior finding the reply does not mention stands (findings are never discarded for being old). Every reported finding is quote-verified against the head files like an ordinary reply's, and a label that names no prior finding refuses the whole reply. **The record.** A re-assessment is recorded as attempts on the lens's own `#reassess` ledger key, never on the lens key: the lens's settled state — attestation, causes, coverage — stays the full run's, and a garbled supplementary reply cannot turn a clean lens partial; the reuse path composes the effective result from the settled outcome and every recorded re-assessment, in order, on every review (`reassess.effective_outcome`), so the composition is durable and reproducible from the ledger alone, and the draft lists the withdrawn findings with their reasons and a revision's replacement id ("Withdrawn findings (re-assessed with newer evidence)"). A settled result that was not attested earns no re-assessment — no prior findings to keep, and a single no-tools call is not a lens run; the ordinary retry policy governs it — and a review whose spend is halted or whose wall clock has passed reuses as it was, noted in the lens line. The dry-run script answers the re-assessment turn from a new `reassessment` scenario section, validated at load; the no-script dry-run model confirms by silence. **Paths.** The one-shot path re-reviews every time and carries no settled-result reuse, so there is nothing to wire there; the deployed service's GitHub permission failures keep their existing handling — a refused listing is an omission (D42), moves the digest, and the re-assessment proceeds with what is available and the gap recorded. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_reassess.py`, each mutant applied by hand and killed): reuse without new evidence makes no extra call, pinned by a request-count assertion (`test_a_reused_lens_with_no_newer_evidence_makes_no_extra_call`, kills M1, re-assessing whenever the digest is wired); reuse with a new CI failure and with a newer comment each earn exactly one bounded call whose turn carries the prior findings and the delta inside the one fence (`test_a_reused_lens_reassesses_when_newer_ci_evidence_exists`, kills M5, the composition that keeps only the reply's own findings, and M10, shipping the delta outside the fence; `test_a_reused_lens_reassesses_when_a_newer_comment_arrives`); a withdrawn finding keeps its stated reason in the draft (`test_a_withdrawn_finding_keeps_its_stated_reason`, kills M4, recording withdrawals without their reasons, and M14, dropping the withdrawals before the draft); a revision replaces the prior finding and the withdrawal line names the replacement's minted id (`test_a_revision_replaces_the_prior_finding_and_names_its_replacement`); the call is charged to the shared spend and its digest stored (`test_the_reassessment_call_is_charged_to_the_review_s_spend`, kills M6, spending outside the `Spend` object, and M3, never storing the digest); a re-assessment over the reservation is refused, keeps the priors, and is re-earned on the next review (`test_a_reassessment_over_the_reservation_is_refused_and_retried_later`); a reply that never met the contract keeps every prior finding and re-runs the call next review (`test_a_reply_that_never_meets_the_contract_keeps_the_prior_findings`); a pre-digest settled result re-assesses once and then stands (`test_a_result_settled_before_the_digest_was_stored_reassesses_once_and_stands`, also killing M2′, reading the stored digest from the lens key alone, so a recorded re-assessment's own digest would never settle the reuse); a never-attested settled result earns none (`test_a_settled_result_that_never_attested_earns_no_reassessment`, kills M9); the composition replays recorded re-assessments in order against the set each was labeled against (`test_the_composition_replays_recorded_reassessments_in_order`), with an unresolvable recorded label skipped and named (`test_a_composition_note_names_an_unresolvable_reference`); the ledger round-trip keeps the references and the digest (`test_a_reassessed_outcome_reads_back_exactly` and its neighbors in `test_attestation.py`, kills M13); a judge that accepts a label naming no prior finding dies on `test_a_label_that_names_no_prior_finding_is_refused_at_judge` (M12); the CLI hands the reuse path the snapshot (`test_the_agentic_review_hands_the_reuse_path_the_evidence_snapshot` in `test_agentic_context.py`, kills M8, dropping the argument); and the scripted reviewer answers the re-assessment turn from the scenario, not as a first turn (`test_lens_script.py`'s D73 section). **Overlap.** #730 (`cli.py`, `service_run.py`, tests, `docs/session-log.md`), #652 (`coordinator.py`, tests, docs logs) and the drafts #654 and #655 (`cli.py`, `lens_loop.py`, `agentic_config.py`, README, docs logs) touch nearby files; `lens_loop.py` and `agentic_config.py` are untouched here, and the docs logs are appended only.
