**Update (2026-11-16, round-1 review of #731 — four fixes, each with its test or mutant).** *(agent, 2026-11-16; same offline rules)* **M2 — a withdrawal is an annotation, not a deletion.** The owner's "keeps its findings" rule, hardened: `reassess.effective_outcome` no longer removes a prior finding on a withdrawal — the finding stays in the set `decide` sees, and the withdrawal travels as an annotation that the draft's withdrawn section shows with its stated reason, so an injected comment can lower a finding's visibility but never flip a Blocker review to approve (`test_an_injected_withdrawal_of_a_blocker_still_sets_the_verdict`, kills the mutant that deletes on withdrawal). A revision still takes the prior finding's place — except one that lowers a Blocker's or a Major's severity, which is annotation-only too, so the higher severity still counts (`test_a_revision_that_lowers_a_blocker_is_annotation_only`, kills the mutant that applies the lowering replacement); a revision that keeps or raises severity replaces as before. The reply instructions state the proposal semantics. Option (c) also shipped: the status line carries `withdrawn`, the count of prior findings a re-assessment annotated (`test_an_injected_withdrawal_of_a_blocker_still_sets_the_verdict` and the withdrawal test assert it; the mutant that drops the field dies on them), and `atlas_reviewer/README.md`'s status-line paragraph names it. **M1 — the re-assessment call is traced.** The reuse branch builds the review's scrubber and hands `lens_trace.wrap_model(model)` to `_reassess_call`, so the call records its completion span — cost, latency, reservation — like every other model call (`test_a_reassessment_call_records_its_completion_span`, kills the mutant that hands the raw model). **M3 — one malformed reply cannot pin the head at COMMENT forever.** `dropped` is composed from the settled run plus only the latest re-assessment attempt's notes, so an earlier attempt's malformed-severity entry is superseded by the later reply that re-lists it or stays silent (`test_an_earlier_reassessments_malformed_severity_does_not_accumulate`, kills the mutant that extends every attempt's notes); the latest reply's own note still persists, the settled run's own semantics. **N1 — only verified priors participate**, stated in the module docstring and `judge`'s. **N2 — a reply that both revises and withdraws the same label is refused** at `judge` (`test_a_reply_that_revises_and_withdraws_the_same_label_is_refused`, kills the mutant that accepts the combination; end to end the refusal records an UNPARSED attempt, keeps the priors, and stores no digest). **N3 — `snapshot_digest`'s docstring names what is not digested**: the PR's title, body, labels and commit subjects, so "the conversation" is not read as the whole block. **N4 — the status line's `limit_stop` names a re-assessment's own stop**: the composed outcome carries the latest re-assessment attempt's `limit_hit` when it has one (`test_the_status_line_names_a_reassessments_limit_stop`, kills the mutant that keeps the settled run's). Latest `main` (#730, `45794bca`) merged first; its `cli.py` hunks are disjoint from this branch's.
**Update (2026-11-16, round-2 review of #731 — the routine's Major fixed, plus its Minor and three Nits).** *(agent, 2026-11-16; same offline rules)* **The Major (the routine's round-1 finding at `coordinator.py`, still open at the round-1 fix head): the re-assessment quote check only saw the changed files.** A verified prior finding can cite an unchanged file -- the ordinary run's lens read it there, through its own `read_file` -- and the re-assessment's quote check, built from `read_sources(trees.head, diff, scope)` plus an empty head-reads set, could not verify that same quote, so a revision of such a prior replaced a verified finding with an unverified copy and the finding disappeared from the reported set. Both of the routine's suggested shapes shipped: `judge` now takes the head tree and quote-checks against `with_head_files(sources, head_tree, cited)`, where `cited` is every path the reply's and the prior findings' locations cite (`reassess._cited_paths`), so a revision of a prior citing an unchanged file verifies the same quote (`test_a_revision_of_a_prior_citing_an_unchanged_file_verifies`, the routine's requested test -- P1 cites `docs/notes.md:1`, read at head by the ordinary run, revised with its quote unchanged; the mutant that checks changed files only dies on it); and `effective_outcome` keeps the prior whenever its replacement does not verify -- a failed revision cannot be the evidence for removing a verified finding -- showing it as an annotation that carries the quote check's reason (`test_a_revision_whose_replacement_does_not_verify_keeps_the_prior`, kills the mutant that removes the prior on an unverified replacement; the withdrawn line now says "the prior finding stands"). **The Minor: the re-assessment attempt is on the ledger before its first model call**, the way the lens path's is, and a failure beyond the recorded causes is finished as the `MODEL_ERROR` attempt it is -- an exception in the re-assessment no longer fails the whole review; the prior findings stand and the note says so (`test_an_exception_in_the_reassessment_does_not_fail_the_review`, kills the propagate mutant; `test_a_death_mid_reassessment_leaves_the_started_attempt_abandoned`, a `BaseException` the guard correctly does not catch, kills the start-after-the-call mutant via the started-with-no-finish record the file carries). **The Nits.** A revision's `reason` is capped at `MAX_TEXT_CHARS`, the way a withdrawal's is (`test_a_revision_reason_over_the_text_cap_is_refused`); `_note`'s counts cover only resolved references, with annotation-only revisions -- a proposed downgrade or a failed-verify revision -- counted as `annotated`, separate from `revised` (`test_the_note_counts_resolved_references_and_names_annotations`, kills the count-everything and fold-into-revised mutants); the N2 test uses pytest's `tmp_path` instead of a leaked `mkdtemp` directory; and `effective_outcome`'s docstring states that an abandoned latest attempt carries no dropped notes. 3,105 reviewer tests, tooling 882 passed at 95.20%, ruff/mypy/drift clean. Offline only.
- **D74 — The service's agentic round log is one file per pull request on the volume, passed through `--round-log` (fixes #728) (2026-11-17).** *(agent, 2026-11-17, issue #728; found in the #727 round-1 protocol review, recorded as D70's correction (3), where CodeRabbit raised the same gap; #717 keeps D68 and #711–#714 keep D69–D72, so this slice takes D74 — D71 and D72 stay reserved for #713 and #714)* Offline only: the tests use the shipped fixture, the scripted reviewer and fakes; no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, no secret was read, and no deployed service setting, Fly config value or secret changed — pointing the running app at the per-PR path is the deploy step this PR does not make. **The failure.** `_cmd_agentic_review` hardcoded `round_log=RoundLog(_state_dir(args, env) / "rounds.jsonl")`, and the service passes `--state-dir <run dir>/state` with `run_dir` keyed by head SHA (`service_run.run_dir`), so every pushed head started from an empty round log: round N never read round N-1 in the deployed service, and D67/S1's prior-round context never accumulated there. D67 had deferred exactly this ("the webhook queue will choose its own" path) and the queue never had. **Choice.** `service_run.round_log_path(root, event)` is `<state root>/rounds/<repo>-<pr>.jsonl` — keyed by repository and pull request number, the repository flattened the way `run_dir` flattens it — and `review_argv` now takes the event and appends `--round-log <path>` to the agentic child's argv only: the one-shot `review` command has no round log, so oneshot mode passes none. The event parameter is required, not optional, so an agentic argv cannot be built without deciding where its log lives. On the CLI side, `_round_log_path` prefers `--round-log` when the caller names one and keeps D67's unflagged `<state dir>/rounds.jsonl` otherwise, so a local run's path is unchanged; `rounds.py` itself is untouched — the reader, the renderer, the fencing and the size budgets are exactly D67's, and the contents stay untrusted data under its rules, with no second path built. **Storage, retention, concurrency.** On the Fly app the state root is the volume (`ATLAS_REVIEWER_STATE_DIR` = `/data`), so each pull request's log is `/data/rounds/<repo>-<pr>.jsonl`, outside the per-head run directory: `_drop_run_dir` removes `runs/<repo>-<pr>-<sha>` when a job ends and never touches `rounds/`. No janitor ships in this slice — there is no existing cleanup path that could own one — and the log stays on the volume until a later cleanup slice trims it; at one record per finished review, each capped at `RECORD_CHARS` (8,192 characters of JSON) plus framing, growth is slow enough that the 1 GB volume is not the binding constraint, and deleting or trimming a log loses only prior-round context (D67's reversibility, unchanged). The queue's one-active-job-per-PR rule (`service_queue.enqueue` supersedes any active job of the same PR and cancels it) makes each log single-writer in the ordinary case; the teardown window of a superseded review — the child finishes its SIGTERM cleanup while the next head's review may already run — can still overlap two writers on one file, and the reader's existing semantics absorb that: a line that lands torn names no pull request and blocks nobody (`unattributed`), a line that parses but fails record validation is that pull request's `unreadable record` omission, and a wholly unreadable file is the `round log file` omission — each named in the next draft of the pull request that tried to read it, never a crash. **A missing log is not an omission — decided here, against the issue's wording.** The issue's criterion said "a missing or corrupt log becomes a named omission"; the missing half is wrong as an omission, because a pull request's first review always finds no file, has no prior rounds to omit, and D42 holds a review with an omission at COMMENT — so naming the absence would hold every first review there. A missing file reads as an empty log: no crash, no omission, the review runs to its own verdict, and the record still appends. The corrupt half is the named omission, and was already D67's behavior (`LogRead.file_unreadable`); this slice pins it through the new seam. **Options.** Passing the per-PR path as an environment variable was rejected: the parent computes it per job from the event, so it is argv data, not configuration, and `--round-log` is the caller-chosen seam D67 left open. Reusing the per-head state directory for a shared log (a `rounds.jsonl` the parent symlinks or copies forward) was rejected: the state dir is keyed by head because the attestation ledger's reuse keys are, and a copied-forward log would silently diverge from the append-only file the child re-opens. A per-PR `--state-dir` was rejected for the same reason — it would move the ledger's keying. **Cost.** None offline; live, one read and one append of the PR's own file per review, no new model call, and no budget number changed. **Reversibility.** Drop the `--round-log` argument from `review_argv` and the service is back to per-head logs (the defect, nothing else); drop the CLI's flag and the unflagged path is exactly pre-D74; the log is keyed by repository and pull request with each record labelled by head SHA, so re-keying later is a file move, not a migration. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_service_run.py`, `test_agentic_context.py`; each behavior a test that fails when reverted, each mutant applied by hand and killed): the agentic child gets the per-PR flag and the oneshot child none (`test_the_agentic_child_reviews_with_a_per_pr_round_log`: the path is `<state root>/rounds/<repo flattened>-<pr>.jsonl`, outside the run directory, identical for two different heads — kills M1, the flag omitted so the child keys the log by its per-head state dir, and M2, `round_log_path` keyed by head; the mode-selects test now passes the event, so the required parameter itself is the structural kill for an argv built without the decision); two heads of one pull request through the real CLI's dry run share the flagged log (`test_two_heads_of_one_pr_share_the_service_round_log`: the second head's every lens first turn opens with the first round's fenced `prior-review` block naming the first head superseded, both records land at the flagged path, and the state dir's default `rounds.jsonl` never appears — kills M3, the CLI ignoring `--round-log`); a missing log is the first round, not a crash and not an omission (`test_a_missing_round_log_is_the_first_round_not_a_crash`, pinning the decision above); a corrupt log is a named omission and not a crash (`test_a_corrupt_round_log_is_a_named_omission_not_a_crash`: a binary file at the flagged path, the draft names `the round log file could not be read`, the review exits incomplete); the coordinator-level unreadable-log omission and the round log's reach into the next round's first turn were already pinned by D67's tests (`test_an_unreadable_round_log_is_an_omission`, `test_the_round_log_reaches_the_next_rounds_first_turn`) and pass unchanged. **Overlap.** #652 (`coordinator.py`, tests, docs logs) and the drafts #654 and #655 (`cli.py`, `lens_loop.py`, `agentic_config.py`, README, docs logs) touch nearby files; this slice edits `atlas_reviewer/cli.py` (the flag and its seam only), `atlas_reviewer/service_run.py`, the two test files, `atlas_reviewer/README.md`, `atlas_reviewer/deploy-service.md` and `docs/agentic-reviewer.md`, leaves `coordinator.py`, `lens_loop.py` and `agentic_config.py` untouched, and appends only to the docs logs. **Update (2026-11-18, D79):** the one-active-job-per-PR rule's supersede is now the cancel side of the head-change policy: under the default keep, a newer head is held back as the pull request's pending follow-up instead of superseding the running review, and the log stays single-writer the same way -- the follow-up is queued only when the running review settles. A queued follow-up a newer push supersedes passes its delta base on, so the chain stays anchored on the last head a review actually finished.
**Update (2026-11-17, round-1 review of #732 — the standing-omission Major fixed, plus three Nits).** *(agent, 2026-11-17; same offline rules)* **The Major: a per-PR log made every round-log omission permanent for the pull request.** Under D67's per-head logs a garbled line died with its run directory; under this entry's per-PR file it held every later review at COMMENT with no way to heal — a `ROUND_VERSION` bump (or a rollback to older code) turning every existing line of every open pull request's log into an `unreadable record`, an undecodable file never healing because `append` reopens the same path, and a teardown-window garble the same. Two fixes, each with its test or mutant. (1) The `unreadable record` charge is now head-scoped in `prior_blocks`, the way its budget omission already was: a line that names this pull request but is not a current record carries the head it claims (`rounds.UnreadableLine`, extracted by `_owner` when the field itself is readable), and `prior_blocks` charges only a line whose head is the head under review — at an older head it is set-aside superseded data, and a line with no readable head is not proven to hide current-head context, so it charges nobody. Killed there: the charge-any-named-line mutant dies on `test_an_unreadable_line_at_an_older_head_is_set_aside_not_charged` and, end to end, on the routine's requested test `test_a_version_skewed_line_at_an_older_head_does_not_block_the_next_head`; the set-aside-everything mutant dies on `test_an_unreadable_line_at_the_current_head_is_an_omission_even_when_the_record_fits` and `test_an_unparseable_line_at_the_current_head_still_blocks`. The old coordinator pin that a head-less bad line blocks the PR it names (`test_a_bad_line_blocks_only_the_pull_request_it_names`) contradicted the new rule and is re-pinned as `test_a_bad_line_charges_nobody_it_cannot_tie_to_the_current_head`; the blocks case now lives with the current-head tests. (2) `RoundLog.read` quarantines a file it cannot decode: the file is moved aside under a `.corrupt-<UTC stamp>-<nanoseconds>` name beside the log (`os.replace`, then a directory fsync), the review that saw it reports the `round log file` omission once with the quarantine path named, and the next review starts fresh — a later read returns an empty log and the first append creates a new file. Killed there: the no-quarantine mutant dies on `test_an_undecodable_file_is_quarantined_and_the_next_read_starts_fresh`, on `test_an_unreadable_round_log_is_an_omission` (which now also pins the healed location), and end to end on `test_a_quarantined_log_heals_the_next_head`. A parent directory the process cannot reach cannot be repaired from here — the file stays and every attempt keeps its omission, and that is the operator's case: repair or remove `/data/rounds/<repo>-<pr>.jsonl` by hand, documented in `deploy-service.md`, which also now states the persistence truth the first wording understated — a charged line holds every later review of the pull request until the log is repaired, not just the next draft. **The Nits.** (N1) `round_log_path` casefolds the repository, matching the reader's case-insensitive key, so a case-only repository rename keeps the log instead of silently starting a fresh one under the missing-file-is-first-round rule (`test_a_case_renamed_repository_resolves_to_the_same_round_log`; killed by reverting the casefold); the `/`→`-` flattening stays as `run_dir`'s shared convention, harmless because per-record attribution filters other pull requests. (N2) The corrupt-log test's docstring now names causes that can produce such a file (disk corruption, a file something else wrote): this reader's own appends are ASCII (`json.dumps`'s `ensure_ascii` default), so a torn write leaves a truncated ASCII line that is `unattributed` and charges nobody, never `file_unreadable`. (N3) `RoundLog`'s class docstring no longer says the service builds no path — it names the unflagged CLI default and the per-PR service path; `rounds.py` is a source file, not generated or mirrored, so no regenerate or re-vendor was needed (`tooling.cli drift` clean).
**Update (2026-11-17, round-2 review of #732 — the quarantine narrowed, and the permission tests made root-independent).** *(agent, 2026-11-17; same offline rules; the round-2 protocol review returned APPROVE, these are its cleanups)* The quarantine fires only when the log's bytes cannot be decoded (`UnicodeError`, which carries `UnicodeDecodeError`): `read`'s `OSError` branch now renames nothing — a permission refusal, an unreachable parent, or a directory at the log's path stays exactly where it is, every review that tries keeps its named omission, and the repair stays the operator's — so a transient read failure can never move a healthy log out from under its path. Killed there: the quarantine-on-any-OSError mutant dies on `test_a_directory_at_the_log_path_stays_put_and_stays_unreadable` (re-pinned from round 1's quarantine-a-directory behavior), `test_a_permission_refusal_does_not_quarantine_and_the_file_stays_put` and `test_an_unreachable_log_does_not_quarantine_and_stays_put`. Those two replace round 1's `chmod(0)`-based tests, which a root runner ignores: each raises the failure through a fake scoped to the target path only (root-independent, verified by running the four quarantine tests under `sudo`), and the fragile `finally`-restore the round-1 locked test carried is gone with it. `deploy-service.md`'s recovery paragraph and `rounds.py`'s docstrings state the narrowed rule; the round-1 session-log entry's test count (3,116) was corrected in place to the suite's 3,119 — the entry is this branch's own, unmerged.
- **D75 — The service's posted COMMENT review carries the draft's verified findings (fixes #738; amends D46's and D53's body list) (2026-11-17).** *(agent, 2026-11-17, issue #738; found on #654 at badc60e5, where the live service posted a COMMENT review with no findings while its round log held seven verified findings (five Minor, two Nit); #717 keeps D68 and #711–#714 keep D69–D72, so this slice takes D75 — D71 and D72 stay reserved for #713 and #714)* Offline only: the tests use fakes and the real coordinator's `render_draft`; no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, no secret was read, and no deployed service setting, Fly config value or secret changed. **The failure.** `ReviewReport` had no findings field, both report builders (`_oneshot_report`, `_agentic_report`) read the draft's lens, coverage, limit and cost sections but skipped `## Findings (verified quotes only)`, and `render_comment` posted only the banner, the recommendation, the lenses, the files note, the limit stops and the cost — the defect has held since a3219f9b, so every service-posted review omitted exactly the part a reviewer reads first. **Choice.** `ReviewReport` gains `findings: tuple[str, ...]` (default `()`, so every positional construction and existing caller is unchanged), filled by one `_findings(draft)` helper both builders call: the draft's `Findings (verified quotes only)` bullets verbatim, each bullet the whole finding line the draft wrote — minted id, severity tags, location, lens, finding, fix — so severity and location survive into the post and no second parsing path exists. The `No verified findings.` line is prose, not a bullet, so the empty-findings draft yields none, and the one-shot draft has no findings section and yields none too. `render_comment` renders a **Findings** section right after the recommended-event line, ahead of the lenses, files, limit stops and cost, so a comment cut to the 65,000-character post limit keeps the findings; each item goes through `_section`'s `quote_untrusted`, the same inert code span every other untrusted field gets, so a model-authored finding line cannot break out of the quoting or open markup. **What this does not change.** The draft's `## Not verified (not reported as findings)` section is still not posted, and posts stay COMMENT-only (`comment_payload` and `comment_only` are unchanged); the recommendation line is unchanged. **Options.** Minting a structured findings field and re-rendering it for the post was rejected: the draft is the record the round log and the draft file agree on, and a second rendering path would drift from `_finding_line` the copied-parallel-way the standing authoring rules warn against. Posting the findings inside the existing sections was rejected: findings are the review's payload, not an annotation on coverage. **Cost.** None offline; live, a longer comment body bounded by the existing 65,000-character cut, whose placement the findings now survive. **Reversibility.** Drop the field and the section and the post is exactly the pre-D75 body; the draft is unchanged, so nothing on the child side moves. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_service_run.py`, `test_service_post.py`; each behavior a test that fails when reverted, each mutant applied by hand and killed): an agentic draft from the real `render_draft` with two verified findings yields both, in order, with severity and location in each line (`test_an_agentic_draft_with_findings_carries_them_into_the_report`; kills M1, `_findings` returning `()` always, which the two findings fail on); the `No verified findings.` draft's `findings == ()` assertions on both the agentic and the one-shot paths pin the empty case (the prose line is not a bullet, so `_bullets` already yields none); the posted body carries each finding quoted, and `None.` when there are none (`test_the_findings_reach_the_post_quoted_with_the_recommended_line` and `test_a_report_without_findings_posts_none`; the section-dropped mutant dies on both); a cut-to-limit comment keeps the findings (`test_the_findings_reach_the_post_quoted_with_the_recommended_line`'s second half; kills M3, the section rendered after the cost lines, which the cut trims away first); and a finding carrying markup cannot break out of the quoting (`test_a_hostile_finding_cannot_break_out_of_the_quoting`, kills M4, the findings posted raw outside the section's inert code spans). **Overlap.** The #732 round-2 work and the drafts #654/#655 touch `service_run.py` and nearby files; this slice edits `atlas_reviewer/service_run.py`, `atlas_reviewer/service_post.py`, the two test files, `atlas_reviewer/README.md` and `atlas_reviewer/deploy-service.md`, leaves `coordinator.py` untouched, and appends only to the docs logs.
**Update (2026-11-17, round-1 review of #739 — the findings' re-quote truncation and double-escaping fixed, plus the Minor and the wording Nit).** *(agent, 2026-11-17; same offline rules; the round-1 protocol review and the routine review both returned COMMENT on the same Major)* **The Major: the Findings section re-quoted each line through `quote_untrusted`'s 200-character default (`_section`), cutting real findings mid-line — the controller mints finding text at 600 and evidence at 1,200 characters (`coordinator.QUOTED_CHARS`/`EVIDENCE_CHARS`), so a realistic 448-character finding posted as 225 and lost its `Fix:` and `Evidence:` — and hid each line's inner code spans inside one outer span, whose backticks surfaced as `\u0060` and whose quotes as `\"`. The entry above's Cost paragraph said the body is "bounded by the existing 65,000-character cut, whose placement the findings now survive" and named neither loss mechanism; both were real. **The fix.** The Findings section no longer re-quotes: `_findings_section` posts each bullet as the controller rendered it, scrubbed and uncapped — a finding bullet is controller-assembled text whose every model-authored field is already one inert code span (the controller's own `quote_untrusted` escaped its backticks, markup and control characters at draft time) and whose scaffolding (minted id, severity tags, lens name, the `Fix:`/`Evidence:` labels) is controller text — so the inner spans stay readable, nothing is cut at 200, and the only bound is the 65,000-character cut the section's position already survives. Every other section keeps the 200 default, now pinned. **The Minor.** The round-0 post-side tests were a 135-character hand-written fixture and a real-draft test that never rendered the post, and a mutant raising the quote default to 100,000 survived all 84 of them. The new round-trip test goes from a real `render_draft` draft with a 400-plus-character finding through `summarize_draft` and `render_comment`, asserting `Fix:` and `Evidence:` survive, the inner spans render as spans (no `\u0060`, no `\"`), and no truncation marker appears; the 200 default itself is pinned on a 300-character limit stop. **The Nits.** The round-0 session-log entry's "minus the placeholder" wording, contradicted two paragraphs later, is corrected in place — the entry is this branch's own, unmerged — and the CRLF-heading and wrapped-bullet hardening is noted as unreachable in `_findings`'s docstring rather than added: the coordinator renders the draft with `"\n".join` (never `\r`) and every finding field is whitespace-collapsed before it lands in a bullet, so a draft violating either is not a draft this coordinator wrote, and a marker miss degrades to an empty Findings section, never a crash. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_service_run.py`, `test_service_post.py`; each mutant applied by hand and killed, each run with bytecode caching disabled after a same-size mutant restore was found serving a stale `__pycache__`): the round-trip test kills M6, the findings re-quoted at the 200 default (the `Fix:`/`Evidence:` assertions fail, and the hostile test's spans double-escape); the 200-cut pin kills M7, the default raised to 100,000; the secret test's new findings arm kills M8, the findings scrub dropped; and the round-0 kills were re-applied against the new structure — M1 (`_findings` always empty), M3 (the section moved after the cost lines), M5 (the section dropped) — all still dying. The hostile round-trip test re-checks the r1 list against the new path: an HTML comment, an injected backtick, a mention, a fake heading, an RTL override and the APPROVE/REQUEST_CHANGES words each stay span-bound and escaped, and the recommendation line is unchanged. 3,182 reviewer tests at 96.38%, 882 tooling at 95.20%, ruff/format/mypy/drift clean. Offline only.
**Update (2026-11-17, round-2 review of #739 — the section anchor pinned, the siblings escaping pinned, and the cut moved to a finding boundary; the round-2 protocol review and the routine review both APPROVE, with one shared Minor and two Nits).** *(agent, 2026-11-17; same offline rules)* **The Minor: `_findings` found its section through `section`'s unanchored, first-occurrence match, and posting raw is safe only because nothing before the real heading can carry the heading's literal line — true today, pinned by nothing.** The class fix went into `section` itself, not only the findings caller: the match is now anchored to a whole heading line (`\n## heading\n`, with a start-of-text fallback) and takes the **last** occurrence, for every section. A pre-section field the draft renders unquoted — the selection note, a lens note — that quotes a heading's literal line can no longer steal the extraction, and the real section is always the latest one the controller wrote; no quoted field can carry a whole line (`quote_untrusted` collapses whitespace), so a doubled heading needs unquoted prose. Killed there: the revert to the unanchored first-occurrence find dies on `test_a_pre_findings_field_quoting_the_heading_cannot_steal_the_section` — a selection note carrying the literal `## Findings (verified quotes only)` line plus a fake bullet, through the real `render_draft`; pre-fix the report's findings are the fake bullet and the post shows it, post-fix only the real finding survives end to end. **Nit 1: the minted `siblings` string's draft-time escaping was unpinned** — a mutant dropping its `quote_untrusted` in `_finding_line` survived all 86 tests. `test_the_siblings_field_is_escaped_like_every_other_model_field` builds a finding whose siblings carry a backtick, an HTML comment and a mention, and pins the escaped span form (`\u0060b.py:9\u0060`, `\u003c!--`), killing the mutant. **Nit 2: the 65,000-character hard cut could land inside a finding's inner code span and leave an unclosed backtick run that swallows the rest of the comment.** `render_comment` now drops whole findings from the end — the draft lists findings most severe first — until what remains fits, and the Findings section names the drops (`[1 of 3 findings cut to fit the post limit]` after the kept bullets; `[all N findings cut to fit the post limit]` when every finding was dropped, never a false none); a body that still overflows with no finding left to drop ends in the plain hard cut that predates findings. Killed there: the revert to the hard cut dies on `test_the_cut_drops_whole_findings_and_never_splits_a_span` (backtick parity even across the post, the kept findings whole, the third absent, the drop named), the all-dropped-note revert to a bare none dies on `test_the_findings_reach_the_post_whole_with_the_recommended_line`'s tail-overflow case, and removing the partial-drop note dies on the same boundary test. All mutants were run with bytecode caching disabled (`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`), per the round-1 stale-`__pycache__` lesson. 3,185 reviewer tests at 96.41%, 882 tooling at 95.20%, ruff/format/mypy/drift clean. Offline only.
**Update (2026-11-17, round-3 review of #739 — the label echo escaped at the source, the section anchor pinned, and the fallback cut moved to a line boundary; the protocol's round-3 review APPROVEs).** *(agent, 2026-11-17, on the branch rebased onto main 36842631, which carries #654's merge; same offline rules)* **The Minor (the r2 anchor's own hazard, closed at its source): the re-assessment judge interpolated the model-authored `revises` label raw into its refusal message**, which becomes `outcome.detail`, which `retry_reasons` echoes and the lens line joins raw into the coverage section — a section that comes after Findings, where this entry's r2 last-occurrence `section` would favor a later forgery; a label of 32 characters or fewer could carry `## Context omissions` plus a bullet, putting fake limit stops into the post. The judge now reprs the label, as the withdrawn path already does, so the detail stays one line with the label's newlines escaped. Killed there: the raw-interpolation revert dies on the judge-level pin (`test_a_hostile_revises_label_is_reprd_not_interpolated`) and on the end-to-end test (`test_a_hostile_label_cannot_forge_a_section_in_the_posted_review`: the real refusal → detail → draft → post; the draft keeps exactly one real `## Context omissions` heading, the section reader takes it, and the post carries no fake stop and no forged heading). The cap itself is pinned independently: `test_a_label_over_the_prior_cap_is_refused_by_length_and_never_echoed` refuses a 33-to-64-character label carrying the Findings heading with the length message alone (which never echoes the label), so no label length can forge a heading through any refusal path — killed there, the mutant raising `PRIOR_LABEL_CHARS` from 32 to 64, which had survived every earlier test. **The nits.** The r2 whole-line anchor is itself pinned: a post-findings line that merely ends with the Findings heading's literal text (the ceiling note, rendered unquoted) is not a heading (`test_a_post_findings_line_ending_in_the_heading_cannot_steal_the_section`; the unanchored-rfind mutant that would take it as the last occurrence dies there). And the hard-cut fallback now lands on a line boundary, not mid-line — every rendered item is one line of whole code spans, so the line the plain cut would have split is dropped whole (`test_the_hard_cut_fallback_lands_on_a_line_boundary`; the mid-line-cut mutant dies on the last kept line's integrity). 3,202 reviewer tests at 96.43% and 882 tooling at 95.20% on the rebased head, one measured pair each; ruff/format/mypy/drift clean. Offline only.

- **D76 — A later clean run of a lens supersedes its earlier NOT RUN entries for coverage; the failure stays in the record (fixes #692) (2026-11-17).** *(agent, 2026-11-17, issue #692; found in the Nov 14 model-trials rerun (Haiku #640), where `sweeping-for-security` was NOT RUN after a Sprites 502 and its continuation ran it to clean as entry L13 while the original NOT RUN entry L2 stayed in `lenses`; #717 keeps D68 and #711–#714 keep D69–D72, so this slice takes D76 — D71 and D72 stay reserved for #713 and #714; the parallel #693 PR may take D77)* Offline only: the tests use the scripted reviewer, `LocalSprites`, the fakes and the real draft renderer; no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, no secret was read, and no deployed service setting, Fly config value or secret changed. **The failure.** `review_lenses` keeps one `LensResult` per run, so a lens whose first run never started and whose continuation ran clean appears twice in `lenses` — NOT RUN, then clean — and `_lens_gap`'s per-entry check (`any(_blocks_coverage(result))`) read the NOT RUN entry as a standing gap: `coverage_complete` false, the draft's REVIEW INCOMPLETE banner up, the event held at COMMENT, and the service post's "Lenses not run" listing a lens that had in fact run. **Choice.** The coverage rule is now per lens, not per entry: a lens blocks coverage only when **none** of its entries is clean (`coordinator._lens_gap`, via `_entries_by_lens` grouping, in both the scoped and the no-scope paths). Supersession is by presence, not by position — a later clean run supersedes an earlier NOT RUN, and a later NOT RUN does not undo an earlier clean run's coverage — because neither direction can be "the latest entry wins" without breaking the other. The per-entry criteria are unchanged (`_blocks_coverage` still reads state, attestation and the unread-only carve-out; the pre-gate `changed_files is None` check stays per entry), D42's per-file gate is unchanged — `_pending_by_lens` already unions a lens's entries, so a clean continuation must still have read the failed run's whole scope before the unread gap clears — and the runner gate is unchanged: a runner that did not complete is not approved away by a clean lens, exactly as before. The status line's lens counts stay per entry, so the superseded failure keeps its `not_run` count; the record is not rewritten, only its coverage weight. **Findings are untouched.** Minting still walks every attested entry, so D61's and D73's keep-findings rules are not regressed: supersession is a coverage rule, never a findings one — a superseded entry's carried verified findings stay in the verdict's set. **The record.** `render_draft` marks every non-clean entry of a lens that also has a clean run with a controller note appended last: `; recovered by L<n>'s clean run`, naming the recovering entry. The note is controller text outside the quoted reason, and the service reads the marker off the line's **end** (`service_run._AGENTIC_RECOVERED`, anchored `$`), so a quoted reason carrying the same words — which `quote_untrusted` preserves verbatim inside its code span — can never forge a recovery: a forged copy inside the reason is always followed by the closing backtick, never at the line's end. `_agentic_coverage` sends a recovered line to neither `run` nor `not_run` from that line: the lens is already run through its clean line, and the whole ledger line is carried among the limit notes, so the posted COMMENT keeps the failure ("Limit stops") while "Lenses not run" stays truthful and the eval harness's parity gate does not fail a lens that ran. **Options.** Dropping the superseded entry from `lenses` was rejected: the entry is the record of what happened, the status counts would go quiet, and every consumer re-deriving per-entry facts would lose it. Latest-entry-decides ordering was rejected: it makes a later NOT RUN undo an earlier clean run, the mirror of the defect. Listing the recovered lens under "Lenses not run" with a recovered suffix was rejected: a lens that ran clean is not a lens that did not run, and the parity gate reads that section as failure. **Cost.** None offline; live, none — the computation is over entries the review already holds. **Reversibility.** Revert `_lens_gap`'s two grouped checks to the per-entry `any(...)` and the review is exactly the pre-D76 gate (the recovered draft keeps its note, now merely non-load-bearing); drop the note and the marker and the service falls back to reading every NOT RUN line as not-run. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_coordinator.py`, `test_service_run.py`; each behavior a test that fails when reverted, each mutant applied by hand and killed): the superseded NOT RUN gives complete coverage and the unsuperseded one does not (`test_a_superseded_not_run_no_longer_blocks_coverage`, kills M1, the supersede check dropped back to per-entry `any`); a later NOT RUN does not undo an earlier clean run (`test_a_later_not_run_does_not_undo_an_earlier_clean_runs_coverage`, kills M5, last-entry-decides ordering — and M1's per-entry form is first-entry-decides for the other direction, killed by the same pair); one lens's clean run never covers another lens's failure (`test_one_lens_clean_run_does_not_cover_another_lens_failure`, kills M2, the grouping collapsed to one bucket, ignoring lens identity); the failure record survives in the rendered draft with the recovered note (`test_a_recovered_entry_keeps_its_failure_and_its_note_in_the_draft`, and the end-to-end `test_a_later_clean_run_supersedes_an_earlier_not_run_for_coverage` — the real refused-create → NOT RUN → continuation-clean flow through `LocalSprites`, killing M3, the note dropped from the draft); the incomplete banner does not name a recovered entry (`test_the_incomplete_headline_does_not_name_a_recovered_entry`); and the service report agrees — the recovered lens is run, not not-run, with the failure carried among the limit stops and into the posted COMMENT (`test_a_recovered_not_run_is_run_in_the_report_with_the_failure_kept`, `test_a_recovered_not_run_is_visible_in_the_posted_comment`, both killing M4, the recovered branch dropped, and M3 end to end through `render_draft` → `summarize_draft` → `render_comment`), while a NOT RUN with no clean run still parses as not-run with its reason (`test_a_not_run_without_a_clean_run_is_still_not_run_in_the_report`). **Overlap.** The parallel #693 PR touches `atlas_reviewer/bridge.py` and possibly lens retry code in the coordinator; this slice edits `atlas_reviewer/coordinator.py` (the coverage computation, the headline's named gaps and the draft's lens line only — no retry, ledger or bridge code), `atlas_reviewer/service_run.py` (the coverage parser), the two test files, `atlas_reviewer/README.md`, `atlas_reviewer/deploy-service.md`, and appends only to the docs logs.
**Update (2026-11-17, round-1 review of #744 — the scoped path's carve-out fixed, the anchor pinned, and the cross-references added).** *(agent, 2026-11-17; same offline rules; the round-1 protocol review, Brandon's routine review and the atlas-reviewer-service review all returned COMMENT on the same Major)* **The Major: the scoped path could approve a lens that never ran clean.** This entry said "a lens blocks coverage only when none of its entries is clean", but the scoped path implemented "none of its entries blocks" through `_blocks_coverage`, whose unread-only carve-out (PARTIAL, attested, `FILES_UNREAD` only) returns False for a partial that merely left a file unread -- so such a partial excused a sibling that failed for a real cause (the reviewer's repro: an unread-only partial plus a continuation that read everything and failed `QUOTES_FAILED` reached `coverage_complete` with no clean entry, APPROVE where origin/main held COMMENT). Both paths now use one per-lens predicate (`_uncovered_lens`: at least one clean entry, presence not position, per lens), `_blocks_coverage` is retired, and the unread-only partial's files gate through `_unread_paths` as before -- the stated rule is now the implemented rule in both paths. Killed there: the carve-out-restored mutant dies on `test_an_unread_only_partial_does_not_excuse_a_failed_sibling` (with the positive control `test_a_clean_run_still_covers_an_unread_only_partial_and_a_failure` guarding against over-correction), and the per-entry-`any` mutant (supersession dropped) still dies on the supersede pair. **Minor: the `$` anchor pinned.** `test_a_quoted_reason_cannot_forge_the_recovered_marker` builds a NOT RUN lens with no clean run whose quoted reason carries the marker's words and pins it staying in `lenses_not_run`, out of the limit stops, and out of the post's recovered note; the unanchored mutant (M6, which had survived all 179 round-0 tests) dies there. **Minor: the vacuous assertion** in `test_a_recovered_not_run_is_run_in_the_report_with_the_failure_kept` is now the exact complete-coverage note text, so `incomplete` can no longer satisfy it. **Cross-references.** D40, D42, D44 and D70 now carry `Update (2026-11-17, D76 ...)` notes.
**Superseded sentences in this entry's original body (2026-11-17, round-2 review of #744).** Two sentences above describe the round-0 code this PR no longer ships, and the round-1 correction supersedes them; they stand as the record of what was believed at round 0, because this entry is append-only (`test_decision_updates_are_appended`). First, the Choice paragraph's "The per-entry criteria are unchanged (`_blocks_coverage` still reads state, attestation and the unread-only carve-out; the pre-gate `changed_files is None` check stays per entry)": the scoped path's `_blocks_coverage` use was the round-1 Major's defect, the carve-out is retired, and of that sentence only the pre-gate `changed_files is None` check remaining per entry still holds. Second, the Reversibility note's "Revert `_lens_gap`'s two grouped checks to the per-entry `any(...)` and the review is exactly the pre-D76 gate (the recovered draft keeps its note, now merely non-load-bearing)": the current revert path is to give either check back its per-entry form -- `any(_blocks_coverage(entry) ...)` scoped, `any(entry.state is not CLEAN ...)` no-scope -- which restores the pre-D76 gate and leaves the recovered notes rendering; drop `_uncovered_lens` and `_clean_runs` with them. The marker wording itself now lives in one place (`notes.RECOVERED_NOTE` and its pattern) rather than the copied f-string and regex those paragraphs described.

- **D49 — A failed model call is sent once more before the lens fails (2026-11-12).** *(user, 2026-11-12)* A live run had a Bedrock reply with `finish_reason=error` at turn 20, and the lens failed with `MODEL_ERROR`, which is not retried. **Decision:** a model call that raises a `BoundaryError` is sent again, with the same request, up to `call_retries` times within the turn. `call_retries` is 0 to 3 and defaults to 1. `--call-retries` and `ATLAS_CALL_RETRIES` set it. The loop waits 2s times the number of failed calls before the next call (2s, then 4s, then 6s; the backoff is deterministic, with no jitter — at most a pool of 16 lenses retries against one shared provider incident, and every send is reserved against the same dollar cap, so the synchronized wave jitter exists to break is bounded on both sides), then checks the lens and review clocks and the turn cap again. Composed with D58's one timeout retry, one logical turn's request can be sent up to 2 × (`call_retries` + 1) times per attempt — 4 at the default, 8 at `call_retries` 3 — because the timeout-retried turn runs its own call-retry budget; every send is separately reserved, counted and clock-gated, and the retry is named on the resend's span (`atlas.call_retry`), not in the lens outcome. Each call prices and reserves its worst case through `Spend` and counts toward `max_calls`. A failed call's reservation stays committed, because the provider may have billed it. A refused limit, a refused worst-case price and a BYOK refusal are not retried. When every call fails, the lens fails with `MODEL_ERROR`, and the detail lists each error. A retry that succeeds is not recorded in the lens outcome. Its extra call shows in the call count. `MODEL_ERROR` stays failing and is still not a cause for a new attempt. Offline-tested. No live model call was made for this decision.

- **D50 — A call the reservation cannot cover whole is sent with the output cap it can cover (2026-11-12).** *(user, 2026-11-12)* A live driver stopped about $2 short of its reservation because it priced each call at the full 128k output cap. The product does the same: with no `--max-output-tokens`, a call's output cap is the model's catalog completion limit, and D43 prices that whole cap at the completion rate plus the reasoning rate. **Decision:** when `Spend.reserve` refuses a call on `reservation_usd`, the loop asks the model for the largest output cap whose worst case fits what the reservation has left, at the catalog prices of that moment. If that cap is at least `min_output_tokens`, the call is priced again with that cap, reserved, and sent with it as `max_tokens`. The reasoning hint is lowered to half that cap when the previous hint does not fit it, so the reply still has the other half. The worst case still bounds the call, because the provider cannot return more output than `max_tokens`. `min_output_tokens` defaults to 16,000, must be at least 2, and is set with `--min-output-tokens` or `ATLAS_MIN_OUTPUT_TOKENS`. Below it, the call stops on `reservation_usd` as before, and the stop names the cap the remainder covered and `min_output_tokens`. A fitted call that fails names its fitted cap in the error. A call that fits whole is unchanged. D43's rule, the per-call caps, and the one-shot `review` are unchanged. Offline-tested. No live model call was made for this decision.

- **D77 — The bridge session budget is per lens attempt, and repeated session-limit refusals stop the reading, not the delivery (fixes #693; amends D39) (2026-11-17).** *(agent, 2026-11-17, issue #693; found in the Nov 14 model-trials rerun, where eight DeepSeek #661 retries, DeepSeek #640's decision-lifecycle retry at 16 `OVER_SESSION_LIMIT` refusals, and one Kimi #640 retry started nearly out of tool calls and ended `TOOL_GAP` and `FILES_UNREAD`, every duplicate-call nudge earned from re-issuing refused calls; #654's call resend and #740's output fitting keep their behavior, and this slice stays out of #692's coverage-computation surface)* `SessionLimits` (`bridge.py`) bounds one attempt, not the runner session (D39's caps re-scoped per attempt): the coordinator calls `Bridge.begin_attempt(lens, number)` before each attempt it runs on a bridge, the attempt's call and shown-output counters start over, and a lens's retry runs on the full 64-call / 1 MiB budget its predecessor spent. The refusal text names the attempt ("all 64 calls of this attempt made (lens attempt 2)"). **Repeated refusals stop the reading, not the delivery.** Once `SESSION_REFUSAL_LIMIT` (3) `OVER_SESSION_LIMIT` results have accumulated in the attempt -- cumulative by construction, because once either cap is spent every later call refuses -- the turn's results go back to the model and the attempt gets one finalize turn asking it to finalize with what it has, and the reply the model then gives is judged like any final reply. The tool schemas stay on the finalize request: a request carrying tool history next to an empty tools array is a shape the pinned Bedrock Converse route is documented to reject -- tool history requires a toolConfig (verify; documented across OpenAI-to-Bedrock translation layers' issue trackers, not yet probed on this repo's own live route) -- which the adapter now refuses before any request -- a tool-less finalize turn would fail the call as a non-retryable model error and lose the findings the turn exists to deliver. The spent bridge refuses whatever the model still calls, so a re-issue is a logged refusal, bounded by the turn cap, the dollar reservation and the wall clocks, and cannot restart the stop -- it fires once per attempt. The stop (`limit: session_limits`, cause `TOOL_GAP`) is built once at that boundary, its configured text names both caps per attempt and its observed text names the lens, the attempt, the refusal count and the last refusal's reason, and it rides on the delivered outcome's `limit_hit` (when a later stop -- the turn cap landing on the finalize turn, say -- claims the outcome instead, the session stop's message is kept in the outcome's detail, so it never vanishes without a trace) -- so the ledger's per-attempt `limit` record, the draft's status line, the round log's `limit_stop` field (rendered back into the next round's first turn; a pre-field line parses with an empty stop) and the service report's limit stops all carry it. A constant, not a knob: the budget itself is the setting, and `SETTINGS["session_limits"]` says so. **Alternatives considered:** ending the attempt outright at the threshold -- the round-1 cut, caught by every round-1 review -- throws away a tool batch straddling the budget before the model can reply, turning a case where the model delivers its findings (attested, as on the pre-#693 code) into an unretried `UNACKNOWLEDGED` attempt with none; cutting only after the model has seen a refusal batch and re-issued anyway keeps the findings but still grants one more full turn of re-issuing, the behavior the issue names; a tool-less finalize request -- the shape between the first cut and this one -- fails on the pinned route for the provider shape above. **Accepted cost:** the finalize turn is one more reserved, counted, clock-gated model call that may carry re-issued refused calls the bridge logs, and an attempt that finalizes with files unread stays partial with `FILES_UNREAD`, exactly as the same shape ends on the pre-#693 code; the stall detector's own nudge does not also fire on the stop's turn, so the model is asked to finalize once. **Bounds unchanged elsewhere.** The dollar ceiling (D68, about $10) and the wall clocks still bound the whole review -- the per-attempt budget bounds tool calls, which were never the review-wide `max_calls`'s object -- and a PR whose diff exceeds 1 MiB still cannot come clean in one attempt, so D42's structural rule stands. **Not model-aware, deliberately:** the Nov 14 numbers do not support a per-model scaling rule yet, so the cap is uniform. **Retry policy follows the delivered reply:** an attested finalize reply settles the lens partial (its causes carry the refusals); an unparsed one is retryable like any unparsed reply, and the retry begins whole -- which is the fix's point. **Revisit** with the next model-trials rerun: if finalize replies come back unusable often enough to matter, or a model's honest work needs more or fewer than 64 calls an attempt, the threshold and the cap are the two numbers to re-measure; the first live run also settles the toolConfig (verify) above -- a finalize reply that still calls tools, or the tools-empty shape turning out to be accepted after all, is the evidence that closes it. Offline only: the tests use the shipped fixture, `LocalSprites`, the scripted reviewer and the fakes; no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, no secret was read, and no deployed service setting, Fly config value or secret changed.

- **D78 — This repo's CI runs on GitHub-hosted runners for every event; the self-hosted fleet is out of this repo's critical path (2026-11-17).** *(user, 2026-11-17)* Brandon decided this at 5:43 PM CT on 2026-11-17, with main's `push` runs stuck `queued` behind the fleet's downed runner since roughly 3 AM CT — the exact single-point-of-failure failure mode the self-hosted guide's own [Accepted Risks](self-hosted-runners.md#accepted-risks) section had recorded. **Decision:** every event of every workflow under `.github/workflows/` runs on GitHub-hosted runners — `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`, left floating rather than pinned to a dated image, because every tool the gate enforces is already version-pinned independently of the runner OS (the hash-pinned `requirements.txt`, exact-pinned pip-tools/pip-audit/pip-licenses, checksum-verified upstream shellcheck/actionlint tarballs), so an image refresh changes the VM, not the gates. Cost is not a factor: the repo is public, so hosted minutes are free — the billing argument in the runner guide's ["Why self-hosted at all?"](self-hosted-runners.md#why-self-hosted-at-all) never applied to this repo. **Workarounds retired with the persistent hardware** (each explained in the migrating PR): #366's fresh-venv install (its precondition — site-packages accumulating across jobs on one shared interpreter — cannot occur on a per-run VM; the image-baked tool-cache interpreter can still in principle satisfy a pin at the pinned version, so the install keeps `--require-hashes --ignore-installed` — pip resolves and downloads every pin regardless of what the interpreter already holds, keeping every hash verified every run; correction from round-1 review of #746, which caught that the first draft's "nothing is ever already-satisfied" overclaimed), the `cache: 'pip'` event-conditional (#475/#589), the fleet's arch-agnostic `runs-on:` notes, and the `$RUNNER_TEMP`-vs-`$HOME` persistent-host defenses. The hash-verified `pip install --require-hashes` gate itself stays. **Concurrency**: `cancel-in-progress` stays `pull_request`-only, and the group now carries the event name (`ci-<event>-<ref>`) so the weekly schedule never shares a group with main pushes — the schedule resolves to the default branch's ref, so a ref-only group would put them together. The first draft's rationale claimed each push to main keeps one complete recorded run; that was false: a concurrency group holds at most one running and one pending run, and a newer run cancels an older *pending* one regardless of `cancel-in-progress` — observed live in this repo, where push run 44087837195 was cancelled at 2026-11-17T23:41:36Z, the instant run 44181631062 queued (correction from round-1 review of #746). What the config actually buys: the scheduled pip-audit (#379) can neither be cancelled while in progress nor superseded while pending by a mid-week push; superseded *pending* main pushes are accepted, because the newest head's result is the one that matters and any head that reaches the front of its group still gets a complete recorded run; PR runs keep the cancel. **What D78 does not close:** #471's residual gap — GitHub evaluates a plain `pull_request` workflow from the PR's own head, so a hostile PR can still edit its own `runs-on:` for its own run; the protection is a Settings value ("Require approval for all outside collaborators"), not this file. Leaving the fleet removes the persistent-hardware exposure #366/#475 defended against and takes `push`/`schedule` off the fleet too, but the in-tree-editability point stands exactly as #471's corrected analysis left it. **Docs and guards:** `docs/self-hosted-runners.md` stays as the fleet how-to for the repos that still self-host, with this repo's state updated in its header and Per-repo notes; `tests/test_ci_runners_are_github_hosted.py` replaces the #471-era `test_ci_pull_request_runner_is_hosted.py` (which pinned the split shape this decision removes) and globs every `*.y*ml` workflow file rather than reading `ci.yml` alone — a second workflow file carrying a self-hosted label must fail CI, not slip past a single-file guard (round-1 review correction, Minor 3, confirmed by a probe); `test_ci_require_hashes_targets_fresh_venv.py` becomes the slimmer `test_ci_require_hashes_install.py`, which pins the `--ignore-installed` flag the install needs once the venv is gone. **Revisit** if the repo ever goes private (hosted minutes start billing), if a job needs hardware hosted runners don't offer, or if a workflow wants a self-hosted label again — that last one reopens this decision rather than re-adding a label silently.

- **D79 — A push that lands while a review runs is decided keep-or-cancel per push; the default keeps the review and queues the new head as its follow-up (issue #702; amends D48's and D53's supersede-on-a-newer-head statements under the default policy) (2026-11-18).** *(agent, 2026-11-18, issue #702; the owner's direction is quoted in the issue: decide per push, let a review finish and follow with a delta review when the new commits only merge the base branch, only touch docs or tests, or touch no file the review is still reading; cancel and restart only when the push changes code under review and the running review is early — roughly under half its lenses or budget spent; Brandon confirmed on Nov 16, 2026 that the default is keep, cancel-and-restart is the narrower exception with a configurable threshold, carry-forward respects D73's never-silently-dropped rule, delta reviews stay inside D68's cost machinery, and posts stay COMMENT-only)* Offline only: the tests use fakes, the shipped fixture shapes and the real queue and worker; no live model, Sprite or GitHub call was made, no secret was read, and no deployed service setting, Fly config value or secret changed — flipping the running app's policy is a separate approved deploy step. **The failure.** The queue superseded any active job of a pull request on every newer head, so a review in flight was cancelled and its spend lost on exactly the pushes that carry the least review risk — the Nov 14 sample was mostly merges of main and docs or test-only fixes, and nearly all of the first review's findings still applied afterward. **Choice.** The decision lives in the service parent, where the push is observed (`service._admit_to_queue`), as a pure rule over three inputs (`head_change.py`): what the push changes (`classify_push`, from GitHub's comparison of the two heads plus the pull request's base — every commit whose second parent is not `merge_base(base, new)` reads as code, so a feature-branch merge is never a base merge, and a cut commit list reads as code too, because the benign kinds are claimed on evidence and never by default; an empty, untruncated range changes nothing the review reads and is benign, which the first wording misstated (round-1 review of #747, F6)); what the review is still reading (the review child records a cumulative `progress` telemetry event into its run's events file after every settled lens and once when its lens loop ends — lenses settled of the planned count, spend against the derived reservation, and the paths the lenses without a clean run are still reading, capped at `READING_CHARS` because a truncated list reads shorter, which keeps a push rather than cancels); and how early the review is (`decide`: the lens fraction and the spend fraction against `ATLAS_HEAD_CHANGE_CANCEL_FRACTION`, default 0.5 — early on either measure as first written; corrected round-1 (below) to early on **both** measures). The order is the owner's: benign keeps however late; code-and-early cancels; otherwise keeps with the follow-up queued. A review already posting is never cancelled. A push no evidence could classify — a failed fetch, or no credentials to fetch with — keeps the review and records the gap: the cancel branch needs the same evidence standard the benign kinds do. **The queue.** `Board.enqueue` keeps its supersede contract (it is the cancel path and the pre-#702 behavior under `ATLAS_HEAD_CHANGE_POLICY=cancel`, which restores it whole); the keep path is `Board.keep_head`, which records the new head as the pull request's **pending follow-up** — persisted in the queue file, stale-checked against it like an installed head, never holding an active slot — and supersedes only the queued or post-pending jobs an enqueue would have. When the running review settles, the worker stores its terminal state and queues the pending head in one lock and one save (`Board.settle`): a success queues it as a **delta review** with `delta_from` set to the settled head (`PrEvent.delta_from`, persisted on the job and written into the child's event file; the delta review itself is the stacked PR that closes #702), a failure queues it as an ordinary review, because there is no finished review to build on. A queued follow-up a newer push supersedes passes its `delta_from` on to the replacement, so the chain stays anchored on the last head a review actually finished. **Measure first.** Every decision is one `head change` telemetry event (both heads, kind, fractions, reason, and the classification gap when there is one); every settled review logs a `review cost` event — from the draft's cost lines when it finished, from the last progress record when it was cancelled or failed (read before the run directory is dropped), `measured: unknown` when neither exists — and the materialized follow-up is a `follow-up queued` event. All are tagged with the heads, so the threshold's tuning numbers and the cancel-versus-finish cost comparison can be read straight out of `service.jsonl`. **Configurability.** `ATLAS_HEAD_CHANGE_POLICY` (`keep` | `cancel`, validated at boot with the mode setting's fail-closed precedent) and `ATLAS_HEAD_CHANGE_CANCEL_FRACTION` (0.0–1.0, validated at boot); the pure `decide` function is the seam a later policy adjusts through, the way D68's `resolve_ceiling` is. **Not done here.** The delta review itself (the stacked PR): range diff with merge-from-base content excluded, carry-forward that re-anchors prior findings through the quote checker and marks the new commits' fixes resolved, dedupe, and the post citing the earlier review. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_head_change.py`, `test_service_receive.py`, `test_coordinator.py`; each behavior a test that fails when reverted, each named mutant applied by hand): the classification kinds (base merge, feature merge, regular commit, docs and tests, cut list, unknown base side — the unknown-side mutant dies on `test_a_merge_whose_base_side_cannot_be_identified_reads_as_code`), the decision order (benign keeps, early code cancels — early on both measures as the round-1 correction pins — past-threshold keeps, missing progress reads as early, unclassifiable keeps, posting never cancels — the missing-progress mutant dies on `test_missing_progress_reads_as_early`), the progress reader (rotation order, garbage lines contribute nothing, the empty reading field resets), the live classification reads (three reads, the diverged-history guard skipping the base read, failures propagating), and the service flows (kept and materialized with its delta base, cancelled on early code, kept on docs, kept on a classification failure with the gap recorded, posting kept, pending-head redelivery a duplicate, queued-delta `delta_from` inheritance, failure materializing a full review, pending surviving a restart, the cancel policy restoring the blanket supersede, the progress record letting a late code push keep its review, and the three cost events' shapes). The coordinator's progress emission is pinned end to end through a real `JsonlSink` and read back with the real reader.

**Correction (2026-11-18, round-1 review of #747).** The protocol review's three Majors and the Minors, fixed on the PR branch; each pinned by a named test or a killed mutant. (F1) The "early" test was `lens_fraction < threshold or spend_fraction < threshold`, and the spend fraction's denominator is D68's derived worst-case **reservation** — it measures budget headroom, not work done — so a review with every lens settled could be cancelled while sitting at a third of its reservation, exactly in the window (between the lens loop's end and the post) where a cancelled review's draft is never posted and the whole spend is re-spent: the waste D79's own failure paragraph exists to prevent. The rule is now **early on both measures** (`and`): finishing is the default, cancelling the narrow exception, and the spend measure can no longer cancel completed work by itself; the threshold stays configurable, and the reason string no longer contradicts itself. The spend gauge itself is recorded here as read: `settled_usd / reservation_usd` — the directive's "actual spend" read as the NUMERATOR (what calls actually cost, `settled_usd`, as opposed to `committed_usd`'s worst-case reservations in flight, which the Spend tracker distinguishes exactly this way), with the denominator left as D68's derived reservation rather than re-gauged against the absolute ceiling; directionally safe either way, because the derived reservation is clamped under the ceiling, so the implemented measure cancels less than a ceiling gauge would — erring toward the owner's keep default — and the F1 case is kept under both readings. The Revisit clause covers Brandon weighing in on the denominator ("100% of its lenses … under the 50% threshold"). The literal parenthetical in the issue ("under half its lenses **or** budget spent") supports the OR reading — this was an ambiguity resolved the wrong way, owned here as such rather than re-read out of the issue. Pinned by `test_a_review_early_on_both_measures_cancels`, `test_a_review_with_every_lens_settled_is_kept_however_cheap` (the reproduced case), `test_a_review_past_the_threshold_on_either_measure_is_kept`, and `test_a_fraction_exactly_at_the_threshold_is_not_early` (the `<`→`<=` mutant, which survived the first round's tests — F5). (F2) `keep_head`'s race branch — the review settled between `head_change_candidate`'s lock and `keep_head`'s, up to three 5-second GitHub reads later — answered `queued` and installed nothing: an acknowledged delivery GitHub will not redeliver, dropping the pushed head while saying it was queued. The branch now installs the head under the same lock and save (an ordinary review, no delta base), subject to the same active-slot cap as `enqueue`, so the acknowledgment is true. Pinned by `test_a_kept_head_with_no_running_review_is_installed_not_dropped` and `test_a_delivery_through_the_race_path_queues_the_new_head`. (F3) A deployment with no usable App credentials got `classifier=None` and the `head change` event was written status-ok with no kind and no gap — indistinguishable from a normally-classified keep, and the cancel branch silently dead, breaking the Done criterion "its decision is diagnosable in telemetry". The classifier's builder now returns the reason there is none; the boot log names it once ("head-change policy is keep with NO classifier …"), and every unclassifiable decision records the gap with an error status, so a deployment that lost its credentials is visible in `service.jsonl`. Pinned by `test_a_no_classifier_decision_records_the_gap_with_an_error_status`. The Minors: F4, the cut-commit-list guard's named mutant survived its named test (the test's input was a regular commit, killing the guard only by accident of its paths) — the test's input is now an all-base-merges cut list, which the dropped guard would read as merge-only; F5, the at-threshold boundary pinned (above); F6, D79 and the session log no longer say an empty commit list reads as code (the code and the README were right; the decision record was wrong in both places it appeared); F7, a kept head stranded by the crash window (the process dying between `finish` persisting and `take_pending` running), the materializing save's failure window, or a full queue at materialize time is now recovered — `Board.recover_pending` installs it as an ordinary review (what it should have built on is not knowable there), called at boot and on every worker pass, with the `follow-up queued` telemetry naming it, and `take_pending` no longer appends past the active-slot cap (F13) but leaves the head recorded for the recovery; pinned by `test_a_stranded_pending_head_is_recovered_at_boot`, `test_a_pending_head_on_a_full_queue_waits_for_the_recovery`, and `test_the_worker_loop_recovers_a_stranded_pending_head`; F8, the `head change` event carries the decision's duration, and a classification that ran past its transport timeout is flagged `timed_out`, so GitHub-side delivery failures correlate with classifications (`test_a_timing_out_classification_is_marked_on_the_event`); F9, the PEM normalization lives in one place (`github_auth.normalize_pem_key`, used by the poster, the CLI and the classifier) and `service_post.repository_names` is public — no underscore reach-through; F10, `_record_progress` compares the step name with `==`, not string identity; F11, this correction is D79's Revisit trigger's company — **Revisit**: the threshold (0.5) and the both-measures rule rest on one sample (the Nov 14 pushes); re-measure on the first weeks of live cost/decision telemetry, and reopen when the recorded decisions show keeps whose follow-up deltas would have been cheaper as cancels, or cancels whose reviews were cheap; F12, the reading-list cap and the reader's tail-seek are pinned by real tests (the old truncation test's input parsed to one path and pinned nothing); F14, the test-count claims corrected (15 service flows, not 13); F15, the dead `PROGRESS_SOURCE` removed and `pending_head` replaced by `pending_heads`, which the boot log reads; F16, the duplicated `_threading` alias import removed; F17, a comparison without a `files` array is refused rather than read as empty (which would claim docs-or-tests vacuously), and a file list at GitHub's comparison cap is treated as cut; F19, the classifier's docstring no longer claims GitHub redelivers a timed-out delivery — D53 records the opposite, and the honest justification is that the decision is made and recorded before the response, so a late answer costs the delivery's acknowledgment, never the review's correctness; F20, the progress record's `lenses_done` field is the one source for the settled count, read back directly instead of recounted over lens names. The Nits noted: F18, the base-merge classification's evidence comes from the same party the review judges — a crafted merge commit can present a code push as a merge of the base, and every consequence lands on the keep side (the follow-up reviews the new head in full), so the cost is at most a missed early cancel; stated here as the trust model's boundary, not fixed with ancestry checks. The older records (D48, D53, D74) carry `Update (2026-11-18, D79)` notes pointing here, so no entry states the reversed supersede as live.

**Update (2026-11-18, the delta review — the stacked PR that closes #702).** *(agent, 2026-11-18, on the delta-review branch; the same offline rules as the entry above)* The follow-up a kept head materializes as is now a **delta review**, in `atlas_reviewer/delta.py` and the pieces it wires. **The delta diff.** The pull request's diff at the new head, restricted to the paths the range `delta_from..head` touched (the lens input is every hunk the pull request's diff shows on a touched path — an older hunk in a touched file lenses too; "range diff" below means the carry-forward's own per-file patches, a different input from the lens diff): the pull request's diff never carries merge-from-base content, because merging the base moves the merge base forward and the base's changes drop out of the three-dot diff — so the restriction IS the range diff with that content excluded, with no per-commit reads. A path the base merge and a delta commit both touched stays in scope (over-inclusive is the safe direction, and the earlier hunks of such a file were worth re-reading); a merge-only push yields an empty delta, and the review then exists to carry the earlier findings forward and re-anchor them. An unfetchable range falls back to the full diff with the fallback recorded in the draft (`delta_scope`, which supersedes the round-1 text's `delta_review_diff`). **The carry-forward.** After the delta's lenses settle, one bounded, tool-less call (`_carry_call`, built like D73's re-assessment and quote-checked through the SAME judge, so the two paths' quote checks cannot drift) judges the earlier review's findings — the round log's records at `delta_from`, their finding lines parsed back (`{id} {severity} "{location}" {finding}`, the quoted-location form `_round_record` writes, the location quoted so a path containing a space cannot be misread as the finding's first word — this Update originally described the unquoted inverse, and the round-3 fix claimed the correction without touching this file; owned and applied here at the round-4 review of #748 (b1); the labels are the carry-forward's own positional `P1`, `P2`, ...). Every prior finding ends somewhere visible: a verified re-statement joins the findings set under its `P` label (and the verdict counts it — reconstructed defect-introduced, the conservative direction); a `withdrawn` entry is **resolved** only on evidence — as this Update first wrote, the file must be one the delta touched, and the round-2 correction below adds the removed-code requirement that makes the gate hold; a refused resolution is recorded as the proposal it is; everything else is carried as the record stored it, in the draft's `Carried, not re-verified` section AND back into this review's round record, so the next review re-offers it — nothing silently dropped (D73). A finding identical to a carried one (same re-anchored location and text) is posted once, as the carried copy. The delta post cites the review it builds on (`Follows the review of <sha>`), and the status line carries `carried`/`resolved` when this is a delta review. The carry-forward call spends against the review's shared `Spend` (D68) like every other call; its failure — a contract refusal, a transport error, a spent-out review — falls back to carrying every prior finding as recorded, with the failure named. The dry-run lens script grew a `carryforward` scenario section (controller-level, not per lens), validated at load with the real carry-forward parser; the scripted reviewer answers the carry turn by it, checked before the body markers because the carry system prompt carries no lens body. **Tests** (`atlas_reviewer/tests/test_delta.py`, the delta sections of `test_coordinator.py`, `test_service_run.py`, `test_service_post.py`, `test_lens_script.py`, and `test_cli.py`'s two delta dry runs over the new `fixtures/sample-pr-delta/`): the parse (id and severity closed sets, the three location shapes), the reply contract (evidence required on a file:line restatement, a withdrawal's reason required, an unknown severity dropped), the composition (each bucket, the off-delta resolution refused, nothing dropped), the scope (the intersection, an empty delta, the fallback), the coordinator flows (carried re-anchored, resolved leaves the chain, the off-delta proposal stands, the failed call's fallback, the dedupe, the no-prior note), and the end-to-end delta dry run over the fixture (the delta scope's two files, the carried blocker, the dedupe, the record's carried line, and the no-prior gap named).

**Correction (2026-11-18, round-1 review of #748).** The protocol review's five Majors, fixed on the PR branch; each pinned by a named test or a killed mutant. (M1) The resolution gate failed **open** wherever the delta was not actually the delta: the unfetchable-range fallback ran the review on the full pull request diff and `apply_carry`'s only gate was `path in delta_paths` over that set, so every PR path counted as delta-touched; and a diverged compare after a rebase or force-push -- whose file list is measured from the wrong base -- arrived with no check at all. The scope is now a `DeltaScope` whose `resolution_paths` is **empty whenever the delta could not be established** (an unfetchable range, a diverged history, a malformed or cut file list -- the comparison caps its `files` array, so a list at the cap is treated as cut), the gate fails closed, every resolution is refused, and the refusal names the scope's own reason; the review still runs, on the intersection scope where one exists or the full diff where none does. Pinned by `test_an_unfetchable_range_falls_back_to_the_full_diff_and_refuses_resolutions`, `test_a_diverged_history_refuses_resolutions_and_names_the_condition`, `test_a_cut_file_list_refuses_resolutions_and_names_the_cap`, `test_a_malformed_files_entry_makes_the_list_incomplete`, `test_an_unestablished_delta_refuses_every_resolution` (the empty-gate mutant), and the coordinator-level `test_a_resolution_on_an_unestablished_delta_is_refused_and_named`. (M2) A resolution no longer stands on the model's say-so: the `withdrawn` entry now carries `{"id", "reason", "evidence"}`, and acceptance requires **every quoted line of the evidence to be among the delta diff's removed lines for that file** -- real evidence the new commits took the cited code out (the prior finding's own quote is not in the record, but the cited code's removal is checkable exactly: code the earlier review verified still standing at the old head can only appear among the PR-side removals if the delta removed it). Without the evidence the finding stays carried and the refusal is named; a withdrawal never removes a finding on a say-so, the D73 path's rule. Pinned by `test_a_resolution_with_evidence_on_a_delta_path_is_accepted`, `test_a_resolution_whose_evidence_still_stands_is_refused`, `test_a_resolution_whose_evidence_partially_matches_is_refused`, `test_a_resolution_without_evidence_is_refused`, and the coordinator-level `test_a_resolution_without_removed_code_evidence_stays_carried`.
