(M3) A prior line that does not parse is no longer dropped while the draft claims it carried: it travels as the record's **raw text verbatim** -- shown in the `Carried, not re-verified` section and re-recorded into this review's round record, so the chain never loses it -- and the same walk that collects parsed findings collects the raw lines, so the count is stated once, not twice (m2). The record's finding-line format now quotes the location (`{id} {severity} "{location}" {finding}`), so a path containing a space parses whole instead of silently corrupting; the unquoted legacy form still parses when its token is unambiguously a line range, and an ambiguous bare token is refused and carried raw, never guessed into a whole-file path. Pinned by `test_an_unparsed_prior_line_is_carried_as_raw_text_and_re_recorded`, `test_a_quoted_location_with_a_space_parses_whole`, `test_an_ambiguous_unquoted_location_is_refused_not_mis_parsed`. (M4) A carried prior that fails to re-anchor still counts for the verdict: `decide` takes the carried-unre-anchored severities as verdict-setting -- they were verified findings of the earlier review of this pull request's code, and a failed re-anchor is not evidence the defect is fixed -- so a standing prior Blocker can never become an approval, including when the carry call fails whole. Pinned by `test_a_prior_blocker_that_fails_to_re_anchor_still_sets_the_verdict` (the mutant that drops the carried severities approves). (M5) A record whose finding lines did not fit the 8,192-character cap is no longer read as "no findings recorded": the draft states the record's `findings_omitted` count and that those findings cannot be carried, and the false sentence is gone. Pinned by `test_a_record_over_the_cap_names_its_omitted_findings`. The round-1 Minors: m1, an unreadable round log names the read failure instead of claiming "no findings recorded" (`test_an_unreadable_round_log_names_the_read_failure`); m2, folded into M3's single walk; m3, the comparison cap and malformed/missing `files` shapes are untrusted-with-a-note rather than silently shrinking the delta (`test_a_cut_file_list_refuses_resolutions_and_names_the_cap`, `test_a_comparison_without_a_files_array_is_untrusted`, `test_a_malformed_files_entry_makes_the_list_incomplete`); m4, carried-vs-carried duplicates across two records at the same `delta_from` are carried once (`test_two_records_carrying_the_same_finding_post_it_once`) -- the moved-line/paraphrase case is left to the prior block's "do not restate" instruction and is stated as the exact-match limitation it is; m5, the instructions no longer invite findings the composition would drop, and a non-`revises` entry is dropped and NAMED (`test_a_reply_addition_is_dropped_and_named`, `test_the_instructions_do_not_invite_findings_the_composition_drops`); m6, this correction's test list replaces the earlier one's phantom claim -- the file:line-requires-evidence rule is `lens_contract`'s own and is not re-pinned here; m7, the no-delta-sections test parses a real full-review draft; m8, the shared parsers are promoted to public names (`lens_contract.last_fenced_object`/`NO_OBJECT`/`UnknownSeverity`/`dropped_note`, `reassess.reassess_finding`) and `delta.py` no longer reaches into underscore internals; m9, #748's body names the #747-branch test rewrite it carried. The Nits: n1, the `review_lenses` docstring's churn reverted to the parent's text plus the D79 paragraph; n2, the dead `prior_findings` helper removed and `apply_carry`'s parameter renamed off the module function; n3, the carry call's model is wrapped by the lens trace (scrubbed, span recorded) and the dead parameters are gone; n4, the status line carries `carried_unverified` (raw lines included) beside `carried` and `resolved`, and the README's status-line list names all three. The earlier Update's test list is corrected by this one wherever they disagree.

**Correction (2026-11-18, round-2 review of #748).** Two Majors remained at the round-1 head, both fixed on the PR branch. (Major A) The resolution evidence pool was the pull request's `base...head` **net diff restricted to range paths** — the `read_sources(...).removed` of the diff the review runs on — not the `delta_from..head` range's own deletions, and the comparison's per-file `patch` fields were never read. Two failure directions, both real: a delta commit deleting PR-added code is invisible in a net diff (the line exists at neither end), so the dominant legitimate resolution — deleting introduced code — could never produce evidence and was refused forever while the draft claimed the code still stood; and a line the PR removed before `delta_from`, in a file the delta also touched, sat in the pool and could clear the gate with evidence that had nothing to do with the new commits. The round-1 correction's premise ("code the earlier review verified still standing at the old head can only appear among the PR-side removals if the delta removed it") was false for the assessor's quote, which is not required to be the prior finding's own evidence — owned here as a wrong premise, not re-read out of the record. The pool is now `DeltaScope.removed`, built from the comparison's per-file patches with each removed line's **old-side line number** (`_patch_removed`) — the range measures its patches from `delta_from`, so those are the coordinates the prior findings' locations cite — and the carry turn shows the range's own patches (`DeltaScope.range_diff`) instead of the restricted net diff, so the assessor can see and quote what the commits actually deleted; the judge's quote sources are built from the same range diff, so a re-statement quoting the range's added lines verifies against the head tree. The evidence check is also **span-bounded** now (a2): every quoted line must sit within the finding's cited span widened by the quote tolerance — any removed line in the file, down to a one-line brace, no longer clears the gate. A file whose patch is absent contributes no pool entries (a resolution for it finds no evidence and is refused) without touching the scope's own trustworthiness. Pinned by `test_the_pool_is_the_range_patch_not_the_restricted_pr_diff` (both directions in one test: the stale net-diff removal refused, the range's real deletion accepted), `test_evidence_outside_the_cited_span_is_refused`, `test_evidence_within_the_tolerance_of_the_span_is_accepted`, `test_a_whole_file_finding_takes_any_removed_line_of_the_file`, `test_a_patch_is_parsed_with_its_old_side_numbers`, and the coordinator-level `test_the_evidence_pool_is_the_range_not_the_restricted_diff`; a3's "no test tells the two diffs apart" is what those tests are. (Major B) The summed `findings_omitted` was named in the draft but never written into the new round record, so the omission left the durable chain after one hop. It now rides on the review (`AgenticReview.carried_omitted`) and into `_round_record`, added on top of `fit_record`'s own drops, so the next hop re-states the gap from the record alone; pinned by `test_the_omission_count_rides_through_the_record_across_two_hops`, which runs two hops and asserts the count in both records and both drafts. The Minors: a1, a comparison with no readable `merge_base_commit.sha` now reads as diverged — untrusted — instead of trusted (`test_a_missing_merge_base_reads_as_diverged`); a4, `_capped`'s truncation branch is pinned (`test_a_delta_over_its_cap_is_cut_with_the_marker`), and its docstring now states why a truncated delta still judges resolutions safely (the pool is structured, and an assessor that cannot see a removed line cannot quote it); a5, this Update's own prose no longer names the dead `delta_review_diff` or the superseded unconditional gate — corrected in place, the way #747's F6 was, rather than only by a later layer. The Nits: n5, `prompt.fence_rule` is public (the last underscore reach-through); n6, `CarryCallRefused` no longer stores the never-read `found`; the failed-carry note names the exception's detail, not only its class. Skipped, with reasons: n7, the fabricated `ReassessReply` shim inside `judged_carry` — harmless while the carry path stores no attestation, and un-fabricating it means splitting the judge's entry point for one caller; n8, the 16-parameter clump on `_with_carryforward`/`_carry_call` — the parameters are the existing `review_lens`/`_review_one` convention, and a context object introduced only for the delta path would be a second convention.
**Correction (2026-11-18, round-5 review of #748 — CodeRabbit's Majors, verified by Brandon's round-10 routine review; Brandon approved the round).** The three Majors and the check, fixed on the PR branch; each pinned by a named test or a killed mutant. (Major 1, the unproven range list must not bound the review.) A diverged history or a cut or malformed comparison file list cannot prove what the range touched, so `delta_scope` no longer narrows the lens diff to such a list's paths — the review runs on the full pull request diff, as the unfetchable case always has, with the resolution gate still closed; files past GitHub's comparison cap can no longer reach no lens while `coverage_complete` read true (pinned by the cut-list and diverged scope tests, whose reverted-narrowing mutants die). The same family's service-side case — a kept review that finished with incomplete coverage — is fixed where the shape is decided: `ReviewReport` carries the draft status line's `coverage_complete` flag, and `Board.settle` keys the follow-up's shape on it — a finished review that did not read every changed file queues its kept head as an ordinary full review, not as a delta on top of it, because the delta's narrowing would leave exactly the unread files to no review at all (pinned at the board and the worker; the worker mutant dies). The one-shot CLI path keeps its narrowing for a hand-written `delta_from`: the operator vouches for the earlier review there, and no round log is read in that mode. (Major 2, unassessable priors keep the verdict off plain approval.) A prior finding the delta cannot assess — a line that parses only as raw text, a legacy unquoted whole-file location among them, or a finding the earlier record's cap never stored — now marks the review's coverage incomplete through `_lens_gap`, the one coverage rule: the headline, the status line and the posted event all read it, so a clean delta over an unassessable prior posts COMMENT and never a plain approval; a complete record with no standing findings still approves (pinned by the omitted-priors, legacy-whole-file, and complete-record tests; the gap's mutant dies). (Major 3, one redaction for both sinks.) The carry-forward's exception note is scrubbed where it is recorded — `scrubber(str(exc))` at the note's construction — so the status line's `delta_note` and the body's quoted note carry the same redacted text; the status line's `_status_log_note` never scrubbed (pinned by a carry call whose exception carries a known secret; the scrub's mutant dies). (The check, the lens ledger's scope.) `LensKey` identified an attempt by head and lens body alone, and the attestation ledger is deployment-wide — a delta run's settled lens at a head shared the key a later full run at the same head computes, and the reused result's unread list was the delta's. The run's scope digest is now part of the key (`lens@<12-hex>`; the `@` cannot collide with a continuation's `#<hex>` or `#reassess`), so the same scope still reuses its settled attempt and a different scope never does (pinned by the delta-vs-full reuse test; the suffix's mutant dies). The round's cheap CodeRabbit fixes: the resolution gate is the range's own touched paths (a follow-up that deletes a file the earlier head added can resolve its findings; renames contribute `previous_filename` to both the gate and the removed-line pool), the patch reader splits on LF only (a U+2028 inside a patch line is content), a double quote is refused in a finding's location at the mint-side contract (the record writes locations quoted, so an embedded quote would truncate the next read's parse), the carry composition's dedupe keeps the duplicate pair's stronger finding (verdict-setting first, then severity, ties to the carried copy), the carry call checks the review wall before its reservation and again before sending (a reservation that crosses the wall is accounted, not sent), the parser's dropped restatements are named in the draft (D54), the post quotes the draft's delta note instead of asserting findings were carried forward, and the post's resolved and carried sections render the controller's bullets scrubbed and uncapped instead of re-quoting them at 200 characters. Skipped, with reasons: the valence/attribution fields in the round record (a conservative over-count never approves wrongly; the record-format change is a follow-up of its own), the stale-anchor SHA check for silent carries (fails closed today — the resolution is refused and the finding stands — and the re-statement path is the re-anchor), matching a withdrawal's quote against the earlier evidence text (the record stores neither evidence nor fix, so the content match needs the same record-format change), the base-merge path separation (the documented, deliberate over-inclusion — it never under-reviews), the quarantined-log note wording (the context omission already blocks approval; the first-read plumbing is a larger refactor), and the CLI fixture's withdrawal scenario (the withdrawal path is pinned at the delta and coordinator levels).
**Correction (2026-11-18, round-6 review of #748).** Two residual records; the round-6 verdict is APPROVE with nothing at Major, and the round's 17 mutants (13 carried forward, 4 new) all died. (b2) The round-5 correction's justification for the one-shot CLI's narrowing claimed no round log is read in that mode — false: the one-shot delta path reads the round log when `--round-log` is passed, and the branch's own end-to-end dry run does exactly that; the clause is corrected in place above, and the exception's safety rests on the shared coverage gate, which the one-shot path runs identically. (b3, recorded as a known limitation rather than a behavior change) The mint-side double-quote refusal makes findings on a double-quoted path unreportable: the offending finding fails the reply's contract, the lens retries into the same wall until its attempts exhaust, the lens ends failed and coverage incomplete, and the review lands COMMENT — never an approval over the gap. Double-quoted paths are pathological, and the escapes (quoting or escaping the location in the record, or the record-format change that would carry them verbatim) are deferred with the record-format follow-up; this paragraph is that limitation's revisit marker.

- **D81 — The reviewed repo's `REVIEW.md` is the reviewer service's user-configuration surface, and a repo sets its own per-review ceiling; the user-configuration plan's shape is accepted with the answers below (2026-11-18).** *(user, 2026-11-18; Brandon answered the eight open questions in [`plans/2026-11-18-user-configuration.md`](plans/2026-11-18-user-configuration.md) §6 at 3:28 PM CT on 2026-11-18; D80 stays #734's, so this decision takes D81)* **The shape accepted.** The reviewed repo's merge-base `REVIEW.md` is the user-facing configuration surface (Q1), parsed by a schema-validated module with bounded values that fail open to the default with a named note, read from the merge-base/base ref only — never the PR head — and unable to widen network, secret, or Sprite access; §4's security rules of the plan stand as written. **The eight answers.** **(Q1)** extend `REVIEW.md` rather than add a `.atlas.yml`-style file, as the plan recommended. **(Q2)** on the ceiling direction, the plan's recommendation to clamp the repo's `review_ceiling_usd:` under the operator's configured value is **not taken** — "let each repo set its own limits": D68's semantics stay exactly as they are, a repo's `review_ceiling_usd:` may raise or lower its per-review ceiling, and the operator's backstop outside repo control is not a ceiling clamp but the key-level spend guard `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD`, which refuses an OpenRouter key whose own credit limit exceeds the operator's bound before any inference; the ceiling a review actually used is still named in the draft and the post through D68's ceiling note. **(Q3)** the severity floor is adopted as recommended: the service's default posts everything (it posts one COMMENT per head, not converging rounds), a repo may raise its floor, and the manifest's floor/preference tier split plus the overlay's `acknowledge` rule govern suppression. **(Q4)** installation-level defaults stay unbuilt — repo-level configuration only for now, as recommended. **(Q5)** PR-level overrides: none for now, as recommended. **(Q6)** user path filters stay ordering-only until Q25 decides whether anything may be excluded from review at all, as recommended. **(Q7)** quiet hours and per-repo rate limits are deferred. **(Q8)** AGENTS.md/coding-guideline ingestion stays in scope as the plan's S6, as recommended. **What this changes in the plan:** §2's "user config tightens, operator config bounds" principle is narrowed — it holds for admission, post shape, and every budget a repo may lower, but the per-review ceiling is the one value a repo may raise (D68's semantics, kept by Q2), backstopped by `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD` rather than by an operator clamp; the plan's S2 (the ceiling-direction slice) is dropped, and S8 (installation defaults, quiet hours) is deferred. **Reversibility.** D68's ceiling read is untouched by this decision, so reversing Q2's answer is a documentation change plus the small slice S2 described; every other answer accepts or defers a recommendation, so nothing built has to come back out. **Overlap.** The remaining slices keep §5's file sets and parity-track dependencies, and S1 still coordinates with #750 on `review_wide.py`'s ceiling read.

**Update (2026-11-18, round-1 fixes on PR #751).** Two slice details the Overlap sentence above states as the plan had them, corrected by the plan's round-1 review; the decision's answers are unchanged. (1) S1 no longer serializes behind #750: checked against the code on main, #750's fix — the issue's "reservation accounting for large PRs" and "a budget-stopped review's posted text can't be mistaken for a clean review" — plausibly touches `budget.py`'s per-call reservation pricing and the budget-stopped post path; all of those *consume* the resolved ceiling, and none plausibly changes the `REVIEW.md` ceiling *read* S1 refactors (`CEILING_LINE`, `review_md_ceiling`, `_lens_preferences`, `resolve_ceiling`'s precedence) — corrected against the fix's actual PR (**#757**, which takes D82): its file list is `budget.py`, `openrouter.py`, `service_post.py`, `tool_model.py`, which includes neither `review_wide.py` nor `coordinator.py`, so S1 is disjoint, not merely concurrent. (2) S4's narrowing is limited to delivery properties where a skip cannot land unreviewed changes on a branch the repo reviews — the base branch (the skip and the landing coincide: the repo's narrowing *is* the choice of where reviews happen) and the event type; the patterns matched against come from the default branch, so a PR cannot write its own admission. The distinguishing property is not author control of the value — the base branch is author-set at open and author-retargetable, and the draft state is author-set too (and already excluded by the operator envelope, which makes a draft filter vacuous) — it is that a head-branch or label skip can diverge from the landing: the author names the head branch or sets the label and can still merge into the protected base, so those sit behind Q5's deferred PR-level overrides, and every rule-based skip records a one-line reason so a skipped review is distinguishable from the service being down.

**Update (2026-11-18, 3:39 PM CT — the per-tenant spend bound; resolves the round-2 Major fix round 1 held).** The plan had claimed `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD` as the spend backstop that stays outside repo control under Q2; the round-2 review's Major was right that it is a cap on the *shared key's total* credit limit, not a per-repo bound, so one repo's raised ceiling could drain spend for every tenant on the key. Brandon decided at 3:39 PM CT on 2026-11-18: **"Add both an operator max per review and a per-repo daily budget."** Q2's answer stands — repos set their own limits, within the operator's bound. The operator max per review clamps the repo's effective ceiling (out-of-range → named fallback, D68's pattern); the per-repo daily budget refuses a repo's further reviews once its day's budget is spent, at admission and re-checked at preflight, and the record says plainly "skipped: daily budget reached" — never a clean review. The reset boundary is the UTC day, and the running total lives in the service's existing durable state: the state root on the Fly volume (`ATLAS_REVIEWER_STATE_DIR`), one append-only `<state root>/spend/<repo flattened>-<UTC date>.jsonl` file per repo per day, the same pattern D74's per-PR round logs use. The plan carries this as slice **S9** (after S1 and S4, rebase-checked against **#757** — its actual file list includes `budget.py`, which S9's daily-total accounting edits, and does not include `review_wide.py`), and S9 is a **precondition for opening the App beyond the operator's allowlisted repos**. The spend file is `<state root>/spend/<owner>-<repo>-<UTC date>.jsonl`, each name segment percent-encoded with every byte outside `[A-Za-z0-9]` escaped so two distinct owner/repo pairs never share a file (the plan's §4 specifies it). The proposed variable names (`ATLAS_REPO_CEILING_MAX_USD`, `ATLAS_REPO_DAILY_BUDGET_USD`) and the working defaults ($25 per review, $100 per repo per UTC day) are proposed bounds pending the owner's numbers, the way D31's caps were.

**Update (2026-11-18, 3:41 PM CT — the BYOK requirement; answers pending).** Brandon: **"I need the github app to allow individuals or orgs to supply their own api endpoints and keys."** This amends the plan's operator-only rule — the model, provider and data-policy variables stay operator-only *for the operator's key*, and an installation (a user account or an org) gains its own endpoint-and-key surface, planned in [`plans/2026-11-18-user-configuration.md`](plans/2026-11-18-user-configuration.md) §2's BYOK paragraph, §3's two BYOK rows, §4's key-handling and endpoint-trust bullets, and slice S10. Keys never enter `REVIEW.md` or any repo file. The requirement's open questions are the plan's new §7, **not answered here** — the owner's answers will be recorded when relayed. One conflict is flagged rather than resolved: BYOK needs installation-level storage, which this decision's Q4 answer ("repo-level only for now") did not build; the plan's §7 Q9 carries it as a new decision point with a recommended narrow amendment (installation state for credentials only, review configuration stays repo-level), not a silent override.

**Update (2026-11-18, the S4 config read source; from #756's protocol review).** The shape paragraph above says config is read "from the merge-base/base ref only — never the PR head"; #756's protocol review caught that the PR's `base.ref` and its merge-base are themselves **author-controllable** in same-repo and stacked PRs — the author can commit configuration to the branch they target, and the merge-base then carries it. The plan's §4 read-source rule is now two-tier: settings a PR must not influence — S4's admission rules first, matching #756's own change — read from the repo's **default branch**; D68's ceiling line and D69's lens preferences keep their existing merge-base read, and the plan states the weakness they share, with its bounded exposure (a ceiling raise is clamped by S9's operator max, and a lowered ceiling only cheapens the author's own review; the lens lists can never widen the depth mode or touch the critical tier, so the floor-tier set cannot be excluded away) and names the default-branch migration as the safer end-state rather than claiming the weakness away. The base-retarget dodge — a PR opened against an excluded base (*skipped* at `opened`), then retargeted via a `pull_request.edited` event that carries no new `opened` — is the gap #756 documents ("documented, not widened"): the default-branch read prevents retargeting from changing the *config's* answer, but a rule whose matched value is the delivery's base branch does move, and closing that needed the owner's envelope decision, recorded in the update below.

**Update (2026-11-18, 4:15 PM CT — the BYOK answers).** Brandon: **"Accept all 9 BYOK recommendations."** The plan's §7 questions are decided as recommended and recorded there as §7's decision list; summarized here: (1) the intake is an operator-hosted settings page authenticated as the installation's admin via GitHub; (2) the endpoint scope is any HTTPS host passing the plan's §4 egress checks — not an operator provider allow-list; (3) the intake carries an explicit consent statement the installation admin acknowledges before the first BYOK review; (4) both per-tenant spend bounds apply to a BYOK review's own spend, user-lowering only, the operator max the outer bound, `ATLAS_KEY_CEILING_USD` staying operator-key only; (5) a BYOK installation may choose the model within its own endpoint, D68's budget shape staying; (6) models with no price-table entry are refused; (7) the operator-key fallback stays off — a BYOK failure names itself plainly; (8) uninstall deletes the stored secret on the `installation.deleted` webhook, with a retention sweep; (9) Q4 is **amended narrowly** — installation-level state is for credentials only, and review configuration stays repo-level. S10's gating updates accordingly: no longer waiting on answers, it still follows S9.

**Update (2026-11-18, 4:28 PM CT — the base-change `edited` envelope).** Resolving the retarget gap (Major 1 of PR #751's round-1 protocol review — #756 documents it as "documented, not widened", since admitting the `edited` event would widen the operator's envelope and that is an operator decision, not a repo-config one), Brandon decided: **"Yes, admit base-change edited events as a follow-up."** The service will admit `pull_request` `edited` deliveries only when `changes.base` is present, re-running admission against the new base, with config still read from the default branch — an explicit, narrow widening of the operator event envelope, tracked as **issue #758**, a follow-up to #756. Until #758 lands, the gap stands named, as #756 documents it.
